## KSI-SVC-SIN — Implemented

### Implementation

- Information is unqualified and therefore broader than federal customer data, which FRD-FCD defines to exclude provider-generated metadata, telemetry and analytics. Scope is everything the offering holds: customer data, audit logs, evidence, secrets, backups, container images and configuration state. The control mappings expand scope further in ways the wording gives no hint of: SC-20, SC-21 and SC-22 place name resolution in scope, CP-9(8) places backups in scope, and AC-17(2) places remote access in scope. Or otherwise secured resolves per location: where the platform encrypts by construction and the meaningful control is access, the claim rests on access control and says so, which is why BigQuery is determined against dataset IAM rather than encryption, since an encryption claim there would be true and evidentially empty. The access half and the modification half need different mechanisms, and this is where implementations are usually thin: encryption at rest does nothing against an authorised principal modifying data, so modification is carried by object lock, versioning, retention, and CloudTrail log file validation, which detects alteration where object lock only prevents deletion. Encryption is also only as strong as who may decrypt, so key policy resolution is part of the claim rather than an adjacent concern, and it is the one check here that requires resolving effective access rather than reading a boolean. DNS integrity is declared as a gap: DNSSEC is the mechanism, but signing requires the registrar to accept a DS record against a real personal domain with a genuine risk of breaking resolution, for a check that is ultimately a boolean, so the mapping is cited to show the requirement was found rather than missed. AC-1 is policy and procedures, non-machine, out of scope project-wide.
- **1. Encryption at rest** — Customer-managed keys across all stores including registries, log groups, topics and state machines. Four to five keys by data class, reused across related stores. *(Both; provisioned by Terraform)*
- **2. Transit enforcement** — ALB TLS with a modern policy, HTTP redirected, TLS continued to the task rather than terminated at the load balancer. rds.force_ssl enforced. TLS-only bucket policies. *(AWS; provisioned by Terraform)*
- **3. Log immutability** — Object lock in compliance mode with retention measured in days, not months, and CloudTrail log file validation enabled. *(AWS; provisioned by Terraform)*
- **4. Storage access posture** — Public access block on all S3. Public access prevention and uniform bucket-level access on GCS. *(Both; provisioned by Terraform)*
- **5. BigQuery access control** — Dataset IAM bound to declared roles only. No primitive roles. *(GCP; provisioned by Terraform)*
- **6. Key policy constraint** — Decrypt granted only to principals in the declared role model from KSI-IAM-ELP. Key policy modification requires JIT elevation. *(Both; provisioned by Terraform)*
- **7. Versioning and retention** — S3 and GCS versioning on state and evidence. RDS backup retention window. *(Both; provisioned by Terraform)*
- **8. Container registries** — ECR and Artifact Registry with customer-managed keys and immutable tags. *(Both; provisioned by Terraform)*

### Validation

- **Cycle.** Required: 3 days. Current practice: the collector runs daily from GitHub Actions (.github/workflows/collect.yml), as a read-only identity in each cloud with no stored credential, and this record was emitted by that run. Daily is stricter than every required cycle above (DECISIONS.md, 2026-10-01).
- **CFG** — Every store reports encryption with the expected key, including registries, log groups and ancillary services. Source: RDS, S3, Secrets Manager, ECR, CloudWatch Logs, GCS, BigQuery, Artifact Registry APIs. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per run. *Row `KSI-SVC-SIN.verify.1`: automated by `svc-sin-cfg-aws-stores-use-declared-keys`, `svc-sin-cfg-gcp-stores-use-declared-keys`.*
- **CFG** — No storage resource permits public access. Source: S3 public access block; GCS public access prevention. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per run. *Row `KSI-SVC-SIN.verify.2`: automated by `svc-sin-cfg-aws-account-public-access-block`, `svc-sin-cfg-aws-buckets-block-public-access`, `svc-sin-cfg-gcp-buckets-prevent-public-access`.*
- **CFG** — rds.force_ssl enabled in the parameter group. Source: RDS parameter group. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per run. *Row `KSI-SVC-SIN.verify.3`: automated by `svc-sin-cfg-aws-database-settings` (judged only while aws-phase1 stands).*
- **CFG** — ALB listener uses a modern TLS policy, HTTP redirects, target group uses TLS. Source: ELB listener and target group configuration. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per run. *Row `KSI-SVC-SIN.verify.4`: automated by `svc-sin-cfg-aws-alb-tls` (judged only while aws-phase1 stands).*
- **CFG** — Central log store has object lock and CloudTrail log file validation enabled. Source: S3 object lock configuration; CloudTrail trail settings. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per run. *Row `KSI-SVC-SIN.verify.5`: automated by `svc-sin-cfg-aws-log-store-object-lock`, `svc-sin-cfg-aws-trail-log-validation`.*
- **CFG** — Key policies grant decrypt only to declared roles, resolved rather than read as a flag. Source: KMS key policies and grants; Cloud KMS IAM. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per run. *Row `KSI-SVC-SIN.verify.6`: automated by `svc-sin-cfg-aws-keys-decrypt-only-declared`, `svc-sin-cfg-gcp-keys-decrypt-only-declared`.*
- **CFG** — BigQuery dataset bindings match the declared role model from KSI-IAM-ELP. Source: BigQuery dataset IAM. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per run. *Row `KSI-SVC-SIN.verify.7`: automated by `svc-sin-cfg-gcp-bigquery-access-declared`.*
- **CFG** — Snapshots report encryption with the expected key. Source: RDS snapshot attributes. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per run. *Row `KSI-SVC-SIN.verify.8`: automated by `svc-sin-cfg-aws-backups-restorable`.*
- **OPS** — Zero decrypt calls by principals outside the declared role model from KSI-IAM-ELP, standing query, zero results expected. Source: CloudTrail KMS events; Cloud KMS audit logs. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per run. *Row `KSI-SVC-SIN.validate.1`: partly automated by `svc-sin-ops-aws-decrypts-by-declared-principals`. Not covered: AWS only: Cloud KMS audit logs are not in the corpus. Decrypts by AWS services are counted but not attributed, since the normalized event does not say which service acted.*
- **OPS** — Zero connections to RDS without TLS, standing query, zero results expected. Source: RDS connection logs. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per run. *Row `KSI-SVC-SIN.validate.2`: partly automated by `svc-sin-ops-aws-db-connections-tls` (judged only while aws-phase2 stands). Not covered: Judged in sessions: the database and its log stand only then. rds.force_ssl already refuses a plain connection (svc-sin-cfg-aws-database-settings); this shows none was made.*
- **OPS** — Zero requests to any bucket over plain HTTP, standing query, zero results expected. Source: S3 server access logs; GCS audit logs. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per run. *Row `KSI-SVC-SIN.validate.3`: partly automated by `svc-sin-ops-aws-s3-requests-over-tls`. Not covered: S3 requests CloudTrail records: management events for every bucket, object requests for the extract bucket only, since data events are scoped to customer data. No server access logs. GCS is not in the corpus.*
- **OPS** — A write attempt against an object-locked log object is rejected. Source: Deliberate test write. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per run. *Row `KSI-SVC-SIN.validate.4`: partly automated by `svc-sin-ops-object-lock-rejects-change`. Not covered: Run every 30 days by the operator and recorded, not every 3: the test changes or provokes the account, so it does not run unattended.*
- **OPS** — CloudTrail digest validation succeeds against a sample of delivered logs. Source: CloudTrail validate-logs. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per run. *Row `KSI-SVC-SIN.validate.5`: automated by `svc-sin-ops-aws-trail-digests-validate`.*
- **OPS** — Every data store in the inventory appears in the encryption check, zero unaccounted. Source: Reconciliation against the inventory generated for KSI-PIY-GIV. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per run. *Row `KSI-SVC-SIN.validate.6`: partly automated by `svc-sin-ops-register-data-store`. Not covered: Each data-store class names its encryption check; that the check's population includes every resource of the class is that check's design (it reads the stores from the APIs), not re-verified here.*
- **Automated evidence.** 20 collector check(s) in the run on 2026-10-26: 3 NOT_STANDING, 17 PASS. Historical metrics (SDR-CSX-KMT) cover 19 day(s) of CI runs since 2026-10-02, in the metrics evidence object below; the year is what exists, not a full year.

### Assessment

- **No independent assessor is engaged.** The statements below are the provider's own assurance reasoning from the determination. They are not an independent assessment and should not be read as one.
- **Automation assurance.** Accuracy is tested by creating an unencrypted bucket and confirming the collector fails it, then removing it. TLS enforcement is tested by attempting a non-TLS connection to RDS and confirming rejection, which proves force_ssl enforces rather than merely being set. The key policy check is tested by granting decrypt to a disposable principal and confirming the collector fails it. Zero-results queries are self-tested. Sufficiency is bounded by provider reporting: encryption status is a reliable API fact, but whether a specific object was written over TLS is inferred from request logs, so a provider that does not log connection protocol leaves a gap in that claim specifically rather than in the indicator as a whole.
- **Limitations and residual risk.** DNS integrity is not implemented; SC-20, SC-21 and SC-22 place name resolution in scope and DNSSEC is the mechanism, declared rather than built for registrar-friction reasons. Encryption at rest does not protect against an authorised principal, so the access half depends on the ELP role model being correct and SIN inherits that dependency. Object lock in compliance mode cannot be shortened or removed, including by us, so a misconfigured retention period is not correctable. Transit between AWS services over interface endpoints is provider-enforced, inherited rather than demonstrated. GitHub's encryption is inherited from a third party and not demonstrated here. Fargate ephemeral storage uses the provider-managed key by default rather than a customer-managed one, stated rather than overridden. Synthetic data has no real sensitivity, so key-to-data-class assignment is asserted rather than driven by a real data inventory.

- Negative control for `svc-sin-cfg-aws-account-public-access-block` (collector/self_test.py): configurations that must fail it: no block, one setting off.
- Negative control for `svc-sin-cfg-aws-alb-tls` (collector/self_test.py): configurations that must fail it: old TLS policy, HTTP forwards, redirect to HTTP, plain HTTP to targets, no HTTPS listener.
- Negative control for `svc-sin-cfg-aws-backups-restorable` (collector/self_test.py): configurations that must fail it: key deleted, key pending deletion, AWS-managed key, unencrypted.
- Negative control for `svc-sin-cfg-aws-buckets-block-public-access` (collector/self_test.py): configurations that must fail it: no block, one setting off.
- Negative control for `svc-sin-cfg-aws-buckets-tls-only` (collector/self_test.py): configurations that must fail it: no policy, allow not deny, one principal, objects only, one action, condition inverted.
- Negative control for `svc-sin-cfg-aws-database-settings` (collector/self_test.py): configurations that must fail it: TLS not forced, parameter unset, no IAM auth, short retention, unencrypted.
- Negative control for `svc-sin-cfg-aws-keys-decrypt-only-declared` (collector/self_test.py): configurations that must fail it: undeclared role named, public principal, account delegation unconfined, wildcard action, NotAction, grant to undeclared.
- Negative control for `svc-sin-cfg-aws-log-store-object-lock` (collector/self_test.py): configurations that must fail it: not enabled, governance mode, no default retention, too short.

## KSI-SVC-ASM — Implemented

### Implementation

- Both defined terms appear in one sentence and they pull in opposite directions, which is deliberate. Regularly means performing the activity on a consistent, predictable and repeated basis at set intervals, automatically if possible, following a documented plan. Persistently means the opposite on the predictability axis, explicitly permitting irregular occurrence with interruptions and waiting periods, and its note says outright that it indicates a process which may not occur on a consistent predictable basis. So rotation must be scheduled and review need not be: a secret's age is itself a security property, whereas review only needs to keep status known. This is also the first indicator requiring a documented plan as a deliverable, since that phrase sits inside the definition of Regularly, so the interval table is versioned in the repository rather than implied. Scope resolves into four classes rotating on different mechanisms: managed secrets through native rotation, encryption keys through provider automatic rotation, certificates through managed renewal, and bearer tokens, where the SCIM provisioning token from AAM has no native mechanism and the rotation is built. The application database credential is deliberately absent from this scope: VCM authenticates that connection by platform identity and disables password authentication for the application user, so there is no database password to rotate. One decision in that indicator removed a secret class from this one. That token is where a debt comes due: AAM specified scheduled rotation with expiry monitoring and SNU set the standard that a credential surviving without a live trust exchange fails, so the workflow generates a new token, updates the provisioning configuration, and verifies provisioning still functions before discarding the old one, because a rotation that silently breaks deprovisioning is worse than no rotation given AAM's lifecycle claim depends on it. Protection is largely SIN's work and is referenced rather than rebuilt; what ASM adds is that no secret exists outside a managed store, including in environment variables, task definitions, repository files or Terraform state. That last is a stronger claim than SIN's: SIN protects the state backend as an asset, ASM requires that secrets are not in it at all.
- **1. Managed secret storage** — Secrets Manager and Secret Manager. No secrets in environment variables or task definitions; injection by reference at runtime. *(Both; provisioned by Terraform)*
- **2. Native rotation** — Secrets Manager rotation on a schedule matching the documented interval table. *(AWS; provisioned by Terraform)*
- **3. Key rotation** — Automatic annual rotation enabled on every customer-managed key. *(Both; provisioned by Terraform)*
- **4. Certificate automation** — Split by hop, because the two halves resolve differently. Load balancer listener: ACM-issued via DNS validation on the offering's domain, automatic renewal, no cost. Task-side: self-signed, because the load balancer does not validate target certificates and AWS documents VPC target traffic as authenticated at the packet level -- a trusted chain would be verified by nothing in the path. Authenticity on that hop is established by platform identity under KSI-SVC-VCM. Validation records created by hand; the certificate is issued in the bootstrap root so teardown does not destroy it. *(AWS; provisioned by Terraform)*
- **5. SCIM token rotation** — Scheduled workflow: generate, update provisioning configuration, verify provisioning functions, then discard the old token. *(Both; provisioned by Terraform)*
- **6. Rotation interval plan** — Versioned table of secret class to interval, satisfying the documented-plan requirement inside Regularly. *(Repo; provisioned by Version control)*
- **7. Secret sprawl detection** — Repository and state scanned for secret material; task definitions checked for literal values. *(Both; provisioned by Terraform and CI)*

### Validation

- **Cycle.** Required: 3 days. Current practice: the collector runs daily from GitHub Actions (.github/workflows/collect.yml), as a read-only identity in each cloud with no stored credential, and this record was emitted by that run. Daily is stricter than every required cycle above (DECISIONS.md, 2026-10-01).
- **CFG** — Every secret has rotation configured with an interval matching the plan. Source: Secrets Manager describe-secret; Secret Manager rotation config. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per run. *Row `KSI-SVC-ASM.verify.1`: partly automated by `svc-asm-cfg-aws-secret-rotation` (judged only while aws-phase1 stands). Not covered: AWS secrets; GCP holds no Secret Manager secrets. Judged while the environment stands, in sessions; the checks are written ahead of session 2.*
- **CFG** — Every customer-managed key has automatic rotation enabled. Source: KMS key rotation status; Cloud KMS rotation period. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per run. *Row `KSI-SVC-ASM.verify.2`: automated by `svc-asm-cfg-aws-keys-rotate`, `svc-asm-cfg-gcp-keys-rotate`.*
- **CFG** — Certificates are managed-issue with renewal eligibility, not imported. Source: ACM certificate status. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per run. *Row `KSI-SVC-ASM.verify.3`: automated by `svc-asm-cfg-aws-certificates-managed`.*
- **CFG** — No task definition or service configuration contains a literal secret value. Source: ECS task definitions; Cloud Run service configuration. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per run. *Row `KSI-SVC-ASM.verify.4`: partly automated by `svc-asm-cfg-aws-no-literal-secrets` (judged only while aws-phase2 stands). Not covered: ECS task definitions, judged while phase 2 stands; the Cloud Run job's configuration is not read.*
- **CFG** — The rotation interval plan exists and covers every secret class. Source: Repository file, checked in CI. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per run. *Row `KSI-SVC-ASM.verify.5`: automated by `svc-asm-cfg-rotation-plan-covers-classes`.*
- **OPS** — Every secret's last-rotated timestamp is within its declared interval. Source: Secrets Manager metadata; key rotation history. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per run. *Row `KSI-SVC-ASM.validate.1`: partly automated by `svc-asm-cfg-aws-secret-rotation` (judged only while aws-phase1 stands). Not covered: As verify 1; last-rotated is read from Secrets Manager's metadata.*
- **OPS** — A rotation is observed completing end to end, with the new version in use. Source: Rotation event correlated with subsequent successful authentication. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per run. *Row `KSI-SVC-ASM.validate.2`: not automated -- gap. No secret here rotates on a schedule within a session: the task TLS secret is regenerated at each apply and the database credential is RDS-managed. A rotation has not been observed end to end. Risk: That the RDS-managed rotation completes and the application picks up the new version is unproven.*
- **OPS** — SCIM provisioning verified working after token rotation, not merely rotated. Source: Post-rotation provisioning test. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per run. *Row `KSI-SVC-ASM.validate.3`: not automated -- descoped. SCIM provisioning (KSI-IAM-AAM) was descoped on 2026-10-03: it needs Cloud Identity Premium and mostly proves group sync for a two-person setup. The one Identity Center user is created by hand. With no SCIM token there is no rotation to verify. Risk: Deprovisioning does not propagate: a user suspended in Google keeps its Identity Center user until removed by hand. Sign-in goes through Google's SAML, so a suspended account cannot start a session; one already open lasts up to its limit, 4 hours read-only or 1 hour elevated.*
- **OPS** — Zero secret material found in the repository or in state, standing query, zero results expected. Source: Secret scanning across repository and state. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per run. *Row `KSI-SVC-ASM.validate.4`: partly automated by `svc-asm-cfg-repository-secret-scan`. Not covered: The repository only. Terraform state is not scanned, and while the environment stands it holds the task TLS private key (tls_private_key, secrets.tf), readable by the roles that read the state bucket: the operator's read-only role and the CI read roles. The key is self-signed, for the load balancer-to-task hop, and lives one session.*
- **OPS** — Zero authentication events using a superseded secret version, standing query, zero results expected. Source: CloudTrail credential usage. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per run. *Row `KSI-SVC-ASM.validate.5`: not automated -- gap. Tasks authenticate to the database by IAM token, not by a stored secret version, and nothing correlates CloudTrail credential use with superseded versions. Risk: Use of a superseded secret version would not be detected.*
- **OPS** — Every secret in the inventory has an owning class in the documented plan, zero unaccounted. Source: Reconciliation against the inventory generated for KSI-PIY-GIV. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per run. *Row `KSI-SVC-ASM.validate.6`: partly automated by `svc-asm-ops-secrets-owned`. Not covered: Certificates are not in the inventory: the Config recorder's declared type list does not include ACM certificates (2026-09-05 cost decision). They are judged by svc-asm-cfg-aws-certificates-managed instead.*
- **Automated evidence.** 8 collector check(s) in the run on 2026-10-26: 2 NOT_STANDING, 6 PASS. Historical metrics (SDR-CSX-KMT) cover 19 day(s) of CI runs since 2026-10-02, in the metrics evidence object below; the year is what exists, not a full year.

### Assessment

- **No independent assessor is engaged.** The statements below are the provider's own assurance reasoning from the determination. They are not an independent assessment and should not be read as one.
- **Automation assurance.** Accuracy is tested by placing a disposable secret outside a managed store and confirming the collector fails it, and by setting a secret's rotation interval past its declared value and confirming the age check fails. The SCIM rotation is tested by running it against a disposable account and confirming provisioning succeeds on the new token before the old is discarded. Zero-results queries are self-tested. Sufficiency is bounded by discoverability: secret scanning finds material matching known patterns, so a secret in an unrecognised format is invisible to it, and the claim is scoped to detectable secret material rather than to all secrets.
- **Limitations and residual risk.** The SCIM token has no native rotation and the mechanism is built rather than provided, so it carries the same operational risk as the JIT elevation workflow: a failure mid-rotation can leave provisioning broken, and verification before discard narrows this rather than eliminating it. Key rotation rotates key material while the key identifier persists, so data encrypted under prior material remains decryptable by design; that is how the mechanism works rather than a gap, but it means key rotation does not limit exposure the way credential rotation does. Secret scanning is pattern-based and bounded by what it recognises. Certificate renewal is provider-managed and inherited rather than demonstrated. The synthetic environment has few secrets, so sprawl detection is easy here in a way it would not be at real scale, where sprawl is the actual failure mode.

### Tests

- Negative control for `svc-asm-cfg-aws-certificates-managed` (collector/self_test.py): configurations that must fail it: imported, email validated, in use, ineligible, idle and near expiry, no certificates.
- Negative control for `svc-asm-cfg-aws-keys-rotate` (collector/self_test.py): configurations that must fail it: rotation off, too slow, no keys.
- Negative control for `svc-asm-cfg-gcp-keys-rotate` (collector/self_test.py): configurations that must fail it: rotation off, too slow, no keys.
- Negative control for `svc-asm-cfg-repository-secret-scan` (collector/self_test.py): a workflow where no scanning step.
- Negative control for `svc-asm-cfg-rotation-plan-covers-classes` (collector/self_test.py): inputs that must fail it: class missing, no interval and no deferral, no mechanism.
- Negative control for `svc-asm-ops-secrets-owned` (collector/self_test.py): inputs that must fail it: a secret no class owns, nothing to judge.
- Designed, not yet automated: SCIM provisioning verified working after token rotation, not merely rotated (Post-rotation provisioning test).

## KSI-SVC-VRI — Partially Implemented

### Implementation

- This is the only indicator in the catalog phrased as an imperative. Every other statement describes a state that must hold; this one prescribes a method, which narrows the answer space: access control, review discipline and process rigour do not substitute, and a resource whose integrity claim rests on anything other than cryptography does not satisfy it. Machine-Based is defined expansively across systems, processes, software, hardware, services and cloud-native capabilities, and the mappings push the same way, with CM-8(3) covering unauthorised component detection and SR-10 covering supply chain inspection, so this is not only about container images. Determined per resource class: images by digest-pinned references with signature verification at deploy; infrastructure definition by commit signing and state checksums; audit logs by the digest files established under SIN; dependencies by hash-verified lockfiles. Two classes cannot be answered cryptographically and are declared rather than stretched. Managed service internals, meaning RDS, BigQuery and the load balancer, are provider-attested and inherited. Running task memory and process state have no cryptographic mechanism at this layer, which is the same gap ACM declared as two of the five named forms of drift. Digest pinning over tag references is the concrete decision and it costs deployment friction deliberately, because a tag is a mutable pointer with no integrity property at all. Validate is a defined term meaning confirmation through objective evidence that capabilities support expected outcomes, which sets the bar above a signature existing: the check must block deployment rather than report, since a reported failure is not confirmation. Relationship to ACM: that indicator asserts everything is declared and matches its declaration, this one asserts the declared artifacts are cryptographically what they claim to be, and drift detection would report clean on a tampered image behind an unchanged tag.
- **1. Image signing** — Images signed at build; signatures stored alongside them in the registry as OCI artifacts. Keyless signing with the pipeline's federated identity rather than a managed signing service: no signing key exists to store, protect or rotate, holding the KSI-IAM-SNU position rather than reopening it. The signing identity is the workflow, repository and commit, recorded in a public transparency log. *(Both; provisioned by Terraform and CI)*
- **2. Digest pinning** — Task and service definitions reference images by digest. Tag references rejected in CI. *(Both; provisioned by Terraform)*
- **3. Deploy-time verification** — Signature verified before deployment, with identity conditions on the signer rather than merely on the presence of a signature. Failure blocks rather than warns. Sequence-enforced rather than admission-enforced: the container service provides no admission control for image signatures, so the bypass is closed by IAM instead -- only the pipeline principal may push images or update services. Declared, not implied. *(Both; provisioned by Terraform and CI)*
- **4. Immutable tags** — Registries configured so tags cannot be overwritten. *(Both; provisioned by Terraform)*
- **5. Dependency integrity** — Lockfiles with hashes. Build fails on mismatch. *(Repo; provisioned by CI)*
- **6. Commit signing** — Signed commits required on the protected branch. *(Repo; provisioned by Version control)*
- **7. Integrity position register** — Every machine-based resource class mapped to a cryptographic method or explicitly marked inherited. *(Repo; provisioned by Version control)*

### Validation

- **Cycle.** Required: 3 days. Current practice: the collector runs daily from GitHub Actions (.github/workflows/collect.yml), as a read-only identity in each cloud with no stored credential, and this record was emitted by that run. Daily is stricter than every required cycle above (DECISIONS.md, 2026-10-01).
- **CFG** — Every task and service definition references an image digest, not a tag. Source: ECS task definitions; Cloud Run service configuration. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per run. *Row `KSI-SVC-VRI.verify.1`: automated by `svc-vri-cfg-aws-tasks-digest-pinned` (judged only while aws-phase2 stands), `svc-vri-cfg-gcp-run-jobs-digest-pinned`.*
- **CFG** — Registry tag immutability enabled. Source: ECR and Artifact Registry configuration. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per run. *Row `KSI-SVC-VRI.verify.2`: automated by `svc-vri-cfg-aws-registry-tags-immutable`, `svc-vri-cfg-gcp-registry-tags-immutable`.*
- **CFG** — Branch protection requires signed commits. Source: Repository settings. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per run. *Row `KSI-SVC-VRI.verify.3`: not automated -- gap. Commits are not cryptographically signed and branch protection does not require it (recorded finding, DECISIONS.md 2026-10-03). Risk: A commit's author is GitHub's account attribution, not a verifiable signature.*
- **CFG** — Deploy pipeline blocks on signature verification failure rather than warning. Source: Pipeline definition. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per run. *Row `KSI-SVC-VRI.verify.4`: partly automated by `svc-vri-cfg-signature-verified-before-digest-recorded-analytics`, `svc-vri-cfg-signature-verified-before-digest-recorded`. Not covered: Verification is in the build, before the digest is recorded, so an unverifiable image never becomes a deployable reference. Nothing re-verifies at deploy.*
- **CFG** — Every resource class in the inventory has a recorded integrity position. Source: Register versus inventory from KSI-PIY-GIV. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per run. *Row `KSI-SVC-VRI.verify.5`: automated by `svc-vri-ops-register-integrity`.*
- **OPS** — Every running image digest matches a signed, verified image in the registry. Source: Running task digests versus registry signature status. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per run. *Row `KSI-SVC-VRI.validate.1`: partly automated by `svc-vri-ops-aws-running-images-signed` (judged only while aws-phase2 stands). Not covered: The signature's presence is checked, not re-verified against the signing identity; verification happens in the build, before the digest is recorded.*
- **OPS** — A deliberately unsigned image is rejected at deploy. Source: Test deployment. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per run. *Row `KSI-SVC-VRI.validate.2`: not automated -- gap. Nothing verifies a signature at deploy: verification happens in the build, before the digest is recorded, and deployment is by that digest (svc-vri-cfg-signature-verified-before-digest-recorded). An unsigned image pushed by hand would deploy if its digest were declared. Risk: Deploy-time trust rests on the build's verification and on declared digests, not on an admission check.*
- **OPS** — Log digest validation succeeds across the period. Source: CloudTrail validate-logs. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per run. *Row `KSI-SVC-VRI.validate.3`: automated by `svc-sin-ops-aws-trail-digests-validate`.*
- **OPS** — Zero deployments proceeded with a failed integrity check, standing query, zero results expected. Source: Pipeline execution history. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per run. *Row `KSI-SVC-VRI.validate.4`: partly automated by `svc-vri-ops-build-integrity`. Not covered: A recorded digest is what deployment uses; the apply that deploys it is the operator's, and nothing re-verifies there (SVC-VRI validate 2's gap).*
- **OPS** — Zero unsigned commits on the protected branch, standing query, zero results expected. Source: Repository commit history. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per run. *Row `KSI-SVC-VRI.validate.5`: not automated -- gap. As verify 3: commits are unsigned, so a count of unsigned commits on main would be all of them. Risk: As verify 3.*
- **Automated evidence.** 12 collector check(s) in the run on 2026-10-26: 2 NOT_STANDING, 10 PASS. Historical metrics (SDR-CSX-KMT) cover 19 day(s) of CI runs since 2026-10-02, in the metrics evidence object below; the year is what exists, not a full year.

### Assessment

- **No independent assessor is engaged.** The statements below are the provider's own assurance reasoning from the determination. They are not an independent assessment and should not be read as one.
- **Automation assurance.** Accuracy is tested by attempting to deploy an unsigned image and confirming rejection, then a signed one and confirming success; a signing pipeline that has never rejected anything is a configuration claim rather than a control. Separately by altering a dependency's expected hash and confirming the build fails. Zero-results queries are self-tested. Sufficiency is bounded by where cryptographic validation is possible at all: the claim covers artifacts this project produces and deploys and explicitly does not cover the internal integrity of managed services, which is inherited. That boundary is part of the determination rather than a caveat on it.
- **Limitations and residual risk.** Managed service internal integrity is inherited from the provider and asserted, which is the largest single gap here and is structural rather than a scoping choice. Running process and memory integrity has no cryptographic mechanism at this layer, the same gap ACM declared for two of the five named forms of drift. Signature verification proves an artifact came from the expected builder, not that it is free of vulnerabilities; integrity and safety are different properties and the second belongs to the supply chain cluster. Commit signing proves authorship, not correctness of the change, which belongs to change management. The synthetic environment has few artifacts, so integrity coverage is easy to achieve here in a way it would not be with a large image estate.

## KSI-SVC-VCM — Implemented

### Implementation

- Authenticity and integrity are two claims and only one is answered by transport security. TLS provides integrity directly, but it answers authenticity in one direction only: the client verifies the server, and the server usually verifies nothing about the client. Pointing at TLS for both halves would answer half the statement twice. Authenticity in this architecture comes from federated platform identity rather than transport: the pipeline job proves it is the expected workload by presenting a token the platform issued, validated against a trust configured to accept exactly that subject and audience, which is stronger than certificate-based mutual TLS here because the identity is issued by the platform rather than by a certificate we manage and could misissue. Scope is between machine-based resources, which excludes the public ingress path: a user's browser is not a machine-based information resource of this offering, so the most exposed path in the architecture is covered by SIN rather than here, which is correct by the statement's wording and worth stating rather than quietly assuming. IAM database authentication is a deliberate choice over a stored password: it authenticates the workload's platform identity rather than possession of a secret, which makes the peer's authenticity provable rather than inferred, and it removes a rotating secret from ASM's scope. Validated carries its defined meaning as in VRI, so the result must be acted on: a failed peer authentication that nothing consumes is authentication rather than validation, which is why failures route to the detection path. Persistently follows the standing reading, satisfied by the machine cycle.
- **1. Cross-cloud federation** — OIDC trust scoped to a specific subject and audience, no wildcards. Established under SNU, verified here for authenticity. *(Both; provisioned by Terraform)*
- **2. IAM database authentication** — RDS IAM auth enabled, task role granted connect, password authentication disabled for the application user. *(AWS; provisioned by Terraform)*
- **3. Task-side TLS** — Certificate on the task, TLS continued from the load balancer rather than terminated at it. *(AWS; provisioned by Terraform)*
- **4. Endpoint policies** — Interface endpoint policies restricting which principals may use them. *(AWS; provisioned by Terraform)*
- **5. Path register** — Every machine-to-machine path mapped to its authenticity mechanism and transport. *(Repo; provisioned by Version control)*
- **6. Authentication failure surfacing** — Failed authentications routed to the detection path built for SUS rather than logged silently. *(Both; provisioned by Terraform)*

### Validation

- **Cycle.** Required: 3 days. Current practice: the collector runs daily from GitHub Actions (.github/workflows/collect.yml), as a read-only identity in each cloud with no stored credential, and this record was emitted by that run. Daily is stricter than every required cycle above (DECISIONS.md, 2026-10-01).
- **CFG** — Federation trust names a specific subject and audience, no wildcards. Source: AWS OIDC provider and role trust policy; GCP workload identity pool. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per run. *Row `KSI-SVC-VCM.verify.1`: automated by `iam-snu-cfg-aws-role-trusts-pinned`, `iam-snu-cfg-gcp-wif-provider-pinned`.*
- **CFG** — RDS IAM authentication enabled and password authentication disabled for the application user. Source: RDS configuration; database role attributes. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per run. *Row `KSI-SVC-VCM.verify.2`: partly automated by `svc-sin-cfg-aws-database-settings` (judged only while aws-phase1 stands). Not covered: IAM authentication is read from RDS; that password authentication is disabled for the application user is a database role attribute, which this does not connect to read.*
- **CFG** — Target group uses TLS and the task certificate is present and valid. Source: ELB target group; task definition. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per run. *Row `KSI-SVC-VCM.verify.3`: partly automated by `svc-sin-cfg-aws-alb-tls` (judged only while aws-phase1 stands). Not covered: The target group's TLS is read; the certificate inside the task is not, since nothing outside the container can read it.*
- **CFG** — Interface endpoint policies restrict principals. Source: VPC endpoint policy documents. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per run. *Row `KSI-SVC-VCM.verify.4`: automated by `cna-rnt-cfg-aws-endpoint-policies` (judged only while aws-phase1 stands).*
- **CFG** — Every path in the register has a recorded authenticity mechanism. Source: Register versus inventory from KSI-PIY-GIV. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per run. *Row `KSI-SVC-VCM.verify.5`: automated by `svc-vcm-cfg-flow-paths-authenticated`.*
- **OPS** — Cross-cloud access observed using a federated token, not a static credential. Source: CloudTrail AssumeRoleWithWebIdentity events. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per run. *Row `KSI-SVC-VCM.validate.1`: automated by `svc-vcm-ops-aws-cross-cloud-federated`.*
- **OPS** — Database connections observed authenticating by IAM token, zero password authentications. Source: RDS connection logs. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per run. *Row `KSI-SVC-VCM.validate.2`: partly automated by `svc-vcm-ops-aws-db-connections-iam` (judged only while aws-phase2 stands). Not covered: Judged in sessions. One password authentication is declared and expected: the migration's master user, which creates the IAM-only roles. The application's own roles hold rds_iam and cannot use a password.*
- **OPS** — Zero non-TLS connections on any internal path, standing query, zero results expected. Source: Connection and access logs across paths. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per run. *Row `KSI-SVC-VCM.validate.3`: partly automated by `svc-vcm-ops-aws-internal-paths-over-tls` (judged only while aws-phase2 stands). Not covered: The task-to-AWS-API paths only. Database connections are not in CloudTrail (rds.force_ssl and the database-settings check stand in), and the load balancer-to-task hop is HTTPS by configuration, checked by svc-sin-cfg-aws-alb-tls, not observed.*
- **OPS** — A deliberately mis-scoped token is rejected. Source: Test with an unexpected subject. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per run. *Row `KSI-SVC-VCM.validate.4`: automated by `svc-vcm-ops-mis-scoped-token-rejected`.*
- **OPS** — Authentication failures appear in the detection path, not only in logs. Source: Failed authentication event correlated with detection ingestion. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per run. *Row `KSI-SVC-VCM.validate.5`: partly automated by `mla-osm-ops-aws-detection-query-ran`. Not covered: The daily detection query reads failed authentication from the corpus and alerts on it; no specific failure has been traced from its event to its alert.*
- **Automated evidence.** 6 collector check(s) in the run on 2026-10-26: 2 NOT_STANDING, 4 PASS. Historical metrics (SDR-CSX-KMT) cover 19 day(s) of CI runs since 2026-10-02, in the metrics evidence object below; the year is what exists, not a full year.

### Assessment

- **No independent assessor is engaged.** The statements below are the provider's own assurance reasoning from the determination. They are not an independent assessment and should not be read as one.
- **Automation assurance.** Accuracy is tested by presenting a token with an unexpected subject and confirming rejection, then the correct one and confirming success; a trust policy naming a specific subject is a configuration claim until something with the wrong subject is actually refused. Separately by attempting a password connection to the database and confirming refusal. Zero-results queries are self-tested. Sufficiency is bounded by observability of the peer: where a provider does not log the authenticating principal for a connection, that path's authenticity rests on configuration rather than observation, and it is marked as such in the register rather than counted as validated.
- **Limitations and residual risk.** Authenticity between AWS services over interface endpoints is provider-enforced; endpoint policies constrain which principals may use them, but the underlying peer authentication is inherited rather than demonstrated. The public ingress path is out of scope by the statement's wording, which is correct but means the most exposed communication path in the architecture is covered by SIN rather than here. Where connection logs do not record the authenticating principal, that path's authenticity is configuration-attested rather than observed. The synthetic environment has few paths, so the register is easy to keep complete here in a way it would not be in a service mesh with dozens of internal calls.

### Tests

- Negative control for `svc-vcm-cfg-flow-paths-authenticated` (collector/self_test.py): inputs that must fail it: path with no authenticity, rule naming an unknown group, empty register.
- Negative control for `svc-vcm-ops-aws-cross-cloud-federated` (collector/self_test.py): inputs that must fail it: no rows, query failed, query failed, zero expected, rows where none expected, unknown expectation.
- Negative control for `svc-vcm-ops-aws-internal-paths-over-tls` (collector/self_test.py): inputs that must fail it: no rows, query failed, query failed, zero expected, rows where none expected, unknown expectation.
- Negative control for `svc-vcm-ops-gcp-pipeline-schedule-runs` (collector/self_test.py): configurations that must fail it: last attempt refused, paused, never attempted, stale, missing.
- Negative control for `svc-vcm-ops-mis-scoped-token-rejected` (collector/self_test.py): inputs that must fail it: never run, latest failed, errored, too old, another scenario's record.
- Designed, not yet automated: A deliberately mis-scoped token is rejected (Test with an unexpected subject).

## KSI-SVC-PRR — Partially Implemented

### Implementation

- The statement names three subjects and they are not the same kind of thing. Plans and procedures are non-machine information resources and out of scope project-wide; the state of information resources is machine-based and buildable. Splitting by subject rather than deferring the indicator is what keeps the technical half, and it is the general rule this project applies wherever a statement mixes machine and non-machine subjects. Residual elements is undefined and is constructed here as anything that outlives the change that created or superseded it. That is distinct from drift under ACM, which compares declared against live at a point in time and would report clean on residue nobody ever declared and nobody deleted. The residue classes in this architecture are orphaned snapshots, detached volumes, superseded task definitions, stale security group rules referencing deleted resources, superseded secret versions, orphaned roles and policies, and unreferenced images. SC-4 is the single mapped control and is specifically about information remaining in shared resources after use, which confirms this is about data and access surviving their purpose rather than tidiness. Likely is a defined term meaning reasonable probability based on context, and it bounds severity: an unreferenced image is residue but unlikely to affect confidentiality, whereas an orphaned snapshot of the application database is, so items are ranked by whether they could plausibly hold or grant access to customer data. The mechanism is a diff across a change rather than a point-in-time scan, since residue is defined relative to a change event, and it reuses the inventory from GIV for enumeration and the change-path attribution from ACM for the event boundary.
- **1. Residue collector** — Inventory diff across change events, classified by residue type and ranked by whether the item could hold or grant access to customer data. *(Both; provisioned by Terraform)*
- **2. Retention register** — Declared exceptions: what may persist, why, and until when. Every entry carries a reason and an expiry. *(Repo; provisioned by Version control)*
- **3. Lifecycle policies** — Snapshot and image retention windows. Secret version deletion once rotation completes. *(Both; provisioned by Terraform)*
- **4. Change-boundary hook** — Residue review triggered on apply completion, not only on the periodic cycle. *(Both; provisioned by Terraform and CI)*

### Validation

- **Cycle.** Required: 3 days. Current practice: the collector runs daily from GitHub Actions (.github/workflows/collect.yml), as a read-only identity in each cloud with no stored credential, and this record was emitted by that run. Daily is stricter than every required cycle above (DECISIONS.md, 2026-10-01).
- **CFG** — Lifecycle policies exist on snapshots, images and secret versions. Source: Snapshot and image lifecycle configuration; Secrets Manager settings. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per run. *Row `KSI-SVC-PRR.verify.1`: partly automated by `svc-prr-cfg-aws-ecr-lifecycle`. Not covered: Images in ECR. Database snapshots expire by the instance's 7-day backup retention, checked while phase 1 stands (svc-sin-cfg-aws-database-settings); secret versions are checked in sessions (svc-prr-ops-aws-secret-versions); Artifact Registry's cleanup policy is in dry run until reviewed.*
- **CFG** — Retention register exists and every entry has a reason and an expiry. Source: Repository file, checked in CI. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per run. *Row `KSI-SVC-PRR.verify.2`: automated by `svc-prr-cfg-retention-register-complete`.*
- **CFG** — Change-boundary hook is wired to apply completion. Source: Pipeline definition. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per run. *Row `KSI-SVC-PRR.verify.3`: not automated -- gap. There is no apply in the pipeline, so no change-boundary hook to wire to its completion; applies are the operator's, by elevation. Risk: Residue review after a change is not triggered automatically.*
- **OPS** — Zero snapshots without a live source resource or a register entry, standing query, zero results expected. Source: Inventory diff against the inventory from KSI-PIY-GIV. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per change. *Row `KSI-SVC-PRR.validate.1`: automated by `svc-prr-ops-aws-snapshots-accounted`.*
- **OPS** — Zero security group rules referencing deleted resources, standing query, zero results expected. Source: Rule target resolution. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per change. *Row `KSI-SVC-PRR.validate.2`: automated by `svc-prr-ops-aws-sg-references-resolve` (judged only while aws-phase1 stands).*
- **OPS** — Zero retrievable superseded secret versions beyond their window. Source: Secrets Manager version listing. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per change. *Row `KSI-SVC-PRR.validate.3`: partly automated by `svc-prr-ops-aws-secret-versions` (judged only while aws-phase1 stands). Not covered: Secrets exist only during sessions and are deleted at teardown, so a superseded version cannot outlive one; checked while they stand.*
- **OPS** — A deliberately orphaned resource is detected after the change that orphaned it. Source: Test: create, deploy, delete source, confirm detection. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per change. *Row `KSI-SVC-PRR.validate.4`: not automated -- gap. As PIY-GIV validate 2: a deliberately orphaned resource would sit in Config's record for up to two days. Orphans are found by the daily declared-inventory and snapshot-accounting checks. Risk: Detection of an orphan from a specific change is not demonstrated end to end.*
- **OPS** — Every change event has a corresponding residue review. Source: Apply history correlated with review runs. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per change. *Row `KSI-SVC-PRR.validate.5`: not automated -- gap. As verify 3: with no pipeline apply there is no change event to correlate a residue review with. Teardown verification (boundary.py) is the per-session residue review, recorded in DECISIONS.md. Evidence: DECISIONS.md, each session's teardown verification. Risk: A change between sessions that leaves residue is found only by the daily inventory and drift checks, not by a review tied to the change.*
- **Automated evidence.** 5 collector check(s) in the run on 2026-10-26: 2 NOT_STANDING, 3 PASS. Historical metrics (SDR-CSX-KMT) cover 19 day(s) of CI runs since 2026-10-02, in the metrics evidence object below; the year is what exists, not a full year.

### Assessment

- **No independent assessor is engaged.** The statements below are the provider's own assurance reasoning from the determination. They are not an independent assessment and should not be read as one.
- **Automation assurance.** Accuracy is tested by deliberately orphaning a resource and confirming detection, then registering it as retained and confirming it stops failing. That second step matters: a collector that cannot accept a legitimate exception will be ignored, and an ignored collector is worse than none. Sufficiency is bounded by change visibility: residue from a change made outside the pipeline has no event boundary to diff against and is caught only by the periodic cycle rather than at the change.
- **Limitations and residual risk.** Plans and procedures are out of scope, so two of three subjects are unaddressed and the indicator is partially satisfied. The resulting risk is that procedural residue, such as a runbook referencing a decommissioned system or an access process for a role that no longer exists, is not reviewed. Residue from out-of-band changes lacks a change boundary and is caught later by the cycle rather than at the event. The synthetic environment changes infrequently and deliberately, so residue accumulates far more slowly than in a real deployment where this is a genuine operational problem.

### Tests

- Negative control for `svc-prr-cfg-aws-ecr-lifecycle` (collector/self_test.py): configurations that must fail it: no policy, no rules, no repositories.
- Negative control for `svc-prr-cfg-retention-register-complete` (collector/self_test.py): inputs that must fail it: no expiry, no reason, negative days, empty register.
- Negative control for `svc-prr-ops-aws-snapshots-accounted` (collector/self_test.py): configurations that must fail it: orphaned.
- Designed, not yet automated: A deliberately orphaned resource is detected after the change that orphaned it (Test: create, deploy, delete source, confirm detection).

## KSI-SVC-RUD — Not Implemented

### Implementation

- **Deferred.** The indicator is triggered by an event this environment cannot produce. It requires removal when requested by an agency in alignment with customer agreements, and the persona has no federal agency customers, no customer agreements, and no real federal customer data as FRD-FCD defines it, which is information an agency uploads or supplies. Building a deletion capability without a request mechanism would demonstrate the easy half and skip the half the statement is actually about, which is responding to a request within an agreed timeframe. The technical difficulty is also worth naming rather than hiding: removing data from backups conflicts directly with the point-in-time recovery window this project enabled for the recovery cluster, since a restorable window necessarily retains prior state, and reconciling those two is a genuine design problem rather than a configuration setting. Resulting risk to customers: a real provider unable to remove spilled data on request leaves that data recoverable from backups for the length of the retention window, which is a direct and well-understood exposure.
- **Resulting risk.** This is a clean deferral: the triggering condition does not exist in the environment rather than the capability being withheld. The tension between data removal and point-in-time recovery is noted as a real design problem a live provider would have to solve, and is not solved here.

### Validation

- **Cycle.** Required: 3 months. Current practice: the collector runs daily from GitHub Actions (.github/workflows/collect.yml), as a read-only identity in each cloud with no stored credential, and this record was emitted by that run. Daily is stricter than every required cycle above (DECISIONS.md, 2026-10-01).
- **N/A** — No evidence produced. Source: Deferred. Required every 3 months; implemented: Not implemented.
- **Automated evidence.** No collector check exists for this indicator yet, so no evidence is attached. The rows above are the determination's design.

### Assessment

- **No independent assessor is engaged.** The statements below are the provider's own assurance reasoning from the determination. They are not an independent assessment and should not be read as one.
- **Limitations and residual risk.** This is a clean deferral: the triggering condition does not exist in the environment rather than the capability being withheld. The tension between data removal and point-in-time recovery is noted as a real design problem a live provider would have to solve, and is not solved here.

### Tests

- None run or designed.

## KSI-SVC-EIS — Partially Implemented

### Implementation

- The mappings make this a vulnerability management and attack-surface indicator rather than a program property: SI-2(2) is automated flaw remediation status, SI-4 is system monitoring, CM-7(1) is periodic review of functions and ports, SC-39 is process isolation, MA-2 is controlled maintenance and SR-10 is supply chain inspection. Opportunities to improve security is undefined and is constructed here as three named sources rather than an open-ended aspiration: vulnerability findings from image and dependency scanning, provider posture findings, and attack-surface findings covering enabled functions, open ports and unused permissions, the last of which is sourced from the ELP collector rather than re-derived. Anything beyond those three would be invented, so the boundary is stated. The second half of the statement is what makes this indicator different from every other one in the cluster: the others ask whether a state holds, this one asks whether a change happened in response to a finding, and a determination proving only that scanning exists would answer half the statement. The improvement mechanism here is rebuild rather than patch, because tasks are immutable and updates arrive as a new image, so improvements made is measured as base image currency and rebuild cadence against open findings, which is more observable than a patch schedule. Cadence takes its numbers from the vulnerability response rules rather than the standing machine cycle, and the wording matters: at Class C those provisions are recommendations rather than obligations, being detection on drift-prone resources at least every 14 days and on representative samples of similar machine-based resources at least every 3 days, so the plan adopts them as a declared choice rather than presenting them as requirements met. Remediation timeframes come from the Potential Agency Impact model already adopted, so this indicator does not invent its own clock. Information resources is untyped, so the non-machine half is in scope by the statement and out by this project's scoping decision, making the determination partial for the same reason GIV is.
- **1. Image and dependency scanning** — Security Hub Essentials with Inspector scanning on push and on schedule. Dependency scanning in CI. *(AWS; provisioned by Terraform and CI)*
- **2. Provider posture findings** — Security Hub findings and CIS benchmark assessments; Security Command Center findings on the GCP side. *(Both; provisioned by Terraform)*
- **3. Attack-surface review** — Open ports, enabled functions and unused permissions enumerated. Unused permissions sourced from the ELP collector rather than re-derived. *(Both; provisioned by Terraform)*
- **4. Rebuild pipeline** — Scheduled base image rebuild, plus an automatic rebuild triggered by a qualifying finding. *(Both; provisioned by Terraform and CI)*
- **5. Finding-to-change linkage** — Every closed finding references the commit, image digest or apply that closed it. *(Repo; provisioned by CI)*
- **6. Declared evaluation plan** — Intervals per resource class and severity thresholds, following the Class C recommendations, versioned. *(Repo; provisioned by Version control)*

### Validation

- **Cycle.** Required: 14 days, 3 days. Current practice: the collector runs daily from GitHub Actions (.github/workflows/collect.yml), as a read-only identity in each cloud with no stored credential, and this record was emitted by that run. Daily is stricter than every required cycle above (DECISIONS.md, 2026-10-01).
- **CFG** — Scanning enabled on registries and in CI. Source: Security Hub and Inspector configuration; pipeline definition. Required every 14 days; implemented: Per plan. *Row `KSI-SVC-EIS.verify.1`: automated by `scr-mon-cfg-dependencies-audited`, `svc-eis-cfg-aws-registry-scanning`, `svc-eis-cfg-gcp-container-scanning`.*
- **CFG** — Posture findings ingested from both clouds. Source: Security Hub; Security Command Center. Required every 14 days; implemented: Per plan. *Row `KSI-SVC-EIS.verify.2`: partly automated by `cna-ibp-cfg-aws-securityhub-standards`. Not covered: AWS only: GCP has no Security Command Center without an organization, so its posture is the collector's GCP checks rather than an ingested service.*
- **CFG** — Declared evaluation plan exists and covers every resource class. Source: Repository file, checked in CI. Required every 14 days; implemented: Per plan. *Row `KSI-SVC-EIS.verify.3`: automated by `svc-eis-ops-register-evaluation`.*
- **CFG** — Rebuild pipeline has both a schedule and a finding trigger. Source: Pipeline definition. Required every 14 days; implemented: Per plan. *Row `KSI-SVC-EIS.verify.4`: not automated -- gap. The build runs on pushes that touch the application and by hand; it has no schedule and no trigger from a finding (build-and-push.yml). Risk: A finding against a deployed image waits for a person to rebuild; base image updates are not picked up on their own.*
- **OPS** — Every deployed image digest has a scan result within its declared interval. Source: Scan results versus running digests. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per plan. *Row `KSI-SVC-EIS.validate.1`: partly automated by `svc-eis-cfg-aws-image-scan-coverage`. Not covered: ECR images, by coverage status, not by a scan date against a declared interval; Inspector rescans on new advisories rather than on a clock. Coverage also lapses: Inspector stops scanning an image 14 days after its push unless it is in use, and the images run only during sessions, so between sessions every deployable image goes INACTIVE (SCAN_ELIGIBILITY_EXPIRED). Red since 2026-10-16, a recorded finding (DECISIONS.md, 2026-10-19). The analytics image in Artifact Registry is scanned by Container Scanning, not read here.*
- **OPS** — Zero findings open beyond their impact-derived timeframe, standing query, zero results expected. Source: Finding age against severity. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per plan. *Row `KSI-SVC-EIS.validate.2`: not automated -- gap. Remediation timeframes are adopted from the Potential Agency Impact model in principle (registers/resources.yaml) but not declared as days per severity, so a finding's age has no number to be held to. Risk: An old finding is visible in Inspector and Security Hub but nothing fails on its age.*
- **OPS** — Zero findings closed without a linked commit, digest or apply, standing query, zero results expected. Source: Finding closure records. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per plan. *Row `KSI-SVC-EIS.validate.3`: not automated -- gap. Findings close in Inspector when a rescan no longer sees them; no closure record links a commit, digest or apply. Risk: A finding that closes because an image was deleted reads the same as one that was fixed.*
- **OPS** — A rebuild is observed occurring in response to a finding, not only on schedule. Source: Finding timestamp correlated with image build. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per plan. *Row `KSI-SVC-EIS.validate.4`: not automated -- gap. As verify 4: no rebuild is triggered by a finding. Risk: As verify 4.*
- **OPS** — Base image age is within the declared currency window. Source: Running image base layer dates. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per plan. *Row `KSI-SVC-EIS.validate.5`: not automated -- gap. No base image currency window is declared; the base image is Debian 12 in LTS, recorded with a mitigation due 2026-10-31 (registers/supply-chain-risks.yaml). Risk: An aging base image is caught as advisories accrue against it, not by its age.*
- **OPS** — Every machine-based resource in the inventory has evaluation coverage, zero unaccounted. Source: Reconciliation against the inventory generated for KSI-PIY-GIV. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per plan. *Row `KSI-SVC-EIS.validate.6`: automated by `svc-eis-ops-register-evaluation`.*
- **Automated evidence.** 4 collector check(s) in the run on 2026-10-26: 1 FAIL, 3 PASS. Historical metrics (SDR-CSX-KMT) cover 19 day(s) of CI runs since 2026-10-02, in the metrics evidence object below; the year is what exists, not a full year.

### Assessment

- **No independent assessor is engaged.** The statements below are the provider's own assurance reasoning from the determination. They are not an independent assessment and should not be read as one.
- **Automation assurance.** Accuracy is tested by introducing a dependency with a known vulnerability and confirming detection, then confirming the rebuild trigger fires and the finding closes with a linked digest. Separately by closing a finding manually without a change and confirming the linkage check fails it, since a closed finding with nothing behind it is a suppressed finding rather than an improvement. Sufficiency is bounded by scanner coverage: findings exist for what the scanner recognises, so a vulnerability in an unscanned layer or an unrecognised package format is invisible, and the claim is scoped to detected opportunities rather than all opportunities.
- **Limitations and residual risk.** Non-machine information resources are out of scope, so opportunities to improve security in procedures, training and organizational practice are not evaluated and the indicator is partial for the same reason GIV is. Scanner coverage bounds detection, so this is a claim about detected opportunities. The evaluation intervals follow Class C recommendations that are stated as SHOULD rather than MUST, so following them is a choice this provider makes rather than an obligation it meets. Managed service internals are patched by the provider, so improvement there is inherited rather than made, which parallels the inherited integrity gap in VRI. The synthetic environment generates few findings, so the improvement loop is exercised deliberately rather than observed operating under real vulnerability pressure.

### Tests

- Negative control for `svc-eis-cfg-aws-registry-scanning` (collector/self_test.py): configurations that must fail it: basic scanning, narrow filter, push only, Inspector off for ECR.
- Negative control for `svc-eis-cfg-gcp-container-scanning` (collector/self_test.py): configurations that must fail it: one disabled, unknown, none named.
- Negative control for `svc-eis-ops-register-evaluation` (collector/self_test.py): inputs that must fail it: inventoried class without an entry, entry without the dimension, reduced availability without a reason, no backup without a reason, objective half-declared, evaluated less often than the plan, data store naming a missing check, empty statement, empty inventory.

## KSI-CNA-RNT — Implemented

### Implementation

- Limit cannot mean that some restriction exists. Security groups deny all inbound by default and allow all outbound by default, so a resource with a stock group already has a restriction on one side and none on the other, and a check accepting any restriction would pass nearly everything. The reading taken is explicit allow-lists in both directions, with default-allow-all outbound failing. Appropriately is undefined and is bounded by SC-7(5), the mapped control, which is deny by default and allow by exception, so appropriate is a structural property rather than a judgment about business need: every allowed flow is enumerated and everything else is denied. Scope has two categories and only one has a network interface. Resources with interfaces are limited by security groups and firewall rules; resources without them, meaning object storage, the analytics warehouse and secrets, are limited by resource policy and access boundary, and a check built only around interfaces silently misses the second category, which is the more damaging exposure because it is data rather than a network path. That second category is largely SIN's work and is referenced rather than rebuilt; what RNT adds for it is the network-boundary dimension specifically, being whether access is restricted to the VPC or reachable from anywhere with the right credential. The architecture makes the outbound claim unusually strong: with no NAT gateway and no internet gateway route on the private subnets, tasks have no route to the internet at all, so outbound is absent rather than filtered, which is a different and better claim and is the reason the endpoint cost was accepted. Coverage is the failure mode that matters most, since a resource with no rule set attached is worse than one with a bad rule because nobody is looking at it, so coverage is checked independently of rule content. Boundary against neighbours: ULN covers logical networking enforcing flow controls at the architecture level, MAT covers attack surface and lateral movement, RVP covers protection against attack traffic, and RNT is narrower than all three at per-resource traffic configuration.
- **1. Security group design** — Per-service groups. Inbound from the specific upstream group only. Outbound enumerated to the endpoint group and the database group, never 0.0.0.0/0. *(AWS; provisioned by Terraform)*
- **2. Public-facing declaration** — Versioned list of resources permitted inbound from the internet. Currently the load balancer only. The application domain resolves to it directly, DNS-only rather than proxied: proxied mode would place the DNS provider inside the offering boundary, terminate TLS there, and add it to KSI-CNA-IBP's third-party register, for capability WAF and Shield Standard already supply. *(Repo; provisioned by Version control)*
- **3. No internet route** — Private subnets carry no internet or NAT gateway route. Egress reaches AWS services through endpoints only. *(AWS; provisioned by Terraform)*
- **4. Endpoint policies** — Interface endpoint policies restricting principals and actions. *(AWS; provisioned by Terraform)*
- **5. GCP firewall rules** — Default-deny ingress and egress with explicit allows. Cloud Run ingress set to internal. *(GCP; provisioned by Terraform)*
- **6. Data resource boundaries** — VPC-restricted access on storage and warehouse resources where the platform supports it. *(Both; provisioned by Terraform)*

### Assessment

- **No independent assessor is engaged.** The statements below are the provider's own assurance reasoning from the determination. They are not an independent assessment and should not be read as one.
- **Automation assurance.** Accuracy is tested by adding an over-permissive egress rule to a disposable security group and confirming the collector fails it, then removing it. The absent-egress claim is tested by attempting an outbound connection from a running task and confirming it fails rather than succeeding slowly, since a route table with no internet route is a configuration claim until a connection actually fails. Zero-results queries are self-tested. Sufficiency is bounded by flow log coverage: flow logs record accepted and rejected flows at the interface level, so traffic between resources that never traverses a logged interface is invisible, and the claim is scoped accordingly.
- **Limitations and residual risk.** Flow logs record traffic at the interface level, so intra-task or intra-process communication is not visible. Egress to AWS services through interface endpoints is permitted by design, so a compromised task can still reach whatever its endpoint policies allow; absent internet egress limits exfiltration paths rather than eliminating them, and the endpoint policies bound the remainder. The GCP side has fewer resources with network interfaces, since Cloud Run and BigQuery are largely boundary-controlled rather than firewall-controlled, so the two clouds are asymmetric here for structural reasons rather than by choice. The synthetic environment has few resources and few flows, so rule review is easy in a way it would not be at scale, where security group sprawl is the real failure mode.

- Negative control for `cna-rnt-cfg-aws-endpoint-policies` (collector/self_test.py): configurations that must fail it: open principal beyond the excepted resource, excepted resource, every action, AWS default policy, service wildcard, anyone, unconditioned, no policy.
- Negative control for `cna-rnt-cfg-aws-no-open-egress` (collector/self_test.py): configurations that must fail it: undeclared group open, declared group, other port, range including the port, all protocols, IPv6 everywhere, no rules.
- Negative control for `cna-rnt-cfg-aws-no-open-ingress` (collector/self_test.py): configurations that must fail it: undeclared group open, declared group, other port, range including the port, all protocols, IPv6 everywhere, no rules.

- Negative control for `cna-rnt-ops-register-network` (collector/self_test.py): inputs that must fail it: inventoried class without an entry, entry without the dimension, reduced availability without a reason, no backup without a reason, objective half-declared, evaluated less often than the plan, data store naming a missing check, empty statement, empty inventory.

- Designed, not yet automated: An outbound connection attempt to an internet destination fails (Deliberate test from a running task).

## KSI-CNA-ULN — Implemented

### Implementation

- And related capabilities is not padding, and the mappings prove it: AC-12 is session termination and SC-10 is network disconnect, neither of which is topological, while SC-7(7) is split tunneling prevention and SC-4 is information in shared resources. The statement therefore reaches beyond subnets and route tables into session lifecycle and boundary enforcement generally, which is what resolves the asymmetry between the two clouds rather than a convenient stretch: the analytics warehouse has no VPC and the pipeline service's ingress control is a service setting rather than a network boundary, so a purely topological reading would leave half the architecture uncovered. Flow control is enforced at four layers here. Topology, being subnet tiering, route tables and the absence of an internet gateway on private subnets. Boundary, being interface endpoint policies and VPC-restricted access on data resources. Service boundary, being internal-only ingress and dataset access on the GCP side. And session, being idle timeouts, connection lifetimes and bounded token lifetimes. The session layer would have been missed without reading AC-12 and SC-10: a connection that never terminates defeats flow control by outliving the conditions that authorised it, and nothing in a security group notices. The distinction from RNT is precise and is built as evidence rather than argued: RNT asks whether each resource's own traffic is bounded, ULN asks whether the topology constrains flows independently of any resource's configuration, and the test is whether flows would still be constrained if every security group were wiped. SC-7(7) has a specific meaning here despite the absence of a VPN: the equivalent property is that a workload cannot simultaneously reach an internal resource and an external destination, which absent internet egress supplies for free.
- **1. Subnet tiering** — Public subnets hold the load balancer only. Compute and database in separate private subnets with distinct route tables. *(AWS; provisioned by Terraform)*
- **2. Route table constraint** — Private route tables reference local and endpoint routes only. No internet or NAT route. *(AWS; provisioned by Terraform)*
- **3. Endpoint policies** — Principal and action restrictions on every interface endpoint, so the endpoint layer is a boundary rather than a tunnel. *(AWS; provisioned by Terraform)*
- **4. GCP service boundaries** — Cloud Run ingress internal. BigQuery dataset access bound to declared roles. *(GCP; provisioned by Terraform)*
- **5. Session bounds** — Load balancer idle timeout, database connection lifetime, bounded token lifetimes on federated trusts. *(Both; provisioned by Terraform)*
- **6. Flow control register** — Every flow path recorded with the layer enforcing it, so no path relies on a single layer. *(Repo; provisioned by Version control)*

- **Cycle.** Required: 3 days. Not covered: The app and data tiers' routes are private, so nothing placed there is public; each workload's subnet is not reconciled against the inventory.*

### Assessment

- **No independent assessor is engaged.** The statements below are the provider's own assurance reasoning from the determination. They are not an independent assessment and should not be read as one.
- **Automation assurance.** Accuracy is tested by placing a disposable resource in a public subnet and confirming failure, and by removing an endpoint policy and confirming failure. The topology claim is tested by permitting all traffic on a disposable resource's security group and confirming internet egress still fails, which is the strongest single piece of evidence in this determination and the one most likely to be skipped, since it is the only test that separates this indicator from RNT rather than describing the same controls twice. Zero-results queries are self-tested. Sufficiency is bounded by what flow logs record; session termination is observable where the service logs it, and where it is not the claim rests on configuration and is marked accordingly in the register, following the pattern set in VCM.
- **Limitations and residual risk.** The GCP side has no topological layer in the classical sense, since the analytics warehouse has no VPC and the pipeline service's ingress control is a service setting, so flow control there rests on boundary and session layers rather than four and the register records which layers apply per path rather than implying parity. VPC Service Controls would supply a topological perimeter on that side and is not used, so the asymmetry is a design gap rather than a platform limitation. Session termination is observable only where the service logs it. The synthetic environment has few flow paths, so the register is easy to keep complete here in a way it would not be with many services.

- Negative control for `cna-uln-ops-permissive-group-still-no-egress` (collector/self_test.py): inputs that must fail it: never run, latest failed, errored, too old, another scenario's record.
