## KSI-CNA-MAT — Partially Implemented

### Implementation

- The mappings make attack surface three-dimensional and the third dimension is invisible from the statement. Network surface covers exposed ports, reachable endpoints and access points, per SC-7(3), SC-7(4) and SC-7(8). Identity surface covers what a compromised resource can reach with the credentials it holds, per AC-20(1). Application surface covers SI-10 input validation, SI-11 error handling and information leakage, and SI-16 memory protection, which are properties of the code inside the container rather than the infrastructure around it, and are the reason this determination is partial. Lateral movement is a graph question rather than a configuration question: what matters is what an attacker reaches from a compromised resource, which combines network reachability and identity permission, and neither alone is sufficient because a task with a narrow security group and a broad role has poor containment despite looking fine on the network. The per-service segmentation added during the architecture review is what makes the network half non-trivial; without it, compromising either service would yield the network position of both. Minimal is comparative and needs a baseline to be checkable, so it is bounded as no port open that no declared flow uses, no permission granted that no declared operation needs, and no capability enabled that no declared function requires, reusing the flow register from ULN and the role model from ELP rather than inventing a third baseline. Boundary against neighbours turns on the assumed starting position: RNT and ULN assume the perimeter holds, MAT assumes it did not. Wireless controls in the mapping do not apply to a cloud-hosted offering and are declared inapplicable rather than dropped.
- **1. Per-service segmentation** — One security group per service with explicit service-to-service rules. Services cannot reach each other. *(AWS; provisioned by Terraform)*
- **2. Task role minimisation** — Per-service task roles scoped to declared operations. No shared execution-plus-task role. *(AWS; provisioned by Terraform)*
- **3. Container hardening** — Read-only root filesystem, non-root user, dropped Linux capabilities, no privileged mode. The cheapest surface reduction available and the container-level analogue of network segmentation. *(AWS; provisioned by Terraform)*
- **4. Blast radius computation** — Combined network and identity reachability per resource, compared against a declared blast radius. *(Both; provisioned by Terraform)*
- **5. Application-layer position** — Static analysis and dependency scanning in the pipeline for input handling. Declared as partial coverage. *(Repo; provisioned by CI)*
- **6. Surface register** — Per resource: open ports, granted permissions and enabled capabilities, each traced to a declared need. *(Repo; provisioned by Version control)*

- **Cycle.** Required: 3 days. Daily is stricter than every required cycle above (DECISIONS.md, 2026-10-01).

- **CFG** — Each service has a distinct security group and task role. Source: Task definitions and group associations. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per run. *Row `KSI-CNA-MAT.verify.3`: automated by `cna-mat-cfg-aws-distinct-service-identity` (judged only while aws-phase2 stands).*
- **CFG** — No task role permission lacks a declared operation. Source: Task role policies versus the role model from KSI-IAM-ELP. Task role permissions are enumerated per role, but not reconciled operation by operation against a declared role model; the flows register covers network paths, not API operations. Risk: A task role permission that no declared operation needs would persist unnoticed.*

Not covered: Run once per environment session and recorded (cadence 30 days), not every 3: the environment it needs stands only during sessions.*

Combined network and identity reachability analysis is the effective-access mechanism, which is not built. Risk: Reachability is shown piecewise -- security groups against flows, routes, deliberate probes -- not computed whole.*

VPC flow logs go to a CloudWatch log group that is part of the session environment and deleted at teardown (network.tf); they are not in the central store and nothing queries them. Risk: Accepted flows on undeclared ports are not queried.*

### Assessment

- **No independent assessor is engaged.** The statements below are the provider's own assurance reasoning from the determination. They are not an independent assessment and should not be read as one.
- **Automation assurance.** Accuracy is tested by opening a port with no declared flow and confirming failure, and by granting a task role an undeclared permission and confirming the blast radius computation flags the widened path. The service-to-service test runs positively and negatively: confirm the connection fails, then temporarily permit it and confirm the collector notices. Sufficiency is bounded in two places. Reachability is computed from declared configuration, so a path created by application logic, such as two services communicating through a shared queue, is outside it. And the application-layer surface is not verifiable by infrastructure inspection at all.
- **Limitations and residual risk.** Application-layer attack surface is in scope by mapping and is not verified here. SI-10, SI-11 and SI-16 concern input validation, error handling and memory protection, which are properties of application code; static analysis and dependency scanning partially address the first two, and memory protection is a runtime property of the language and platform and is inherited. This is the largest declared gap in the determination and it is structural, since an infrastructure collector cannot inspect application logic. Wireless controls in the mapping do not apply to a cloud-hosted offering and are declared inapplicable. Reachability is computed from declared configuration, so application-mediated paths are invisible. The synthetic environment has four resources and few paths, so blast radius computation is tractable here in a way it would not be with hundreds of roles, where this is genuinely hard.

- Negative control for `cna-mat-ops-service-to-service-refused` (collector/self_test.py): inputs that must fail it: never run, latest failed, errored, too old, another scenario's record.
- Designed, not yet automated: A connection attempt from one service to the other fails (Deliberate test).

## KSI-CNA-DFP — Implemented

### Implementation

- Strictly defined is about enumeration rather than size, and that is what separates this indicator from MAT. MAT asks whether the permitted set is small; DFP asks whether it is written down at all. A service with a large but fully enumerated permitted set satisfies DFP and fails MAT, and a service with a small but undeclared set does the reverse. CM-2, baseline configuration, supports that reading: a baseline defines intended state rather than judging how tight it is. The population is infrastructure and services rather than people, so it overlaps ELP and JIT only at task roles, where the question differs: ELP asks whether the role is least-privileged, DFP asks whether its permitted set is explicitly stated rather than inherited or implied. SI-3, malicious code protection, is the awkward mapping and is addressed rather than ignored: a workload that can execute only the code it was defined to run has no path to execute injected code, so immutable digest-pinned images, read-only root filesystems and dropped capabilities are malicious-code protection by construction rather than by scanning. That is a defensible reading and not the conventional one, so it is declared. The failure this indicator catches and others do not is functionality or privilege arriving by default rather than by declaration: a managed service enabled with its default feature set, a role granted a managed policy rather than an explicit one, a container with an unspecified command inheriting the image entrypoint. None of those is necessarily excessive, so MAT might pass them; none is declared, so DFP does not.
- **1. Explicit task definitions** — Command, entrypoint, ports, capabilities and user stated rather than inherited from the image. *(AWS; provisioned by Terraform)*
- **2. Customer-managed policies only** — No AWS-managed or GCP predefined roles on any workload identity. Explicit enumerated policies, since managed policy contents change outside our control. *(Both; provisioned by Terraform)*
- **3. Managed service feature configuration** — Configurable feature sets declared explicitly rather than left at provider default. *(Both; provisioned by Terraform)*
- **4. Pinned pipeline actions** — Workflow steps pinned to commit SHAs rather than tags or branches, applying the digest-pinning reasoning from VRI to the supply chain. *(Repo; provisioned by Version control)*
- **5. Definition register** — Per component: declared functionality, declared privileges, and the mechanism enforcing each. *(Repo; provisioned by Version control)*

### Validation

- **Cycle.** Required: 3 days. Current practice: the collector runs daily from GitHub Actions (.github/workflows/collect.yml), as a read-only identity in each cloud with no stored credential, and this record was emitted by that run. Daily is stricter than every required cycle above (DECISIONS.md, 2026-10-01).
- **CFG** — Every task definition states command, ports, capabilities and user explicitly. Source: ECS task definitions. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per run. *Row `KSI-CNA-DFP.verify.1`: automated by `cna-dfp-cfg-aws-tasks-explicit` (judged only while aws-phase2 stands).*
- **CFG** — No workload identity holds a managed or predefined policy. Source: IAM attached policies; GCP role bindings. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per run. *Row `KSI-CNA-DFP.verify.2`: partly automated by `cna-dfp-cfg-aws-no-managed-policies-on-workloads`. Not covered: AWS only. GCP workload identities hold narrow predefined roles scoped to single resources, a recorded difference: GCP has no practical inline policy for service identities (infra/gcp/pipeline.tf).*
- **CFG** — Configurable managed services carry explicit feature configuration. Source: Service configuration APIs. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per run. *Row `KSI-CNA-DFP.verify.3`: partly automated by `cna-ibp-cfg-aws-securityhub-standards`, `iam-sus-cfg-aws-guardduty-declared-plans`. Not covered: Security Hub's standards, declared explicitly. GuardDuty's plans are declared feature by feature; Security Hub's standards and ECS's Container Insights are declared too, each judged by its own check or position. Not every managed service's features are enumerated here.*

Not covered: A call outside a workload's enumerated policy is denied by IAM, and the denial is what this counts; a successful call is by construction within the policy.*
- **OPS** — Zero processes executing outside the declared container command, subject to platform visibility. Source: Container runtime observation where available. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per run. *Row `KSI-CNA-DFP.validate.3`: not automated -- gap. Process-level observation needs a runtime agent. GuardDuty Runtime Monitoring was decided against on 2026-10-07: on Fargate it creates its own VPC endpoint and security group outside Terraform, which can block teardown, and it bills after its trial. Risk: A process running outside the declared command would not be seen.*
- **OPS** — Every component in the inventory has a definition entry, zero unaccounted. Source: Reconciliation against the inventory generated for KSI-PIY-GIV. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per run. *Row `KSI-CNA-DFP.validate.4`: automated by `cna-dfp-ops-register-definition`.*

### Assessment

- **No independent assessor is engaged.** The statements below are the provider's own assurance reasoning from the determination. They are not an independent assessment and should not be read as one.
- **Automation assurance.** Accuracy is tested by attaching a managed policy to a disposable role and confirming failure, and by removing an explicit command from a task definition and confirming failure. The definition register is tested by adding a component without an entry and confirming the reconciliation flags it. Sufficiency is bounded by what the platform exposes about running state: declared configuration is fully readable, whereas what a container actually executes at runtime is largely not on this platform, so the runtime process check is partial by platform rather than by design and is stated as such rather than presented as complete.
- **Limitations and residual risk.** Runtime execution is not observable on this platform beyond what it exposes, so the claim that a container executes only its declared functionality rests on image immutability and the read-only filesystem rather than on runtime inspection. Malicious code protection has no traditional scanner: the position is that strictly defined executable functionality is the malicious-code control in this architecture, which is a defensible but unconventional reading of SI-3 and is declared rather than assumed. Managed service internal functionality is defined by the provider and inherited, paralleling the inherited gaps in VRI and EIS. The synthetic environment has few components, so the definition register is easy to keep complete here.

## KSI-CNA-EIS — Partially Implemented

### Implementation

- Automated services is read as managed services performing the assessment, with this project's own collector performing a meta-assessment that verifies those services are enabled, correctly scoped and producing results. The alternative is self-referential, a tool asserting that a tool exists, whereas this way the findings come from services independent of anything the project built and the collector's job is to confirm the independent services work. That is the same concern raised in SUS about a responder logging to storage it cannot modify, applied to assessment rather than response. Intended operational state is broader than configuration, or the word operational adds nothing to configured, so it is read as three layers: declared configuration, where live matches declared; running health, where desired task count is met, tasks are healthy and scheduled jobs are executing; and security posture, where rules and standards checks pass. The running-health layer holds this indicator's best evidence and it is not something this project built: the container service maintaining desired count and replacing failing tasks is automatic enforcement in the strictest sense, acting in seconds without a human, and it is a platform guarantee rather than project code, so claiming it is both more honest and stronger than claiming our own remediation. Automatic enforcement is deliberately narrow. Unattended apply of a full plan is dangerous because a plan containing a destructive change executes without anyone reading it, and this project has established that infrastructure destruction is irreversible in a way identity changes are not, so automatic enforcement covers remediation actions for specific safe reversible drift types plus native health enforcement, and everything else is detected automatically and corrected deliberately. The determination is therefore partial, and the partial is a decision about blast radius rather than a capability gap. Boundary against ACM: that indicator detects drift and reports it, this one enforces, same evidence and different obligation, and the difference is the word automatically. Boundary against SVC-EIS: improvement changes the intended state, enforcement restores it.
- **1. Assessment services** — Config rules and a conformance pack; Security Hub standards; Security Command Center posture checks. *(Both; provisioned by Terraform)*
- **2. Safe remediation list** — Declared list of drift types eligible for automatic correction, with a stated reason each is safe and reversible. *(Repo; provisioned by Version control)*
- **3. Remediation actions** — SSM Automation documents wired to rules on the safe list only. *(AWS; provisioned by Terraform)*
- **4. Health enforcement** — Desired count, target group health checks, alarms on service and scheduled job health. *(AWS; provisioned by Terraform)*
- **5. Meta-assessment** — Collector verifying assessment services are enabled, scoped correctly and producing results. *(Both; provisioned by Terraform)*
- **6. Remediation outcome record** — Every automatic remediation records what drifted, what corrected it, and whether it succeeded. *(Both; provisioned by Terraform)*

### Validation

- **Cycle.** Required: 3 days. Current practice: the collector runs daily from GitHub Actions (.github/workflows/collect.yml), as a read-only identity in each cloud with no stored credential, and this record was emitted by that run. Daily is stricter than every required cycle above (DECISIONS.md, 2026-10-01).
- **CFG** — Assessment services enabled with the expected scope. Source: Config recorder and rules; Security Hub; Security Command Center. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per run. *Row `KSI-CNA-EIS.verify.1`: partly automated by `cna-ibp-cfg-aws-securityhub-standards`, `iam-sus-cfg-aws-guardduty-declared-plans`, `piy-giv-cfg-aws-config-recorder`, `svc-eis-cfg-aws-registry-scanning`. Not covered: Security Hub's standards; Config, GuardDuty and Inspector are judged by their own checks. AWS only: GCP has no Security Command Center without an organization, so its posture is the collector's GCP checks rather than an ingested service. GuardDuty alone. AWS only: GCP has no Security Command Center without an organization, so its posture is the collector's GCP checks rather than an ingested service. The Config recorder alone; recording is scoped to the inventory's 17 types (2026-09-05). AWS only: GCP has no Security Command Center without an organization, so its posture is the collector's GCP checks rather than an ingested service. Inspector's ECR scanning alone. AWS only: GCP has no Security Command Center without an organization, so its posture is the collector's GCP checks rather than an ingested service.*
- **CFG** — Remediation actions attached only to rules on the declared safe list. Source: Config remediation configuration versus the safe list. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per run. *Row `KSI-CNA-EIS.verify.2`: not automated -- gap. No automated remediation is built: drift is detected (drift.yml, daily) and corrected by the operator re-applying declared state under an elevation. Risk: Drift persists until the operator acts on the drift run's failure, typically within a day.*

Not covered: Health check and desired counts; the alarms are judged by mla-osm-cfg-aws-alarms-target-detection.*

Risk: As verify 2.*
- **OPS** — A deliberately introduced safe-list drift is corrected without human action. Source: Test: introduce drift, observe remediation. No automated remediation is built: drift is detected (drift.yml, daily) and corrected by the operator re-applying declared state under an elevation. Every drift is detected and none is auto-corrected, which is the boundary this row tests, held trivially. Risk: As verify 2.*

Required every 3 days; implemented: Per run. *Row `KSI-CNA-EIS.validate.3`: partly automated by `cna-eis-ops-stopped-task-replaced`. Not covered: Run once per environment session and recorded (cadence 30 days), not every 3: the environment it needs stands only during sessions.*

Required every 3 days; implemented: Per run. *Row `KSI-CNA-EIS.validate.4`: automated by `cna-eis-ops-register-assessment`.*

Required every 3 days; implemented: Per run. *Row `KSI-CNA-EIS.validate.5`: not automated -- gap. No automated remediation is built: drift is detected (drift.yml, daily) and corrected by the operator re-applying declared state under an elevation. Risk: No remediation actions run, so none can fail unsurfaced.*
- **Automated evidence.** 3 collector check(s) in the run on 2026-10-26: 1 NOT_STANDING, 2 PASS. Historical metrics (SDR-CSX-KMT) cover 19 day(s) of CI runs since 2026-10-02, in the metrics evidence object below; the year is what exists, not a full year.

### Assessment

- **No independent assessor is engaged.** The statements below are the provider's own assurance reasoning from the determination. They are not an independent assessment and should not be read as one.
- **Automation assurance.** Accuracy is tested by four deliberate tests run in both directions: safe drift corrects, non-safe drift does not, a stopped task returns, and a disabled assessment service is flagged. The second of those is what proves the blast-radius boundary is real rather than an assertion about intent. The meta-assessment is tested by disabling a rule and confirming the collector notices, which is the check that stops the assessment layer failing silently. Sufficiency is bounded twice: by what the assessment services support, and by the deliberately scoped recorder, which is a cost decision with a coverage consequence and should be read as such.
- **Limitations and residual risk.** Automatic enforcement is narrow by choice, so most drift is detected automatically and corrected deliberately; a real provider at Class C might automate more, and the reason this project does not is that unattended apply of a full plan can execute a destructive change unread. Assessment coverage is bounded by what the managed services support and by the deliberately scoped recorder. Health enforcement is a platform guarantee rather than project code, so it is inherited rather than demonstrated, though it is observable in action. The synthetic environment produces drift only when introduced deliberately, so enforcement is exercised rather than observed operating.

### Tests

- Negative control for `cna-eis-ops-register-assessment` (collector/self_test.py): inputs that must fail it: inventoried class without an entry, entry without the dimension, reduced availability without a reason, no backup without a reason, objective half-declared, evaluated less often than the plan, data store naming a missing check, empty statement, empty inventory.
- Negative control for `cna-eis-ops-stopped-task-replaced` (collector/self_test.py): inputs that must fail it: never run, latest failed, errored, too old, another scenario's record.
- Designed, not yet automated: A deliberately introduced safe-list drift is corrected without human action (Test: introduce drift, observe remediation).
- Designed, not yet automated: A deliberately introduced non-safe-list drift is detected and not auto-corrected (Test: confirms the boundary holds in both directions).
- Designed, not yet automated: A stopped task is replaced automatically and desired count restored (Test: stop a task, observe replacement).

## KSI-CNA-IBP — Partially Implemented

### Implementation

- Scope is far wider than the statement first reads, because a third-party information resource is defined as anything not entirely inside the Minimum Assessment Scope. That makes both clouds themselves third-party resources rather than only add-ons, along with the code hosting platform and the container base images, so essentially every managed service here is a third party whose configuration must be compared against its own vendor's guidance. The original provider means the vendor of the resource rather than this offering: Provider is a defined term meaning the cloud service provider seeking certification, and original is the qualifier pointing the other way. The comparison basis differs by vendor and that is where the asymmetry sits. On the AWS side, standards checks evaluate against the CIS Foundations Benchmark and are already included in the posture service. On the GCP side, Security Health Analytics at the free tier produces real misconfiguration findings but the mapping of those findings to CIS controls sits behind the paid tier, so that side compares against the vendor's own detectors without being able to state coverage against a named benchmark. That distinction matters for this indicator specifically, because a benchmark is the structured form of a vendor's guidance: findings tell you what is wrong, a benchmark tells you what fraction of the guidance you meet. It is a licensing boundary rather than a capability gap and is accepted as a cost decision. The code hosting platform has no automated comparison at all, since its hardening guidance is published prose; several of its recommendations are already implemented for other reasons, being SHA-pinned actions from DFP and federated identity from SNU, but that is coincidence rather than comparison and claiming it as satisfaction would be the characteristic failure this indicator invites.
- **1. AWS benchmark comparison** — Security Hub standards enabled with the CIS Foundations Benchmark. Findings ingested. *(AWS; provisioned by Terraform)*
- **2. GCP detector comparison** — Security Health Analytics at the free tier, findings ingested, benchmark mapping declared absent. *(GCP; provisioned by Terraform)*
- **3. Base image currency** — Image scanning against maintainer advisories with rebuild on finding, shared with KSI-SVC-EIS. *(Both; provisioned by Terraform and CI)*
- **4. Third-party register** — Every third-party resource with its comparison basis, and whether that basis is automated or manual. *(Repo; provisioned by Version control)*
- **5. Exception record** — Benchmark controls deliberately not met, each with a reason and a review date. *(Repo; provisioned by Version control)*

### Validation

- **Cycle.** Required: 3 days. Current practice: the collector runs daily from GitHub Actions (.github/workflows/collect.yml), as a read-only identity in each cloud with no stored credential, and this record was emitted by that run. Daily is stricter than every required cycle above (DECISIONS.md, 2026-10-01).
- **CFG** — Standards checks enabled with the expected benchmark. Source: Security Hub standards configuration. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per run. *Row `KSI-CNA-IBP.verify.1`: automated by `cna-ibp-cfg-aws-securityhub-standards`.*
- **CFG** — Security Health Analytics enabled at organization level. Source: Security Command Center settings. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per run. *Row `KSI-CNA-IBP.verify.2`: not automated -- inapplicable. Security Health Analytics is part of Security Command Center, which needs an organization. GCP has no organization in this project (a standalone project under a personal billing account), so organization-level services do not exist here. Risk: GCP's posture is judged by the collector's GCP checks only, which cover what was written, not a vendor's detector set.*
- **CFG** — Third-party register covers every third-party resource in the inventory. Source: Register versus inventory from KSI-PIY-GIV. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per run. *Row `KSI-CNA-IBP.verify.3`: automated by `cna-ibp-ops-third-parties-registered`.*
- **CFG** — Every exception carries a reason and a review date. Source: Repository file, checked in CI. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per run. *Row `KSI-CNA-IBP.verify.4`: automated by `cna-ibp-ops-aws-failing-controls-excepted`.*
- **OPS** — Benchmark pass rate recorded, with every failing control either remediated or in the exception record. Source: Security Hub standards findings. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per run. *Row `KSI-CNA-IBP.validate.1`: automated by `cna-ibp-ops-aws-failing-controls-excepted`.*
- **OPS** — Detector findings reviewed within the interval, zero unreviewed beyond it. Source: Security Command Center findings with review state. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per run. *Row `KSI-CNA-IBP.validate.2`: not automated -- inapplicable. As verify 2: no Security Command Center findings exist to review. Risk: As verify 2.*
- **OPS** — Zero third-party resources in the inventory absent from the register, standing query, zero results expected. Source: Reconciliation against the inventory generated for KSI-PIY-GIV. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per run. *Row `KSI-CNA-IBP.validate.3`: automated by `cna-ibp-ops-third-parties-registered`.*
- **OPS** — Base images current against maintainer advisories. Source: Scan results versus running image digests. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per run. *Row `KSI-CNA-IBP.validate.4`: not automated -- gap. As SVC-EIS validate 5: base images are scanned continuously (svc-eis-cfg-aws-image-scan-coverage), but currency against the maintainer's releases is not compared. Risk: As SVC-EIS validate 5.*
- **Automated evidence.** 3 collector check(s) in the run on 2026-10-26: 3 PASS. Historical metrics (SDR-CSX-KMT) cover 19 day(s) of CI runs since 2026-10-02, in the metrics evidence object below; the year is what exists, not a full year.

### Assessment

- **No independent assessor is engaged.** The statements below are the provider's own assurance reasoning from the determination. They are not an independent assessment and should not be read as one.
- **Automation assurance.** Accuracy is tested by introducing a known benchmark violation and confirming it appears as a failing control, then remediating and confirming it clears. The register is tested by adding a third-party resource without an entry and confirming reconciliation flags it. Sufficiency is bounded by what each vendor publishes in machine-comparable form: one publishes a benchmark and a service that evaluates against it, another publishes detectors at this tier and reserves benchmark mapping for a paid one, and a third publishes prose. The comparison is only as structured as the vendor's guidance, which varies by vendor rather than by our effort.
- **Limitations and residual risk.** The GCP comparison lacks a benchmark mapping, which sits behind a paid tier priced per gigabyte of logs plus resource-hour billing; that side therefore compares against vendor detectors without stating coverage against a named benchmark, which is a licensing boundary accepted as a cost decision rather than a capability gap. The code hosting platform is not compared at all, since its hardening guidance is published prose with no automated comparison service, and the recommendations implemented for other reasons are coincidence rather than comparison. Container base image comparison relies on scanning, which covers vulnerabilities rather than the maintainer's recommended use. The synthetic environment is small, so benchmark pass rates are easy in a way they would not be at scale.

### Tests

- Negative control for `cna-ibp-cfg-aws-securityhub-standards` (collector/self_test.py): configurations that must fail it: one missing, not ready.
- Negative control for `cna-ibp-ops-aws-failing-controls-excepted` (collector/self_test.py): configurations that must fail it: exception past its review date, exception without a review date, unexcepted failure, excepted control, carved-out resource, no findings.
- Negative control for `cna-ibp-ops-third-parties-registered` (collector/self_test.py): inputs that must fail it: an unregistered action owner, same value, other kind, entry without a monitoring status, nothing in use.

## KSI-CNA-OFA — Partially Implemented

### Implementation

- This is the only indicator so far with no control mappings at all, which removes the interpretive anchor that resolved appropriately on RNT, where SC-7(5) supplied a deny-by-default structure. Here appropriateness is genuinely relative and the only available referent is the provider's own circumstances, which is how RPL-RRO defines recovery objectives, against the provider's business needs and capabilities; reading OFA the same way is consistent rather than convenient. The claim is therefore not that this architecture is highly available in an absolute sense but that its availability posture is a deliberate position matched to a two-person pre-certification team, with the cost stated. The compute tier is genuinely highly available: services run multiple tasks across availability zones with desired count maintained automatically, and task replacement takes seconds. The data tier is deliberately not: the database is single-AZ with no automatic failover, recovery is by restore from an automated backup with point-in-time recovery measured in tens of minutes, and that is a cost decision since multi-AZ roughly doubles the instance charge. That asymmetry is the honest shape of the determination. The rapid recovery half is where this project can claim strongly, and the argument is worth making rather than treating the partial as a consolation: a tested restore with a measured time is better evidence than an untested failover, because failover is a configuration property whose behaviour and duration are unknown until it happens, whereas a restore that has been performed and timed is demonstrated capability. That is a claim about evidence quality, not a claim that single-AZ is equivalent. Boundary against RPL: that cluster defines the objectives, checks backups and plans align to them, and tests recovery; OFA is upstream, about whether the resources themselves are configured for availability and recoverability.
- **1. Multi-AZ compute** — Services running at least two tasks across availability zones, desired count maintained. *(AWS; provisioned by Terraform)*
- **2. Automated backups** — Retention window with point-in-time recovery, aligned to the declared recovery point objective. *(AWS; provisioned by Terraform)*
- **3. Availability position register** — Per resource: availability zone posture, recovery mechanism, measured or estimated recovery time, and the reason where the posture is deliberately reduced. *(Repo; provisioned by Version control)*
- **4. Restore measurement** — Periodic restore into a disposable instance, timed, result recorded. The cheapest strong evidence in this cluster. *(AWS; provisioned by Terraform and CI)*
- **5. Stateless pipeline design** — Jobs idempotent and re-runnable rather than requiring recovery. *(GCP; provisioned by Terraform)*

### Validation

- **Cycle.** Required: 3 days. Current practice: the collector runs daily from GitHub Actions (.github/workflows/collect.yml), as a read-only identity in each cloud with no stored credential, and this record was emitted by that run. Daily is stricter than every required cycle above (DECISIONS.md, 2026-10-01).
- **CFG** — Compute services configured for multiple tasks across zones. Source: ECS service configuration and subnet spread. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per run. *Row `KSI-CNA-OFA.verify.1`: partly automated by `cna-ofa-cfg-aws-tasks-spread` (judged only while aws-phase2 stands). Not covered: The worker runs one task, deliberately: it is a single extractor whose landed high-water mark turns a gap during replacement into latency, not lost data (registers/resources.yaml).*
- **CFG** — Automated backups enabled with retention matching the declared objective. Source: RDS backup configuration. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per run. *Row `KSI-CNA-OFA.verify.2`: automated by `svc-sin-cfg-aws-database-settings` (judged only while aws-phase1 stands).*
- **CFG** — Every resource holds an availability position, with a reason where reduced. Source: Register versus inventory from KSI-PIY-GIV. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per run. *Row `KSI-CNA-OFA.verify.3`: automated by `cna-ofa-ops-register-availability`.*
- **CFG** — Restore measurement schedule exists and is enabled. Source: Scheduler configuration. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per run. *Row `KSI-CNA-OFA.verify.4`: not automated -- gap. The restore measurement runs per session by the operator (deliberate/), not on a schedule. Risk: As RPL-TRC verify 2.*
- **OPS** — A stopped task is replaced and desired count restored automatically. Source: Test, shared with KSI-CNA-EIS. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per run. *Row `KSI-CNA-OFA.validate.1`: partly automated by `cna-eis-ops-stopped-task-replaced`. Not covered: Run once per environment session and recorded (cadence 30 days), not every 3: the environment it needs stands only during sessions.*
- **OPS** — Tasks observed distributed across more than one availability zone. Source: Running task placement. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per run. *Row `KSI-CNA-OFA.validate.2`: partly automated by `cna-ofa-cfg-aws-tasks-spread` (judged only while aws-phase2 stands). Not covered: The api service's subnets span two zones and it runs two tasks; where each task was placed is not read.*
- **OPS** — A restore completes into a disposable instance and the elapsed time is recorded. Source: Timed restore test. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per declared cadence. *Row `KSI-CNA-OFA.validate.3`: automated by `cna-ofa-ops-timed-point-in-time-restore`.*
- **OPS** — Measured restore time is within the declared recovery time objective. Source: Restore records versus the objective from KSI-RPL-RRO. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per declared cadence. *Row `KSI-CNA-OFA.validate.4`: automated by `cna-ofa-ops-timed-point-in-time-restore`.*
- **OPS** — Zero resources in the inventory without an availability position, standing query, zero results expected. Source: Reconciliation against the inventory generated for KSI-PIY-GIV. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per run. *Row `KSI-CNA-OFA.validate.5`: automated by `cna-ofa-ops-register-availability`.*
- **Automated evidence.** 3 collector check(s) in the run on 2026-10-26: 1 NOT_STANDING, 2 PASS. Historical metrics (SDR-CSX-KMT) cover 19 day(s) of CI runs since 2026-10-02, in the metrics evidence object below; the year is what exists, not a full year.

### Assessment

- **No independent assessor is engaged.** The statements below are the provider's own assurance reasoning from the determination. They are not an independent assessment and should not be read as one.
- **Automation assurance.** Accuracy is tested by reducing a service to a single task and confirming the collector fails it, and by shortening a backup retention window below the declared objective and confirming failure. The restore measurement is self-validating, since it either completes or it does not and the elapsed time is the evidence. Sufficiency is bounded by what can be tested without disruption: compute failure is tested by stopping a task, which is safe, whereas database failover cannot be tested because there is none, and availability-zone failure cannot be simulated, so the availability half rests on configuration while the recovery half rests on measurement.
- **Limitations and residual risk.** The database is single-AZ with no failover, so an availability-zone failure means an outage lasting as long as a restore and a recovery point bounded by the backup mechanism rather than by synchronous replication. That is a cost decision stated as one and it is why this indicator is partial. Availability-zone failure cannot be simulated, so the availability half rests on configuration rather than demonstration and only the recovery half is measured. The synthetic environment has no real load, so availability under stress is untested and the claim is about structure rather than behaviour under pressure. Managed service availability is inherited from the providers, paralleling the inherited gaps in VRI, EIS and DFP.

### Tests

- Negative control for `cna-ofa-ops-register-availability` (collector/self_test.py): inputs that must fail it: inventoried class without an entry, entry without the dimension, reduced availability without a reason, no backup without a reason, objective half-declared, evaluated less often than the plan, data store naming a missing check, empty statement, empty inventory.
- Negative control for `cna-ofa-ops-timed-point-in-time-restore` (collector/self_test.py): inputs that must fail it: never run, latest failed, errored, too old, another scenario's record.
- Designed, not yet automated: A stopped task is replaced and desired count restored automatically (Test, shared with KSI-CNA-EIS).
- Designed, not yet automated: A restore completes into a disposable instance and the elapsed time is recorded (Timed restore test).

### Evidence

Result: NOT_STANDING — aws-phase2 is not standing (no anchor resource exists); not judged ([definition](https://github.com/elvie-valmores/fedramp-20x-ksi-assessment/blob/97cdfd2eee91745843bbb9d17758668091a06d60/collector/checks/cna-ofa-cfg-aws-tasks-spread.json))

Result: PASS — all 15 inventoried classes carry a recorded availability position ([definition](https://github.com/elvie-valmores/fedramp-20x-ksi-assessment/blob/97cdfd2eee91745843bbb9d17758668091a06d60/collector/checks/cna-ofa-ops-register-availability.json))

## KSI-CNA-RVP — Partially Implemented

### Implementation

- The operative word is effectiveness rather than protection. Every other reading collapses into checking that a protective service is enabled, which is what the assessor guidance specifically warns against: enabled is a configuration fact, effective is a claim about outcomes. The core problem is that a web application firewall with zero blocked requests could mean it is working perfectly or that nothing ever tried, and those are indistinguishable from the data alone. That is the same shape as the self-testing zero-results rule set in ELP and the resolution is the same: generate traffic that should be blocked and confirm it is. Other unwanted activity is narrowed by the mappings rather than read generically, since SI-8 is spam protection and SI-8(2) is automatic signature updates, neither of which is a generic malicious traffic control; read against this architecture the applicable meaning is content-based protection that updates its own rules automatically, which managed rule groups satisfy exactly, with vendor-maintained rule content mapping onto the signature-update half. Traditional spam protection does not apply because the offering neither sends nor accepts mail, and that is declared rather than stretched into something adjacent. Effectiveness is claimed at three levels and only two are demonstrable here: configured, meaning the protections are active and current; exercised, meaning traffic that should be blocked is blocked; and operationally effective, meaning real attacks were stopped, which this environment cannot produce. Shield Standard is honestly weak for this indicator and is named as such rather than padded: it is automatic, free, always on and exposes almost nothing about its own operation, so the denial-of-service claim rests on rate limiting and load balancer metrics, with the premium tier that would supply the visibility costing roughly 3,000 USD per month and therefore not used.
- **1. WAF association** — Web ACL on the load balancer with managed rule groups and rate-based rules. *(AWS; provisioned by Terraform)*
- **2. WAF logging** — Full logging to the central log store, so blocked and allowed decisions are reviewable. *(AWS; provisioned by Terraform)*
- **3. Rate limit declaration** — Threshold declared with a reason and versioned, rather than left at a default. *(Repo; provisioned by Version control)*
- **4. Exercise test** — Requests matching known managed rule patterns and requests exceeding the rate threshold, sent deliberately, outcomes recorded. *(AWS; provisioned by Terraform and CI)*
- **5. Protection position register** — Per mechanism: what it protects against, how effectiveness is evidenced, and whether that evidence is configuration, exercise or operational. *(Repo; provisioned by Version control)*
- **6. Load balancer metrics review** — Request volume, error rates and rejected connection counts, as the denial-of-service signal the standard protection tier does not provide. *(AWS; provisioned by Terraform)*

### Assessment

- **No independent assessor is engaged.** The statements below are the provider's own assurance reasoning from the determination. They are not an independent assessment and should not be read as one.
- **Automation assurance.** Accuracy is tested by the exercise test itself, run in both directions: a request that should be blocked is confirmed blocked, and a legitimate request is confirmed allowed. The second direction matters as much as the first, because a firewall blocking everything would pass a one-sided test while breaking the service. Sufficiency is bounded at the operational level: exercise testing proves the mechanism responds to traffic we generate, not that it is adequate against traffic an attacker would generate, and no amount of testing in this environment closes that gap.
- **Limitations and residual risk.** Operational effectiveness cannot be demonstrated, since the environment receives no real attack traffic; the strongest available claim is that protections respond correctly to traffic generated deliberately. A real provider's evidence here would be blocked attacks, this project's is exercised mechanisms, and the difference is stated rather than blurred. The standard protection tier exposes almost nothing about its own operation, so the denial-of-service claim rests on rate limiting and load balancer metrics; the premium tier that would supply the visibility costs roughly 3,000 USD per month. Spam protection under SI-8 does not apply, since the offering neither sends nor accepts mail. Managed rule group content is vendor-maintained, so what those rules detect is inherited rather than determined here, paralleling the inherited gaps in VRI, EIS, DFP and OFA.

### Tests

- Negative control for `cna-rvp-ops-waf-blocks-and-allows` (collector/self_test.py): inputs that must fail it: never run, latest failed, errored, too old, another scenario's record.

- Designed, not yet automated: Requests exceeding the rate threshold are blocked during the exercise test (Deliberate test; WAF logs).
- Designed, not yet automated: A legitimate request is allowed during the same test (Deliberate test; WAF logs).

## KSI-MLA-LET — Partially Implemented

### Implementation

- The final clause is the whole indicator. Persistently reviewed to ensure these activities occur means the review is of whether the logging described is actually happening, not of whether the list is tidy; a maintained list sitting over a silently failed pipeline satisfies the first half and fails the statement. That decides the evidence design, which needs a per-source check that events are arriving rather than a check that the list exists. The event categories are not ours to choose freely: the mappings name them, with AC-2(4) covering automated account management actions, AC-6(9) privileged function execution, AC-17(1) and AC-20(1) remote and external access, AU-12 audit record generation, and SI-4(4) and SI-4(5) inbound and outbound traffic monitoring and alerting. Logged, monitored and audited are three different things and the statement lists all three: logged means the record is produced, monitored means something watches it and can alert, which is the detection path built for SUS, and audited means it is reviewed, which is RVL. Several sources will be logged and audited but not monitored, so the list records a position per source rather than implying uniformity. Data access logging is scoped to the stores holding customer data rather than all stores, because data events are billed per event and can dwarf management event costs; that is a cost decision with a coverage consequence, stated as one, following the precedent set when the configuration recorder was scoped. Information resources is untyped, so non-machine resources are in scope by the statement and out by this project's scoping decision, making the determination partial for the same reason as GIV.
- **1. Log source enablement** — Control plane, identity, network flow, edge, application, database, data access, elevation and detection sources, all delivering to the central store. *(Both; provisioned by Terraform)*
- **2. Event type list** — Versioned, per source: event types, expected volume interval, and whether the source is logged, monitored, audited or some subset. *(Repo; provisioned by Version control)*
- **3. Delivery confirmation** — Per-source check that events arrived within the expected interval. *(Both; provisioned by Terraform)*
- **4. Data event scoping** — Data access logging scoped to customer-data stores, with the coverage consequence recorded. *(AWS; provisioned by Terraform)*
- **5. Coverage reconciliation** — Inventory compared against the list in both directions. *(Both; provisioned by Terraform)*

### Validation

- **Cycle.** Required: 3 days. Current practice: the collector runs daily from GitHub Actions (.github/workflows/collect.yml), as a read-only identity in each cloud with no stored credential, and this record was emitted by that run. Daily is stricter than every required cycle above (DECISIONS.md, 2026-10-01).
- **CFG** — Every source in the list is enabled and its destination is the central store. Source: Source configuration across both clouds. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per run. *Row `KSI-MLA-LET.verify.1`: not automated -- gap. Nine of the sixteen event sources are outside the central log store, most unmonitored (registers/event-sources.yaml, recorded 2026-10-03). Every source is enabled; the gap is the destination. Risk: Events from those sources -- CloudWatch application and database logs, GCP's Cloud Audit Logs, GitHub -- are not in the corpus, so no standing query sees them.*
- **CFG** — The list covers every event category named in the mappings. Source: List versus mapped control categories. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per run. *Row `KSI-MLA-LET.verify.2`: not automated -- gap. The event type list (registers/event-sources.yaml) is organised by source, not reconciled against the mapped control categories one by one. Risk: A mapped category with no source would not be flagged.*
- **CFG** — Every source carries a logged, monitored and audited position. Source: Repository file, checked in CI. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per run. *Row `KSI-MLA-LET.verify.3`: automated by `mla-let-cfg-event-sources-positioned`.*
- **CFG** — Data event scoping matches the declared customer-data stores. Source: CloudTrail data event selectors versus the declared list. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per run. *Row `KSI-MLA-LET.verify.4`: automated by `mla-let-cfg-aws-trail-data-events-scoped`.*
- **OPS** — Every listed source has delivered events within its expected interval, zero silent sources. Source: Central store partition timestamps per source. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per run. *Row `KSI-MLA-LET.validate.1`: partly automated by `mla-let-ops-aws-source-not-silent`. Not covered: The AWS source only. GCP's audit and data-access logs deliver to their own log bucket, outside the corpus, so their silence is not judged here.*
- **OPS** — A deliberately generated event of each mapped category appears in the store. Source: Test event per category. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per run. *Row `KSI-MLA-LET.validate.2`: not automated -- gap. No test event per category is generated. The deliberate tests produce events in several categories (IAM changes by elevation, KMS use, failed authorization), and the standing queries read them, but no test checks each category's arrival. Risk: A category whose events stopped arriving would be noticed only if a query on it failed.*
- **OPS** — Zero resources in the inventory producing no logs without a recorded reason, standing query, zero results expected. Source: Reconciliation against the inventory generated for KSI-PIY-GIV. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per run. *Row `KSI-MLA-LET.validate.3`: automated by `mla-let-ops-register-logging`.*
- **OPS** — Zero listed sources configured but not delivering, standing query, zero results expected. Source: Configuration compared against arrival. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per run. *Row `KSI-MLA-LET.validate.4`: partly automated by `mla-let-ops-aws-source-not-silent`. Not covered: The AWS source only, for the same reason.*
- **Automated evidence.** 4 collector check(s) in the run on 2026-10-26: 4 PASS. Historical metrics (SDR-CSX-KMT) cover 19 day(s) of CI runs since 2026-10-02, in the metrics evidence object below; the year is what exists, not a full year.

### Assessment

- **No independent assessor is engaged.** The statements below are the provider's own assurance reasoning from the determination. They are not an independent assessment and should not be read as one.
- **Automation assurance.** Accuracy is tested by disabling a log source and confirming the delivery check fails, then re-enabling it. Generating an event of each mapped category and confirming it lands proves the path end to end, where checking the source is enabled proves only the first link. The silent-source check is tested by shortening an expected interval deliberately and confirming it fires. Sufficiency is bounded by what each source records: a source delivering events proves the pipe works, not that the source captures everything it should, which depends on the provider's own logging completeness and is inherited.
- **Limitations and residual risk.** Non-machine information resources are out of scope, so the list covers machine-based resources only and the determination is partial for the same reason as GIV. Data access logging is scoped to customer-data stores for cost, so access to other stores is not individually logged, which is a cost decision with a coverage consequence. Source completeness is inherited: a provider that does not log a particular action leaves a gap nothing here detects. The synthetic environment produces low and irregular event volume, so expected-interval checks are weak, and a source legitimately producing nothing for days is hard to distinguish from a broken one, which is the same shape as the not-observed state in APM and would be much sharper in a real environment.

### Tests

- Negative control for `mla-let-cfg-aws-trail-data-events-scoped` (collector/self_test.py): configurations that must fail it: none, undeclared extra, wrong store.
- Negative control for `mla-let-cfg-event-sources-positioned` (collector/self_test.py): inputs that must fail it: monitoring unstated, no destination, empty list.
- Negative control for `mla-let-ops-aws-source-not-silent` (collector/self_test.py): inputs that must fail it: no rows, query failed, query failed, zero expected, rows where none expected, unknown expectation.
- Negative control for `mla-let-ops-register-logging` (collector/self_test.py): inputs that must fail it: inventoried class without an entry, entry without the dimension, reduced availability without a reason, no backup without a reason, objective half-declared, evaluated less often than the plan, data store naming a missing check, empty statement, empty inventory.
- Designed, not yet automated: A deliberately generated event of each mapped category appears in the store (Test event per category).

## KSI-MLA-OSM — Partially Implemented

### Implementation

- Or similar system(s) is plural and the determination rests on that. This architecture has no SIEM and does not claim one; it has a composition, and the plural reads as anticipating exactly that. Continuous detection and alerting come from the managed threat services, centralized tamper-resistant retention from the object-locked store with digest validation, investigation from a serverless query engine over partitioned Parquet, cross-source correlation from schema normalization plus scheduled queries, and response from the responder built for SUS. Calling any single component a SIEM would be wrong; calling the composition similar systems is accurate. Correlation is the part that would otherwise be missing, and it is closed by normalizing both clouds' events to the Open Cybersecurity Schema Framework in the delivery pipeline: a user in one cloud is an account in the other, and a block is a deny or a drop depending on the vendor, so one schema means a single query correlates across both natively. That is this project's original multi-cloud normalization thesis in a harder form, now with a vendor-neutral standard behind it. Four mapped controls impose obligations the statement gives no hint of. AU-4 storage capacity becomes cost and growth monitoring rather than disk space, since the store has no capacity limit. AU-5 response to audit processing failure is the significant one, because delivery failure previously had no defined response and silently losing logs is worse than most findings this project detects. AU-8 time stamps becomes a concrete field-mapping requirement, since correlation across clouds is meaningless if time representations differ. AU-11 retention is a separate declaration from immutability. Tamper-resistance is two properties rather than one, following SIN: object lock prevents deletion and digest validation detects alteration, and neither alone is tamper-resistance.
- **1. Central store** — Object lock in compliance mode with retention measured in days, not months, and log file digest validation enabled. *(AWS; provisioned by Terraform)*
- **2. Schema normalization** — Lambda mapping both clouds' events to OCSF classes before they land, critical event classes first, which is the documented adoption norm. *(Both; provisioned by Terraform)*
- **3. Parquet conversion and partitioning** — Partitioned by source and date with partition projection rather than a crawler, which avoids crawler charges entirely. *(AWS; provisioned by Terraform)*
- **4. Query layer** — Athena workgroup with per-query scan limits and a Glue catalog database. Scan limits set before rather than after a billing surprise. *(AWS; provisioned by Terraform)*
- **5. Detection queries** — Scheduled queries fired by EventBridge, results routed into the detection path, versioned as code. *(AWS; provisioned by Terraform)*
- **6. Delivery failure response** — Alarm on delivery failure and on store growth, both routed to the detection path. This is the AU-5 obligation. *(Both; provisioned by Terraform)*
- **7. Retention declaration** — Declared period per source class, enforced by object lock and lifecycle policy. *(Repo and AWS; provisioned by Terraform)*

### Validation

- **Cycle.** Required: 3 days. Current practice: the collector runs daily from GitHub Actions (.github/workflows/collect.yml), as a read-only identity in each cloud with no stored credential, and this record was emitted by that run. Daily is stricter than every required cycle above (DECISIONS.md, 2026-10-01).
- **CFG** — Object lock enabled with retention matching the declaration, and digest validation enabled. Source: S3 object lock configuration; CloudTrail settings. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per run. *Row `KSI-MLA-OSM.verify.1`: automated by `svc-sin-cfg-aws-log-store-object-lock`, `svc-sin-cfg-aws-trail-log-validation`.*
- **CFG** — Every source listed under KSI-MLA-LET delivers to the central store. Source: Source destinations versus the event type list from KSI-MLA-LET. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per run. *Row `KSI-MLA-OSM.verify.2`: not automated -- gap. Nine of the sixteen event sources are outside the central log store, most unmonitored (registers/event-sources.yaml, recorded 2026-10-03). Risk: As MLA-LET verify 1.*
- **CFG** — Partition projection configured and workgroup scan limits set. Source: Glue table properties; Athena workgroup configuration. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per run. *Row `KSI-MLA-OSM.verify.3`: automated by `mla-osm-cfg-aws-corpus-query-limits`.*
- **CFG** — Delivery failure and store growth alarms exist and target the detection path. Source: CloudWatch alarm configuration. ### Assessment

- **No independent assessor is engaged.** The statements below are the provider's own assurance reasoning from the determination. They are not an independent assessment and should not be read as one.
- **Automation assurance.** Accuracy is tested by disabling a delivery path and confirming both the failure alarm and the source-silence check from KSI-MLA-LET fire. Normalization is tested by feeding a known event from each cloud and confirming both produce valid records with matching field semantics. The scan limit is tested by issuing a query designed to exceed it and confirming rejection. Sufficiency is bounded by normalization coverage: event classes not yet mapped are stored raw and remain queryable but are not correlatable, so the correlation claim is scoped to mapped classes rather than to all events.
- **Limitations and residual risk.** There is no continuous correlation engine. Scheduled queries have a gap between runs and only look for what they were written to find, where a real SIEM's central value is continuous correlation across all sources; this architecture has periodic correlation across mapped sources, which is the honest shape of the gap and the sole reason this determination is partial. Schema coverage is partial by design, since mapping critical classes first is the documented norm, so correlation covers a subset until all classes are mapped. Detection query quality is ours: a SIEM ships vendor-maintained detection content, whereas here coverage reflects what we thought to look for. Retention is bounded by compliance-mode object lock, which cannot be shortened, so an over-long retention declaration is not correctable.

### Tests

- Negative control for `mla-osm-cfg-aws-alarms-target-detection` (collector/self_test.py): configurations that must fail it: missing, actions disabled, other target.
- Negative control for `mla-osm-cfg-aws-corpus-query-limits` (collector/self_test.py): configurations that must fail it: projection off, projection unset, not enforced, no limit, limit too high, unlimited second workgroup, no tables.

- Negative control for `mla-osm-ops-aws-store-sources-catalogued` (collector/self_test.py): configurations that must fail it: stored, not catalogued, empty store.
- Negative control for `mla-osm-ops-aws-timestamps-consistent` (collector/self_test.py): inputs that must fail it: no rows, query failed, query failed, zero expected, rows where none expected, unknown expectation.
- Negative control for `mla-osm-ops-delivery-failure-alerts` (collector/self_test.py): inputs that must fail it: never run, latest failed, errored, too old, another scenario's record.
- Negative control for `mla-osm-ops-normalized-events-queryable` (collector/self_test.py): inputs that must fail it: no rows, query failed, query failed, zero expected, rows where none expected, unknown expectation.
- Designed, not yet automated: A write against a locked object is rejected (Deliberate test).
- Designed, not yet automated: A deliberately induced delivery failure produces an alert (Test: disable a delivery path).

## KSI-MLA-RVL — Partially Implemented

### Implementation

- Six words, and the mappings carry almost all the meaning, which makes this the clearest instance of a pattern this project has hit repeatedly. AU-6 is audit record review, analysis and reporting for indications of inappropriate activity; AU-6(1) is automated process integration; SI-4 and SI-4(4) are system and traffic monitoring; AC-2(4) and AC-6(9) name account management actions and privileged function execution as things specifically to review. So reviewed and audited resolves to automated analysis of log records for indications of inappropriate activity, with particular attention to account changes, privileged actions and traffic. Reviewed and monitored are not the same thing and LET already drew that line: monitored means something watches continuously and can alert, which is the detection path, whereas audited means examined for indications of inappropriate activity, which is slower and is what this indicator covers. AU-6(1) is the operative requirement, so review is automated analysis whose output goes somewhere rather than a person reading logs on a schedule. That produces one design consequence worth stating: a detection query fires on a match and is silent otherwise, but a review query must produce a record even when it finds nothing, because persistently reviewed is a claim about the activity occurring rather than about what it found, and without a nil-result record a review that never ran and one that found nothing are indistinguishable. Scope follows the split-by-subject rule set on PRR: logs are machine-based information resources and the automated examination is in scope, while the human half of AU-6, someone reading output and forming a judgment that activity is inappropriate rather than merely unusual, is non-machine and declared out.
- **1. Review queries** — One per mapped category, versioned as SQL against the normalized corpus: account lifecycle events, privileged function execution cross-referenced against grant windows, traffic anomalies, authentication failures, denied access attempts, and log integrity and coverage. *(AWS; provisioned by Terraform)*
- **2. Review scheduling** — EventBridge rules firing each query on its declared interval, with the examined window overlapping the interval so consecutive runs leave no gap. *(AWS; provisioned by Terraform)*
- **3. Review record** — Every run writes query, window, records examined, and findings, including nil results. *(AWS; provisioned by Terraform)*
- **4. Finding disposition** — Every finding carries a disposition: benign, remediated, or escalated to the detection path. *(Repo and AWS; provisioned by Terraform)*
- **5. Coverage reconciliation** — Every source marked audited under KSI-MLA-LET maps to at least one review query. *(Repo; provisioned by Version control)*

### Validation

- **Cycle.** Required: 3 days. Current practice: the collector runs daily from GitHub Actions (.github/workflows/collect.yml), as a read-only identity in each cloud with no stored credential, and this record was emitted by that run. Daily is stricter than every required cycle above (DECISIONS.md, 2026-10-01).
- **CFG** — Every mapped category has a versioned review query. Source: Repository versus mapped control categories. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per run. *Row `KSI-MLA-RVL.verify.1`: partly automated by `mla-rvl-ops-audited-sources-reviewed`. Not covered: Reviewed per audited source, each naming its versioned standing queries; not reconciled against the mapped control categories one by one, and the sources outside the central store have no query (registers/event-sources.yaml).*
- **CFG** — Every audited source under LET maps to a review query. Source: Event type list versus query coverage. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per run. *Row `KSI-MLA-RVL.verify.2`: automated by `mla-rvl-ops-audited-sources-reviewed`.*

Not covered: The schedule is checked; that each query's window is three days is in its SQL, not compared with the schedule here. mla-rvl-ops-review-windows-continuous shows the overlap held in practice.*

### Assessment

- **No independent assessor is engaged.** The statements below are the provider's own assurance reasoning from the determination. They are not an independent assessment and should not be read as one.
- **Automation assurance.** Accuracy is tested by generating one inappropriate action per mapped category and confirming the corresponding query surfaces it, then confirming a clean period produces a nil-result record rather than no record. A query that has never returned a row is an assumption. Sufficiency is bounded by what the queries look for: review finds patterns we wrote queries for, which is the same limit declared in OSM for detection content and is the honest ceiling on any query-based review.
- **Limitations and residual risk.** Review finds what it was written to find. There is no anomaly detection, no baselining and no vendor-supplied review content, so coverage reflects our queries. Review is periodic rather than continuous, so there is a window between runs in which inappropriate activity is logged but unreviewed; the detection services partly cover that in real time, but not for categories visible only in the normalized corpus. The human half of AU-6, reading output and judging activity inappropriate rather than merely unusual, is non-machine and out of scope project-wide, which is the reason this determination is partial. The synthetic environment generates little activity, so most runs produce nil results and the queries are exercised mainly by deliberate test actions.

## KSI-MLA-ALA — Implemented

### Implementation

- The least-privileged, role-based and just-in-time assertions are word for word what ELP and JIT already built, applied to one data type, so they are referenced rather than rebuilt; what ALA contributes is the subject and a sensitivity basis. Organizationally defined data sensitivity is the genuinely new part and it required a judgment about where the project's own line sits: a classification is built rather than declared absent, because classifying log sources classifies artifacts that genuinely exist and whose contents we designed, which is design input in the same way the role model from KSI-IAM-ELP was, where inventing a personnel register would have been fabrication about people who do not exist. SI-11, the single mapping, is error handling, and it says what the classification is for: error detail lands in logs and reveals internals, so the sensitivity basis is a question about what each source can disclose rather than an abstract taxonomy. Three tiers follow. Sensitive covers application logs, database logs and data access events, which may carry customer data fragments, query content and error detail. Operational covers control plane, identity, network flow, edge and elevation records, which reveal structure and activity rather than content. Public-safe covers aggregate metrics and compliance evidence. The sharp claim is that nobody holds standing access to the sensitive tier: reading an application or database log requires an elevation with a justification, which is SI-11 enforced as access control. Write-time redaction was considered as the apparently stronger alternative and rejected, because stripping identifiers destroys evidence the review queries under RVL depend on, and a denied access attempt with the principal removed is much less useful. Enforcement sits at the catalog with tier-partitioned databases, since a permission model can be correct on every individual table and still leak through a join, and direct object access to the store is closed by bucket policy so that the query layer is the only path rather than merely the controlled one.
- **1. Sensitivity classification** — Every source in the event type list tagged to a tier with a stated reason, versioned. *(Repo; provisioned by Version control)*
- **2. Tier-partitioned catalog** — Separate catalog databases per tier so access is grantable per tier rather than per table. *(AWS; provisioned by Terraform)*
- **3. Standing grants** — Operational and public-safe tiers per the lane model. No standing grant to the sensitive tier for any identity. *(AWS; provisioned by Terraform)*
- **4. Elevation for sensitive access** — Entitlement and elevation workflow extended to log access, justification required, bounded duration. *(Both; provisioned by Terraform)*
- **5. Query-level enforcement** — Workgroup and catalog permissions per tier, so a cross-tier join fails without elevation. *(AWS; provisioned by Terraform)*
- **6. Direct access closure** — Bucket policy denying object reads to any principal other than the query engine role, so the catalog is the only path to the data. *(AWS; provisioned by Terraform)*
- **7. Access review queries** — Who read which tier, when, and under which grant. *(AWS; provisioned by Terraform)*

### Validation

- **Cycle.** Required: 3 days. Current practice: the collector runs daily from GitHub Actions (.github/workflows/collect.yml), as a read-only identity in each cloud with no stored credential, and this record was emitted by that run. Daily is stricter than every required cycle above (DECISIONS.md, 2026-10-01).
- **CFG** — Every source in the event type list carries a tier with a reason. Source: Repository file, checked in CI. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per run. *Row `KSI-MLA-ALA.verify.1`: automated by `mla-ala-cfg-event-sources-tiered`.*
- **CFG** — Catalog is partitioned by tier and permissions are granted per tier. Source: Glue database and permission configuration. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per run. *Row `KSI-MLA-ALA.verify.2`: not automated -- gap. The tiered log access model -- sensitive and operational tiers, granted per tier, with elevation for the sensitive one -- is not built (recorded 2026-10-02 and 2026-10-03). Log reads are confined to declared readers by the log store's bucket policy and the evidence key, one tier for all. Risk: No catalog partitioning by tier exists.*
- **CFG** — No identity holds a standing grant to the sensitive tier. Source: Catalog permissions versus the lane model. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per run. *Row `KSI-MLA-ALA.verify.3`: not automated -- gap. The tiered log access model -- sensitive and operational tiers, granted per tier, with elevation for the sensitive one -- is not built (recorded 2026-10-02 and 2026-10-03). Log reads are confined to declared readers by the log store's bucket policy and the evidence key, one tier for all. Risk: Every declared reader holds standing access to all logs, sensitive or not.*
- **CFG** — Elevation entitlements exist for sensitive log access with justification required. Source: Entitlement configuration. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per run. *Row `KSI-MLA-ALA.verify.4`: not automated -- gap. The tiered log access model -- sensitive and operational tiers, granted per tier, with elevation for the sensitive one -- is not built (recorded 2026-10-02 and 2026-10-03). Log reads are confined to declared readers by the log store's bucket policy and the evidence key, one tier for all. Risk: No elevation entitlement exists for log access; the operator's read-only role reads logs standing. Account elevation (JIT) is separate.*
- **CFG** — Bucket policy denies object reads to all principals except the query engine role. Source: S3 bucket policy. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per run. *Row `KSI-MLA-ALA.verify.5`: partly automated by `mla-ala-cfg-aws-log-store-reads-restricted`. Not covered: The row names the query engine role alone; the normalizer, the collector and the operator also read, by need, and are the same readers the evidence key admits. The tiered lane model the row belongs to is not built.*
- **OPS** — Zero accesses to sensitive-tier data without an active grant, standing query, zero results expected. Source: Query history correlated with grant windows. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per run. *Row `KSI-MLA-ALA.validate.1`: not automated -- gap. The tiered log access model -- sensitive and operational tiers, granted per tier, with elevation for the sensitive one -- is not built (recorded 2026-10-02 and 2026-10-03). Log reads are confined to declared readers by the log store's bucket policy and the evidence key, one tier for all. Risk: There is no sensitive tier to account accesses against.*
- **OPS** — A query joining sensitive and operational tiers fails without elevation. Source: Deliberate test, run separately from single-table tests. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per run. *Row `KSI-MLA-ALA.validate.2`: not automated -- gap. The tiered log access model -- sensitive and operational tiers, granted per tier, with elevation for the sensitive one -- is not built (recorded 2026-10-02 and 2026-10-03). Log reads are confined to declared readers by the log store's bucket policy and the evidence key, one tier for all. Risk: There are no tiers to join across.*
- **OPS** — A direct object read by a non-query principal is denied. Source: Deliberate test. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per run. *Row `KSI-MLA-ALA.validate.3`: partly automated by `mla-ala-cfg-aws-log-store-read-denied`. Not covered: By simulation, not a live read: the drift role cannot be assumed outside GitHub Actions. The simulation includes the bucket policy, so the deny it shows is the one a live read would meet.*
- **OPS** — Every elevation for log access carries a justification. Source: Grant records. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per run. *Row `KSI-MLA-ALA.validate.4`: not automated -- gap. The tiered log access model -- sensitive and operational tiers, granted per tier, with elevation for the sensitive one -- is not built (recorded 2026-10-02 and 2026-10-03). Log reads are confined to declared readers by the log store's bucket policy and the evidence key, one tier for all. Risk: No log-access elevations exist to carry justifications.*
- **OPS** — Zero sources in the event type list from KSI-MLA-LET without a tier, standing query, zero results expected. Source: Reconciliation against the event type list. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per run. *Row `KSI-MLA-ALA.validate.5`: automated by `mla-ala-cfg-event-sources-tiered`.*
- **Automated evidence.** 3 collector check(s) in the run on 2026-10-26: 3 PASS. Historical metrics (SDR-CSX-KMT) cover 19 day(s) of CI runs since 2026-10-02, in the metrics evidence object below; the year is what exists, not a full year.

### Assessment

- **No independent assessor is engaged.** The statements below are the provider's own assurance reasoning from the determination. They are not an independent assessment and should not be read as one.
- **Automation assurance.** Accuracy is tested by attempting a sensitive-tier query without elevation and confirming denial, then with elevation and confirming success. The cross-tier join test runs separately from single-table tests, because a permission model can allow single-table access correctly and still permit a join that leaks, and that is the characteristic bypass of a query-layer access model. The direct-read test confirms the bucket policy holds, since catalog permissions mean nothing if the objects are readable around them. Sufficiency is bounded by query history fidelity: the engine records the query and the principal, so access is attributable, and the direct path is closed by policy rather than observed.
- **Limitations and residual risk.** The classification is this project's own. Three tiers over ten source types is a judgment, and a different organization would draw the lines differently, particularly on whether network flow logs are operational or sensitive given they reveal communication patterns. Redaction at write time was rejected in favour of access control, so sensitive content exists in the store and is protected rather than absent, which is a deliberate trade for evidence quality and means a store compromise exposes more than a redacted store would. The synthetic environment has few identities and little log content, so tier enforcement is easy here and the interesting failures, such as a support engineer needing one sensitive record during an incident, never arise.

### Tests

- Negative control for `mla-ala-cfg-aws-log-store-read-denied` (collector/self_test.py): configurations that must fail it: the bucket allows it, the identity could not anyway.
- Negative control for `mla-ala-cfg-aws-log-store-reads-restricted` (collector/self_test.py): configurations that must fail it: no policy, only the TLS deny, undeclared reader exempted, declared reader locked out, old versions readable, another bucket, allow, not deny.
- Negative control for `mla-ala-cfg-event-sources-tiered` (collector/self_test.py): inputs that must fail it: unknown tier, tier without reason.
- Designed, not yet automated: A query joining sensitive and operational tiers fails without elevation (Deliberate test, run separately from single-table tests).
- Designed, not yet automated: A direct object read by a non-query principal is denied (Deliberate test).

### Evidence
