## KSI-MLA-EVC — Implemented

### Implementation

- The mappings and the statement point in different directions and the determination takes both halves. CA-7 continuous monitoring, CM-2 baseline configuration, CM-6 configuration settings with deviation identification, and SI-7(7) integration of change detection into incident response all describe live monitoring, which ACM and CNA-EIS already cover and which is referenced here rather than rebuilt; SI-7(7) in particular is already satisfied, since the mutation query under ACM routes to the detection path. Only the statement's own words, tested and especially infrastructure as code, point at evaluation before application, and that is this indicator's new contribution. The distinction that makes it worth building separately: ACM tells you the environment drifted from what you declared, whereas EVC tells you what you declared is wrong before it is applied. A misconfiguration that is correctly declared and correctly applied is invisible to drift detection, because live state matches the declaration perfectly, and only evaluation of the declaration itself catches it. Plan output rather than source is the evaluation target, which is a real technical point rather than a preference: static analysis of source misses anything resolved at plan time, meaning variables, data sources, module outputs and computed values, and the tooling documentation is explicit that plan evaluation provides additional dependencies and context producing a more complete result. Two tools doing different jobs: a rules-shipping scanner supplies thousands of maintained checks at near-zero setup cost, and a policy engine evaluated against the plan supplies the rules that encode this project's own determinations, since it ships with none. Boundary against CMT-VTD, which is genuinely close: EVC tests the configuration artifact against policy, VTD tests the change through the deployment lifecycle, and that split is a reading rather than something the catalog states. Especially infrastructure as code is a weighting rather than a scope limit, though container and workflow artifacts are evaluated under SVC-EIS and CNA-DFP rather than duplicated here.
- **1. Baseline scanner** — Rules-shipping scanner run against plan JSON for breadth. Near-zero setup, thousands of maintained checks, unmapped by design. *(Repo; provisioned by CI)*
- **2. Project policy set** — Ten to fifteen Rego rules encoding this project's determinations: no public access, no wildcard policies, encryption with customer-managed keys, no managed or primitive policies, digest-pinned images, no standing sensitive log access. *(Repo; provisioned by Version control)*
- **3. Plan evaluation** — Plan written to binary, rendered to JSON, evaluated by both tools. Source scanning is explicitly not the target. *(Repo; provisioned by CI)*
- **4. Merge gate** — Failure at or above declared severity blocks the merge rather than annotating it. *(Repo; provisioned by Version control)*
- **5. Exception register** — Policy exceptions with a reason and an expiry. Never a weakened rule. *(Repo; provisioned by Version control)*
- **6. Coverage mapping** — Each authored Rego rule mapped to the determination it enforces. The scanner baseline is unmapped breadth and is stated as such. *(Repo; provisioned by Version control)*
- **7. Action pinning** — All scanning actions pinned to commit SHAs rather than mutable tags, per KSI-CNA-DFP. A scanning action in this category was compromised in a March 2026 supply chain incident. *(Repo; provisioned by Version control)*

### Validation

- **Cycle.** Required: 3 days. Current practice: the collector runs daily from GitHub Actions (.github/workflows/collect.yml), as a read-only identity in each cloud with no stored credential, and this record was emitted by that run. Daily is stricter than every required cycle above (DECISIONS.md, 2026-10-01).
- **CFG** — Policy set versioned with a coverage mapping for every authored rule. Source: Repository file, checked in CI. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per run. *Row `KSI-MLA-EVC.verify.1`: automated by `mla-evc-cfg-policy-coverage`.*
- **CFG** — Merge gate configured to block rather than annotate. Source: Branch protection and workflow configuration. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per run. *Row `KSI-MLA-EVC.verify.2`: not automated -- inapplicable. The project pushes to main rather than merging pull requests, so there is no merge for a gate to block. The policy workflow fails a push's run on a blocking finding, which is recorded and counted (mla-evc-ops-no-failed-push). Risk: A non-compliant declaration can land on main; it is caught after landing and before it is applied, not before it lands.*
- **CFG** — Evaluation runs against plan JSON, not source. Source: Workflow definition. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per run. *Row `KSI-MLA-EVC.verify.3`: automated by `mla-evc-cfg-evaluates-plan-json`.*
- **CFG** — Every exception carries a reason and an expiry. Source: Exception register, checked in CI. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per run. *Row `KSI-MLA-EVC.verify.4`: automated by `mla-evc-cfg-policy-exceptions-bounded`.*
- **CFG** — Every scanning action is pinned to a commit SHA. Source: Workflow definition. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per run. *Row `KSI-MLA-EVC.verify.5`: automated by `mla-evc-cfg-actions-pinned-policy`.*
- **OPS** — A deliberately non-compliant plan is blocked at the gate. Source: Test pull request. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per change. *Row `KSI-MLA-EVC.validate.1`: partly automated by `mla-evc-ops-deliberate-test-passed`. Not covered: A fixture plan stands in for a test pull request: the project pushes to main and has no pull requests to test with.*
- **OPS** — A compliant plan passes. Source: Same test, opposite direction. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per change. *Row `KSI-MLA-EVC.validate.2`: partly automated by `mla-evc-ops-deliberate-test-passed`. Not covered: The same fixture, the compliant direction; not a pull request.*
- **OPS** — A misconfiguration introduced through a variable rather than a resource block is caught. Source: Test designed to distinguish plan evaluation from source scanning. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per change. *Row `KSI-MLA-EVC.validate.3`: automated by `mla-evc-ops-deliberate-test-passed`.*
- **OPS** — Zero merges to the protected branch without a policy evaluation, standing query, zero results expected. Source: Merge history versus evaluation records. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per run. *Row `KSI-MLA-EVC.validate.4`: partly automated by `mla-evc-ops-every-push-evaluated`. Not covered: The project pushes to main rather than merging pull requests, so each push is evaluated once it lands, not before: a failing evaluation is a finding to fix or except before the change is applied, not a blocked merge.*
- **OPS** — Zero policy failures above declared severity merged, standing query, zero results expected. Source: Evaluation results versus merge history. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per run. *Row `KSI-MLA-EVC.validate.5`: partly automated by `mla-evc-ops-no-failed-push`. Not covered: The project pushes to main rather than merging pull requests, so each push is evaluated once it lands, not before: a failing evaluation is a finding to fix or except before the change is applied, not a blocked merge.*
- **Automated evidence.** 7 collector check(s) in the run on 2026-10-26: 7 PASS. Historical metrics (SDR-CSX-KMT) cover 19 day(s) of CI runs since 2026-10-02, in the metrics evidence object below; the year is what exists, not a full year.

### Assessment

- **No independent assessor is engaged.** The statements below are the provider's own assurance reasoning from the determination. They are not an independent assessment and should not be read as one.
- **Automation assurance.** Accuracy is tested in both directions, and specifically with a misconfiguration introduced through a variable rather than a resource block, which is the case that distinguishes plan evaluation from source scanning: a source scanner passes that test and a plan evaluator fails it. Sufficiency is bounded by policy coverage, since the gate enforces rules that were written; a misconfiguration nobody encoded passes cleanly. The coverage mapping bounds this by making visible which determinations have pre-application enforcement and which rely only on post-application detection.
- **Limitations and residual risk.** Policy coverage is this project's own, so the gate catches what was encoded; the coverage mapping makes the gap visible rather than closing it, and the scanner baseline is deliberately unmapped breadth. Plan evaluation sees what will be created, not how the provider will behave with it, which remains ACM and CNA-EIS territory. The overlap with CMT-VTD is real: both use the same pipeline, and the split between evaluating the artifact and validating the deployment is a reading rather than a statement of the catalog. The synthetic environment has few changes, so the gate is exercised deliberately rather than under real development pressure, where the interesting failure is someone bypassing it against a deadline.

- Negative control for `mla-evc-ops-no-failed-push` (collector/self_test.py): configurations that must fail it: a failed push, cancelled, nothing completed.

- Designed, not yet automated: A compliant plan passes (Same test, opposite direction).
- Designed, not yet automated: A misconfiguration introduced through a variable rather than a resource block is caught (Test designed to distinguish plan evaluation from source scanning).

## KSI-CMT-RMV — Implemented

### Implementation

- This is the indicator the architecture was partly chosen for, which is worth stating rather than presenting as coincidence: container compute was selected over instances partly because containers are replaced rather than patched, declared infrastructure is the change mechanism, and ACM already bars console changes and detects them as drift. What this determination adds is a boundary on wherever reasonable, which left unbounded swallows the indicator, since anything can be called unreasonable to redeploy under pressure. Redeployment is the default and the exception is bounded to two categories, each recorded per instance rather than invoked. First, resources that cannot be redeployed without data loss, which is the database: replacing the instance destroys its data, so schema and parameter changes are applied in place. That is narrower than it sounds, because the instance can be replaced through snapshot and restore, and SIN already requires exactly that for encryption remediation, so the exception rests on disproportionality rather than impossibility. Second, emergency response actions, where the responder attaches a deny policy directly because redeploying during an active incident is slower than the attack. Anything else is a finding rather than an exception. Version controlled is doing work the statement understates: it is not enough that a resource is redeployed, the thing redeployed must come from version control, so a container rebuilt from an untracked file and pushed by hand satisfies redeployment and fails the statement. That makes provenance part of the claim and connects to VRI's digest pinning and DFP's action pinning, which established that the deployed artifact is cryptographically what it claims; RMV adds that it came from the declared source. SI-3 appears again as it did in DFP and the same reading applies: a resource replaced rather than modified has no persistent state for injected code to survive in.
- **1. Redeployment-only compute** — Task definitions replaced rather than edited in place. No interactive session into a running container for the purpose of changing it. *(AWS; provisioned by Terraform)*
- **2. Pipeline-only push** — Registry push permitted only to the pipeline principal. *(Both; provisioned by Terraform)*
- **3. Pipeline-only apply** — Declared-state apply permitted only to the pipeline principal, enforced by role rather than by convention. Currently held open by a dated entry in the exception register: a human identity retains apply because the elevation path is KSI-IAM-JIT's and the dependency is circular -- the pipeline deploys JIT. The exception carries a named closing condition rather than being undated. *(Both; provisioned by Terraform)*
- **4. Provenance record** — Every image digest traced to the commit that built it. *(Repo and registry; provisioned by CI)*
- **5. Exception register** — The two declared categories, with per-instance entries carrying a reason. *(Repo; provisioned by Version control)*
- **6. Post-incident reconciliation** — Emergency modifications reconciled into declared state, with the reconciliation recorded. *(Repo; provisioned by Version control)*

### Validation

- **Cycle.** Required: 3 days. Current practice: the collector runs daily from GitHub Actions (.github/workflows/collect.yml), as a read-only identity in each cloud with no stored credential, and this record was emitted by that run. Daily is stricter than every required cycle above (DECISIONS.md, 2026-10-01).
- **CFG** — Registry push and declared-state apply are restricted to the pipeline principal. Source: IAM policy simulation on both clouds. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per run. *Row `KSI-CMT-RMV.verify.1`: partly automated by `cmt-rmv-cfg-publish-limited-to-default-branch`. Not covered: Reads the publishing workflow's triggers against the branch the OIDC trust pins. It does not simulate IAM, so it does not show that no other principal can push, and it does not cover declared-state apply.*
- **CFG** — Exception register contains only the two declared categories and every entry carries a reason. Source: Repository file, checked in CI. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per run. *Row `KSI-CMT-RMV.verify.2`: automated by `cmt-rmv-cfg-exception-register-bounded`.*
- **CFG** — Every running image digest has a provenance record. Source: Provenance store versus running digests. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per run. *Row `KSI-CMT-RMV.verify.3`: automated by `cmt-rmv-ops-aws-running-images-provenance` (judged only while aws-phase2 stands).*
- **OPS** — Zero applies by a principal other than the pipeline, standing query, zero results expected. Source: CloudTrail and GCP audit logs filtered by principal. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per run. *Row `KSI-CMT-RMV.validate.1`: not automated -- gap. Declared state is applied by the operator, under a justified, time-bound elevation, not by the pipeline: the standing change exception, narrowed 2026-10-05 (registers/change-exceptions.yaml). A query for applies by anyone but the pipeline would return every apply. Risk: Applies rest on a person, bounded by elevation's justification, window and record, until an apply job exists in CI.*
- **OPS** — Zero registry pushes outside the pipeline, standing query, zero results expected. Source: Registry push events. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per run. *Row `KSI-CMT-RMV.validate.2`: partly automated by `cmt-rmv-ops-aws-pushes-by-pipeline-only`. Not covered: ECR only: Artifact Registry pushes are in Cloud Audit Logs, which are not in the corpus.*
- **OPS** — Every running image digest traces to a commit, zero unattributable. Source: Running digests versus provenance records. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per deployment. *Row `KSI-CMT-RMV.validate.3`: automated by `cmt-rmv-ops-aws-running-images-provenance` (judged only while aws-phase2 stands).*
- **OPS** — Zero direct modifications outside the exception register, standing query, zero results expected. Source: Mutation query from KSI-SVC-ACM cross-referenced against the register. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per run. *Row `KSI-CMT-RMV.validate.4`: partly automated by `svc-acm-ops-aws-changes-by-declared-principals`. Not covered: The exception register's standing entry -- elevated applies by the operator -- is encoded as the elevated role's allowance, not cross-referenced entry by entry. AWS only.*
- **OPS** — Every emergency modification has a reconciliation record. Source: Responder actions versus reconciliation entries. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per run. *Row `KSI-CMT-RMV.validate.5`: not automated -- manual. Emergency modifications are entered in registers/change-exceptions.yaml under emergency_response and reconciled the same day (INCIDENT-RESPONSE.md step 4). None has occurred; the signal report counts them (emergency_modifications, 0). Evidence: registers/change-exceptions.yaml; docs/INCIDENT-RESPONSE.md step 4. Risk: Reconciliation of an emergency change rests on the operator following the runbook; nothing correlates a change made during an incident with its reconciliation entry.*
- **OPS** — Every deployed artifact in the inventory has a provenance record, zero unattributable. Source: Reconciliation against the inventory generated for KSI-PIY-GIV. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per run. *Row `KSI-CMT-RMV.validate.6`: partly automated by `cmt-rmv-ops-aws-running-images-provenance` (judged only while aws-phase2 stands). Not covered: Running ECS containers; the analytics image's provenance is its pinned digest in pipeline.tf, built by build-and-push, not traced here.*
- **Automated evidence.** 4 collector check(s) in the run on 2026-10-26: 1 NOT_STANDING, 3 PASS. Historical metrics (SDR-CSX-KMT) cover 19 day(s) of CI runs since 2026-10-02, in the metrics evidence object below; the year is what exists, not a full year.

### Assessment

- **No independent assessor is engaged.** The statements below are the provider's own assurance reasoning from the determination. They are not an independent assessment and should not be read as one.
- **Automation assurance.** Accuracy is tested by making a direct modification as a non-pipeline principal and confirming both the drift query and the register check fail it, then adding a register entry and confirming the register check clears while drift detection still reports the change. That divergence is correct and worth testing: a permitted direct change is still drift, and the two determinations should each say what they mean rather than one silencing the other. Sufficiency is bounded by principal attribution, since a change made using the pipeline's own credentials outside the pipeline is indistinguishable from a pipeline change; the claim therefore depends on that principal being federated and short-lived, which SNU and VCM established.
- **Limitations and residual risk.** The database is a standing exception, with schema and parameter changes applied in place; the instance could be replaced through snapshot and restore, but doing so for routine changes would be disproportionate, and this is the largest carve-out in the determination and is declared rather than minimised. Emergency modification is direct by design, and the reconciliation requirement bounds it without removing it, so during an incident the environment is genuinely in undeclared state. Attribution depends on the pipeline principal not being usable outside the pipeline. The synthetic environment changes deliberately and infrequently, so the pressure that produces a console fix under a deadline never arises, and that pressure is the real reason this control fails in practice.

### Tests

- Negative control for `cmt-rmv-cfg-exception-register-bounded` (collector/self_test.py): inputs that must fail it: a third category, entry outside the categories, entry without a reason, standing without a closing condition.
- Negative control for `cmt-rmv-cfg-publish-limited-to-default-branch` (collector/self_test.py): a workflow where also triggers from develop.
- Negative control for `cmt-rmv-ops-aws-pushes-by-pipeline-only` (collector/self_test.py): inputs that must fail it: no rows, query failed, query failed, zero expected, rows where none expected, unknown expectation.

## KSI-CMT-LMC — Implemented

### Implementation

- The subject is wider than RMV's and the definition is what widens it. RMV governed changes to machine-based information resources; LMC governs modifications to the cloud service offering, and the definition's note points at the Minimum Assessment Scope, which is everything likely to handle federal customer data or affect its confidentiality, integrity or availability. A modification with no infrastructure footprint therefore still counts, and three change surfaces fall outside everything built so far: repository content, pipeline definition, and policy and detection content. Each alters the offering's security posture without touching a resource. A change to the policy set alters what the merge gate enforces, a change to a detection query alters what log review examines, and a change to the role model from KSI-IAM-ELP alters what least-privilege checks compare against. That is the substantive finding here and it generalises a position this project has reached piecemeal: the controls that govern the offering are themselves part of the offering. Monitored carries the stricter meaning established in LET, where logged means the record exists and monitored means something watches and can alert, and CM-8(3) automated unauthorized component detection and CM-3(2) testing and validating changes both point at the monitoring half having teeth. Monitoring is applied selectively rather than uniformly: changes to the policy set, role model and detection content are alerted, because a silent weakening of a control is exactly what an actor with repository access would do, whereas routine infrastructure changes are logged and reviewed rather than alerted, because the pipeline is the expected source and alerting on expected events trains people to ignore alerts. MA-2 controlled maintenance is the odd mapping, since there is no hardware maintenance in a cloud-hosted offering; the applicable reading is maintenance actions on the offering itself, being restore tests, rotation workflows and rebuild executions, which modify the offering and are logged as such rather than treated as background.
- **1. Repository event delivery** — Commit and pull request events delivered to the central store and normalized with the rest. Emitted by the pipeline to the event bus using the federated identity it already holds, rather than a webhook into a public endpoint -- no new ingress and no shared secret to rotate. Captures events that trigger a workflow run rather than every repository event; branch protection routes changes through pull requests, so coverage is good rather than total. *(Repo and AWS; provisioned by Terraform)*
- **2. Control-content alerting** — Changes to policy rules, detection queries, the role model from KSI-IAM-ELP and declared baselines alert to the detection path. *(Repo; provisioned by Terraform)*
- **3. Maintenance action logging** — Restore tests, rotation runs and rebuild executions logged as maintenance modifications. *(Both; provisioned by Terraform)*
- **4. Change attribution** — Every change record carries the principal, and unattributable changes are surfaced. *(Both; provisioned by Terraform)*
- **5. Surface register** — Every change surface with its log source and whether it is monitored or logged only. *(Repo; provisioned by Version control)*

### Validation

- **Cycle.** Required: 3 days. Current practice: the collector runs daily from GitHub Actions (.github/workflows/collect.yml), as a read-only identity in each cloud with no stored credential, and this record was emitted by that run. Daily is stricter than every required cycle above (DECISIONS.md, 2026-10-01).
- **CFG** — Repository events deliver to the central store. Source: Delivery configuration versus store partitions. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per run. *Row `KSI-CMT-LMC.verify.1`: not automated -- gap. GitHub's repository events -- pushes, workflow changes, settings -- are not delivered to the central store; the git history and GitHub's own audit log are the record. Risk: A repository change is visible in git and GitHub, but no query over the corpus sees it.*
- **CFG** — Control-content changes have alert rules targeting the detection path. Source: Alert rule configuration. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per run. *Row `KSI-CMT-LMC.verify.2`: not automated -- gap. No alert rule fires on a change to control content -- policy rules, checks, workflows. Such changes are reviewed only as commits. Risk: A change weakening a control would not alert; it would show in the next run's results and in git.*

Required every 3 days; implemented: Per run. *Row `KSI-CMT-LMC.verify.3`: partly automated by `mla-let-cfg-aws-trail-data-events-scoped`. Not covered: Maintenance by the operator is management-plane activity, which the trail records into the log store; GitHub-side maintenance (workflow runs) is not delivered there.*

Not covered: AWS changes only; GitHub commits carry GitHub's account attribution, unsigned (SVC-VRI verify 3), and GCP's changes are not in the corpus.*

Only the AWS change surface delivers to the corpus; its daily presence is implied by the standing queries, not checked per surface. The other surfaces -- GitHub, GCP, Google Admin -- are not delivered (CMT-LMC verify 1). Risk: A surface falling silent would not be detected.*

The timed restore of 2026-10-06 appears in CloudTrail as RestoreDBInstanceToPointInTime and DeleteDBInstance by the elevated role, seen in the corpus the same day. Not a standing check: the corpus's query window is three days. Evidence: DECISIONS.md, 2026-10-07 (the change survey); deliberate-tests/scenario=timed_point_in_time_restore/. Risk: Shown once, by a real maintenance action, not re-tested each cycle.*

### Assessment

- **No independent assessor is engaged.** The statements below are the provider's own assurance reasoning from the determination. They are not an independent assessment and should not be read as one.
- **Automation assurance.** Accuracy is tested by committing a change to a monitored control artifact and confirming the alert fires, then to an unmonitored one and confirming it logs without alerting. The second direction matters as much as the first, because alerting on everything is the same failure as alerting on nothing. Sufficiency is bounded by delivery: repository events depend on the hosting platform's audit export continuing to work, and a failure there would be silent were it not covered by the source-silence check from KSI-MLA-LET, which it is, since repository events are a listed source.
- **Limitations and residual risk.** Repository event fidelity depends on the hosting platform; force-pushes and history rewrites appear in the audit stream but reconstruct differently from the commit history, so an actor with repository access has a path this does not fully close. Alerting on control content is only as good as the definition of control content: a change to an infrastructure module that indirectly weakens a control is logged but not alerted, and would be caught by drift detection rather than here. The synthetic environment has a single contributor, so attribution is trivially correct and the case this control exists for, distinguishing a legitimate change from a malicious one by the same principal, never arises.

- Negative control for `cmt-lmc-ops-aws-changes-attributed` (collector/self_test.py): inputs that must fail it: no rows, query failed, query failed, zero expected, rows where none expected, unknown expectation.
- Negative control for `cmt-lmc-ops-register-change-surface` (collector/self_test.py): inputs that must fail it: inventoried class without an entry, entry without the dimension, reduced availability without a reason, no backup without a reason, objective half-declared, evaluated less often than the plan, data store naming a missing check, empty statement, empty inventory.
- Designed, not yet automated: A deliberate change to a policy rule produces an alert (Test commit).
- Designed, not yet automated: A deliberate change to an unmonitored surface logs without alerting (Test commit, opposite direction).
- Designed, not yet automated: A maintenance action appears as a logged modification (Restore test correlated with change records).

## KSI-CMT-VTD — Implemented

### Implementation

- Throughout is the operative word and it resolves the boundary with MLA-EVC better than the earlier reading did. That reading had EVC testing the artifact and VTD testing the change through deployment, which put EVC outside VTD; but throughout deployment plainly includes the pre-apply gate as one stage among several. So VTD is the whole sequence and EVC is its first stage, which is cleaner than two adjacent indicators dividing the pipeline between them, and it means VTD's own contribution is the post-apply half plus the chaining. The stages: pre-merge policy evaluation from EVC; build, where dependencies are hash-verified and a signature is produced, from VRI and SVC-EIS; pre-deploy signature verification, digest pinning and provenance, from VRI and RMV; apply; post-apply health; post-apply verification that the change did what it was supposed to do; and rollback on failure. The last four are new here. Post-apply verification is the substantive one and it connects to a commitment made early in this project, that the remediation loop is generate-apply-reverify rather than read-only: VTD is where that becomes a property of deployment itself rather than something each indicator handles separately, so after any change the determinations covering affected resources re-run. Validation carries its defined meaning, the same one that forced VRI's checks to block rather than report: confirmation through objective evidence that capabilities support expected outcomes. A deployment that completes without error is therefore not validated, because completing is not evidence the intended outcome was achieved. Rollback is the part most likely to be skipped and it is what makes the chain meaningful, since without it a failed post-apply check produces a finding and leaves broken state running; the native deployment circuit breaker supplies it without building anything, which is worth claiming for the same reason CNA-EIS claimed native health enforcement. SI-2 flaw remediation appears here as well as in SVC-EIS, and the reading is that a change deployed to remediate a flaw must be validated to have remediated it, which is the same re-verify requirement arriving from a different direction. The stages defined here are themselves subject to effectiveness review under CMT-RVP, which tracks what each gate actually catches, so a stage that never fails anything becomes a finding rather than a passing check.
- **1. Stage chaining** — Pipeline stages ordered with failure stopping progression. No manual override without a recorded reason. *(Repo; provisioned by Version control)*
- **2. Apply validation** — Declared-state apply failure stops the deployment and surfaces rather than being retried silently. *(Repo; provisioned by CI)*
- **3. Post-apply health** — Service health, desired count and endpoint response checked after apply before the deployment counts as complete. *(AWS; provisioned by Terraform)*
- **4. Deployment rollback** — Native deployment circuit breaker, so a failing deployment reverts automatically. *(AWS; provisioned by Terraform)*
- **5. Determination re-run** — After a change, the determinations covering affected resources re-run and their records supersede. *(Both; provisioned by Terraform)*
- **6. Stage criteria register** — Each stage with its validation criterion and what a failure stops. *(Repo; provisioned by Version control)*

### Validation

- **Cycle.** Required: 3 days. Current practice: the collector runs daily from GitHub Actions (.github/workflows/collect.yml), as a read-only identity in each cloud with no stored credential, and this record was emitted by that run. Daily is stricter than every required cycle above (DECISIONS.md, 2026-10-01).
- **CFG** — Stages are ordered with failure gating progression. Source: Workflow definition. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per run. *Row `KSI-CMT-VTD.verify.1`: partly automated by `scr-mit-cfg-manifest-integrity-checked`, `svc-vri-cfg-signature-verified-before-digest-recorded`. Not covered: One ordered gate: the hashed-manifest requirement precedes the scanner's install in the build, so an unhashed manifest stops the job before anything runs. It also proves SCR-MIT build row 4, which no VERIFY or VALIDATE row asks for. Ordering is checked where it is load-bearing -- verification before the digest is recorded, the hashed manifest before the scanner installs -- not for every stage of every workflow.*
- **CFG** — Deployment circuit breaker enabled on every service. Source: ECS service deployment configuration. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per run. *Row `KSI-CMT-VTD.verify.2`: automated by `cmt-vtd-cfg-aws-circuit-breakers` (judged only while aws-phase2 stands).*

Not covered: The load balancer's health check is the post-apply check, as declared; a failing deploy is rolled back by the circuit breaker (cmt-vtd-ops-failing-deploy-rolls-back).*

Not covered: Tested per stage where a test exists: the deploy stage, by a revision that fails its health checks. The image signing and verification stage has no deliberate failing test. Tested per stage where a test exists: the policy stage, by a violating fixture plan. The image signing and verification stage has no deliberate failing test. Tested per stage where a test exists: the build's dependency audit, which exits non-zero on a known advisory. The image signing and verification stage has no deliberate failing test.*
- **OPS** — A deployment that applies but fails health checks rolls back. Source: Deliberate test: deploy a task that fails its health check. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per deployment. *Row `KSI-CMT-VTD.validate.2`: partly automated by `cmt-vtd-ops-failing-deploy-rolls-back`. Not covered: Run once per environment session and recorded (cadence 30 days), not every 3: the environment it needs stands only during sessions.*

Nothing correlates a remediation deployment with the determination re-running; the daily collector re-runs every check regardless, so a fix shows the next day. Risk: A remediation's effect is seen at the next daily run, not tied to the change that made it.*
- **OPS** — Zero deployments completed with a skipped stage, standing query, zero results expected. Source: Pipeline execution history. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per run. *Row `KSI-CMT-VTD.validate.4`: not automated -- inapplicable. Deployments are operator applies by elevation, not pipeline runs with stages that could be skipped. The build pipeline's stages run unconditionally in sequence. Risk: As CMT-RMV validate 1: a person applies, so stage-skipping is a question about the person, not a pipeline.*
- **OPS** — Every deployment has a post-apply validation record. Source: Deployment records versus validation records. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per run. *Row `KSI-CMT-VTD.validate.5`: not automated -- gap. No post-apply validation record is written per deployment; the collector's next run and, in sessions, the full run with the environment up are the validation. Risk: A deployment's validation is tied to a day's run, not to the deployment itself.*
- **Automated evidence.** 3 collector check(s) in the run on 2026-10-26: 1 NOT_STANDING, 2 PASS. Historical metrics (SDR-CSX-KMT) cover 19 day(s) of CI runs since 2026-10-02, in the metrics evidence object below; the year is what exists, not a full year.

### Assessment

- **No independent assessor is engaged.** The statements below are the provider's own assurance reasoning from the determination. They are not an independent assessment and should not be read as one.
- **Automation assurance.** Accuracy is tested per stage by introducing a failure at that stage and confirming the deployment stops. The rollback test runs separately, because a deployment can fail at apply and never reach the stage rollback protects, so proving one says nothing about the other. Deploying a deliberately unhealthy task and watching it revert is cheap and is the only evidence the circuit breaker is configured correctly. Sufficiency is bounded by what post-apply checks can observe: health checks confirm the service responds, not that it behaves correctly, and functional correctness of the application is outside this project's scope in the same way application-layer attack surface was under CNA-MAT.
- **Limitations and residual risk.** Post-apply validation confirms health, not correctness; a service that starts and responds but returns wrong answers passes every check here. The determination re-run covers determinations that have collectors, and those resting on behavioural evidence may need an observation window longer than a deployment, so their re-verification is delayed rather than immediate. Rollback is native for service deployments and is not available for infrastructure applies, where a failure leaves partial state and recovery is re-apply rather than revert; that asymmetry between the two halves of the pipeline is real and is stated rather than smoothed. The synthetic environment deploys rarely, so the chain is exercised deliberately rather than under the volume where stage-skipping pressure appears.

### Tests

- Negative control for `cmt-vtd-cfg-stage-criteria` (collector/self_test.py): inputs that must fail it: nothing a failure stops, empty.
- Negative control for `cmt-vtd-ops-failing-deploy-rolls-back` (collector/self_test.py): inputs that must fail it: never run, latest failed, errored, too old, another scenario's record.
- Designed, not yet automated: A deployment with a failing stage does not progress (Deliberate test per stage).
- Designed, not yet automated: A deployment that applies but fails health checks rolls back (Deliberate test: deploy a task that fails its health check).
- Designed, not yet automated: After a remediation deployment, the relevant determination re-runs and its record supersedes (Remediation test correlated with determination records).

## KSI-CMT-RVP — Partially Implemented

### Implementation

- The question that decides this indicator is whether a documented procedure means prose a human follows or whether an enforced pipeline counts. The case for prose is real: CM-9 is a configuration management plan and CM-3(4) is security representative involvement in a change control board, both of which describe organizational process. The case that wins is that in this architecture the change management procedure is the pipeline. There is no separate prose describing what people should do, because people cannot do it any other way: RMV restricted apply and registry push to the pipeline principal, so the procedure is not documentation of intent, it is the only available path. A pipeline definition in version control is also documented in a more literal sense than a document, being written down, versioned, and executed exactly as written. Splitting by subject, the encoded procedure is machine-based and its effectiveness is measurable, while the organizational wrapper, being a change advisory board and a security representative reviewing proposed changes, is non-machine and out of scope, which is why this is partial. Effectiveness is the harder word and needs distinguishing from conformance: every other indicator in this cluster asks whether the procedure was followed, and only this one asks whether following it works, which a procedure can fail while scoring perfectly on the first. Six signals are derivable from records the pipeline already produces: changes that passed all gates and still required rollback, findings arising from changes that passed review, manual overrides and their reasons, emergency direct modifications under RMV's exception, time from finding to remediated change, and the distribution of stage failures. That last one is the most easily overlooked and the most useful, because a gate that has never failed anything is either protecting against something that never happens or is misconfigured to pass everything, and both are effectiveness findings that no conformance check surfaces. This indicator reviews the project's own change machinery, which makes it the clearest instance of the position generalised under LMC: a control whose subject is other controls.
- **1. Procedure as code** — Stage order, gates, approval requirements and change access restrictions, versioned in the repository. *(Repo; provisioned by Version control)*
- **2. Effectiveness signal collection** — The six outcome-derived signals, computed from pipeline and deployment records. *(AWS; provisioned by Terraform)*
- **3. Gate efficacy tracking** — Per gate: invocations, failures, and a recorded position where a gate has never failed anything. *(Repo and AWS; provisioned by Terraform)*
- **4. Override record** — Every manual override with a reason, reviewed as an effectiveness signal rather than only as an exception. *(Repo; provisioned by Version control)*
- **5. Effectiveness signal report** — Section emitted by the shared signal report generator, aggregating this indicator's signals over the period. One generator serves all indicators that declare signals; no per-indicator scheduled job. *(AWS; provisioned by Terraform)*

### Validation

- **Cycle.** Required: 3 months. Current practice: the collector runs daily from GitHub Actions (.github/workflows/collect.yml), as a read-only identity in each cloud with no stored credential, and this record was emitted by that run. Daily is stricter than every required cycle above (DECISIONS.md, 2026-10-01).
- **CFG** — Procedure is encoded and versioned, and changes to it are recorded. Source: Repository history; change alerting from KSI-CMT-LMC. Required every 3 months; implemented: Per review. *Row `KSI-CMT-RVP.verify.1`: not automated -- manual. The procedure is the workflows and scripts in the repository, versioned in git; changes to them are commits. Change alerting is not built (CMT-LMC verify 2). Evidence: The repository's history of .github/workflows/, infra/aws/*.sh and deliberate/. Risk: A change to the procedure is recorded but not alerted.*
- **CFG** — Signal collection is configured and the review is scheduled. Source: Query and scheduler configuration. Required every 3 months; implemented: Per review. *Row `KSI-CMT-RVP.verify.2`: partly automated by `cmt-rvp-cfg-signal-generation-scheduled`. Not covered: The human review of what the signals mean was cut by decision (DECISIONS.md, 2026-09-14); generating them is what is checked.*
- **CFG** — Every gate has an efficacy record. Source: Gate efficacy store. Required every 3 months; implemented: Per review. *Row `KSI-CMT-RVP.verify.3`: partly automated by `cmt-rvp-ops-signal-report`. Not covered: Each gate's runs and failures are computed daily (gate_runs); there is no separate efficacy store, the signal report is the record.*
- **CFG** — Every override carries a reason. Source: Override register, checked in CI. Required every 3 months; implemented: Per review. *Row `KSI-CMT-RVP.verify.4`: partly automated by `mla-evc-cfg-policy-exceptions-bounded`. Not covered: Overrides of the policy gate are its exceptions, each with a reason and an expiry. Re-running a failed workflow is not recorded as an override.*
- **OPS** — Signal report section generated for the period, with signals computed including nil results. Source: Signal report output. Required every 3 months; implemented: Per review. *Row `KSI-CMT-RVP.validate.1`: automated by `cmt-rvp-ops-signal-report`.*
- **OPS** — Zero gates with invocations and no failures lacking a recorded position, standing query, zero results expected. Source: Gate efficacy records. Required every 3 months; implemented: Per review. *Row `KSI-CMT-RVP.validate.2`: not automated -- gap. Every gate has failed at least once in the period (the signal report's gates_never_failed is empty), so none needs a position today; nothing enforces one if that changes. Risk: A gate that stops failing anything would be listed by the signal report but not required to justify itself.*
- **OPS** — Zero overrides without a reason, standing query, zero results expected. Source: Override records. Required every 3 months; implemented: Per review. *Row `KSI-CMT-RVP.validate.3`: partly automated by `mla-evc-cfg-policy-exceptions-bounded`. Not covered: As verify 4: policy exceptions only.*
- **OPS** — Every rollback following a fully passed pipeline has a recorded gate review. Source: Rollback records versus review records. Required every 3 months; implemented: Per review. *Row `KSI-CMT-RVP.validate.4`: not automated -- gap. No revert has followed a fully passed pipeline (the signal report's reverts: 0), and no gate review record is defined for when one does. Risk: A rollback would be counted but its gate review would rest on the operator.*
- **OPS** — Signal report section generated for the period, with signals computed including nil results. Source: Signal report output. Required every 3 months; implemented: Per review. *Row `KSI-CMT-RVP.validate.5`: automated by `cmt-rvp-ops-signal-report`.*
- **Automated evidence.** 2 collector check(s) in the run on 2026-10-26: 2 PASS. Historical metrics (SDR-CSX-KMT) cover 19 day(s) of CI runs since 2026-10-02, in the metrics evidence object below; the year is what exists, not a full year.

### Assessment

- **No independent assessor is engaged.** The statements below are the provider's own assurance reasoning from the determination. They are not an independent assessment and should not be read as one.
- **Automation assurance.** Accuracy is tested by deliberately causing a change to pass all gates and fail at deployment, then confirming the effectiveness review flags it and identifies which gate should have caught it. That test is more work than most in this project and it is the only one exercising effectiveness rather than conformance. Sufficiency is bounded by signal availability: effectiveness is inferred from outcomes, so a procedure that is ineffective in ways producing no failed deployment, no override and no rollback is invisible. The clearest example is a gate that is too strict and causes changes to be batched into larger riskier deployments, which would show as a healthy pipeline.
- **Limitations and residual risk.** The organizational wrapper is out of scope, so there is no change advisory board, no security representative review of proposed changes, and no human judgment on change risk; CM-3(4) contemplates exactly that and it is not addressed, which is why this determination is partial. Effectiveness is inferred from outcomes, so ineffectiveness producing no adverse outcome is undetectable. The synthetic environment produces few changes, so most signals will be statistically meaningless, and gate efficacy over a handful of deployments tells you nothing; this is the determination most degraded by low volume in the whole project. The procedure being reviewed is the one this project built, so the review is self-assessment of self-authored machinery with no independent party judging whether the procedure is appropriate. The periodic human review step is out of scope project-wide: judging whether signals indicate a problem is organizational activity, the same category as the personnel and training material excluded at KSI-PIY-GIV. Signal generation is machine-based and is built; the judgment on top is declared, not performed.

### Tests

- Negative control for `cmt-rvp-cfg-signal-generation-scheduled` (collector/self_test.py): a workflow where digest recorded before the signature is verified.
- Negative control for `cmt-rvp-ops-signal-report` (collector/self_test.py): inputs that must fail it: no report, a section missing, a signal not computed, a signal absent, stale.

### Evidence

## KSI-SCR-MON — Partially Implemented

### Implementation

- Vulnerability is defined far more broadly than a published CVE. It takes its meaning from statute and covers any attribute of hardware, software, process or procedure that could facilitate defeating a control, explicitly including misconfigurations, exposures, weak credentials, insecure services and gaps in the indicators themselves. Monitoring for upstream vulnerabilities therefore reaches beyond dependency advisories to a provider changing a default, deprecating a capability, or altering behaviour in a way that weakens a control we rely on. That second category is the one nobody builds and it is real: a cloud provider changing a service default produces no advisory and can silently invalidate a determination, which is why provider change monitoring is a distinct mechanism here rather than folded into scanning. Third party software resources under the definition means anything not entirely inside the Minimum Assessment Scope, which IBP already established reaches the cloud providers themselves, so the population spans application dependencies, base images, infrastructure providers and modules, pipeline actions, the cloud services, and the identity provider. Contractual notification requirements, named in the statement's own mechanism list, are unavailable to this persona, which has no vendor agreements; public security bulletins and advisory feeds substitute and are weaker, being pull rather than push with no completeness or timeliness guarantee. SI-5 security alerts and advisories is what makes bulletin ingestion a requirement rather than a courtesy, and IR-6(3) supply chain incident coordination is likewise unavailable without contracts. Monitoring must also cover the offering's actual dependency set rather than a general feed: subscribing to advisories and filing them is not monitoring, so advisories are correlated against the dependency inventory and matches are routed to the detection path. Boundary against SVC-EIS is direction rather than mechanism: that indicator looks inward at our posture and this one looks outward at our suppliers, with the scanner shared rather than duplicated.
- **1. Dependency monitoring** — Scanner against lockfiles and images, shared with KSI-SVC-EIS rather than duplicated. *(Both; provisioned by CI and Terraform)*
- **2. Advisory ingestion** — Provider security bulletins and ecosystem advisory feeds ingested into the central store and normalized. Named sources: OSV as primary and the GitHub Advisory Database as second for ecosystem advisories, both covering the language ecosystems in the pinned manifests; vendor security bulletins for provider-side advisories. *(Both; provisioned by Terraform)*
- **3. Advisory correlation** — Ingested advisories matched against the dependency inventory, with matches routed to the detection path. This is what makes it monitoring rather than subscription. *(AWS; provisioned by Terraform)*
- **4. Provider change monitoring** — Service change and deprecation notifications ingested, since a changed default is a vulnerability under the definition and produces no advisory. Source not yet fixed: the provider health API is the obvious one and programmatic access has historically required a paid support plan. To be verified against current documentation and decided as a cost question rather than assumed in either direction. *(Both; provisioned by Terraform)*
- **5. Monitoring register** — Every third party resource class with its mechanism and whether monitoring is automatic, partial or unavailable. *(Repo; provisioned by Version control)*

### Validation

- **Cycle.** Required: 3 days. Current practice: the collector runs daily from GitHub Actions (.github/workflows/collect.yml), as a read-only identity in each cloud with no stored credential, and this record was emitted by that run. Daily is stricter than every required cycle above (DECISIONS.md, 2026-10-01).
- **CFG** — Every dependency is covered by scanning. Source: Scanner configuration versus dependency manifest. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per run. *Row `KSI-SCR-MON.verify.1`: partly automated by `scr-mon-cfg-dependencies-audited`. Not covered: Shows the build audits each application image's manifest. The collector's own manifest (requirements.txt) and the Terraform providers are not scanned.*
- **CFG** — Advisory feeds configured for every provider in use. Source: Feed configuration versus the third-party register from KSI-CNA-IBP. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per run. *Row `KSI-SCR-MON.verify.2`: partly automated by `scr-mon-ops-third-parties-monitoring-position`. Not covered: Every provider in use has a recorded monitoring position; for six it is 'unavailable', so no feed is configured for them.*
- **CFG** — Correlation configured against the dependency inventory. Source: Correlation query configuration. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per run. *Row `KSI-SCR-MON.verify.3`: partly automated by `scr-mon-cfg-dependencies-audited`. Not covered: pip-audit correlates each Python manifest against OSV at build; images are correlated by Inspector and Container Scanning. Terraform providers and actions are not correlated against advisories.*
- **CFG** — Every third party resource class carries a monitoring position. Source: Repository file, checked in CI. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per run. *Row `KSI-SCR-MON.verify.4`: automated by `scr-mon-ops-third-parties-monitoring-position`.*
- **OPS** — Every monitoring mechanism has produced output within its expected interval. Source: Store partitions per feed, using the source-silence check from KSI-MLA-LET. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per run. *Row `KSI-SCR-MON.validate.1`: partly automated by `svc-eis-cfg-aws-image-scan-coverage`. Not covered: Inspector's coverage of recent images is checked; GuardDuty's publishing and the build's audits are judged by their own checks. A feed falling silent is not detected per feed.*
- **OPS** — A known advisory affecting a resource in use is correlated and surfaced. Source: Test with a historical advisory for a package in the manifest. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per run. *Row `KSI-SCR-MON.validate.2`: partly automated by `scr-mon-ops-historical-advisory-surfaced`. Not covered: Run every 30 days by the operator and recorded, not every 3: the test changes or provokes the account, so it does not run unattended.*
- **OPS** — Zero dependencies in the inventory without scan coverage, standing query, zero results expected. Source: Dependency inventory versus scan results. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per run. *Row `KSI-SCR-MON.validate.3`: partly automated by `scr-mon-cfg-every-manifest-audited`. Not covered: Python manifests only. Terraform providers are pinned by lock-file hash but not correlated with advisories, and the build's matrix values are trusted rather than expanded: a service added to the repository but not the matrix would still fail here, since its manifest is undeclared.*
- **OPS** — Zero third party resources without a recorded monitoring position, standing query, zero results expected. Source: Reconciliation against the third-party register from KSI-CNA-IBP. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per run. *Row `KSI-SCR-MON.validate.4`: automated by `scr-mon-ops-third-parties-monitoring-position`.*
- **OPS** — A provider deprecation notice is ingested and surfaced. Source: Observed against real notices. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per run. *Row `KSI-SCR-MON.validate.5`: not automated -- gap. Provider change and deprecation notices are not ingested: the AWS Health API's programmatic access needs a paid support plan, and Google's notices are not collected (registers/third-parties.yaml). Risk: A deprecation affecting the environment is noticed only by a person reading the provider's announcements, or when something breaks.*
- **Automated evidence.** 4 collector check(s) in the run on 2026-10-26: 4 PASS. Historical metrics (SDR-CSX-KMT) cover 19 day(s) of CI runs since 2026-10-02, in the metrics evidence object below; the year is what exists, not a full year.

### Assessment

- **No independent assessor is engaged.** The statements below are the provider's own assurance reasoning from the determination. They are not an independent assessment and should not be read as one.
- **Automation assurance.** Accuracy is tested by introducing a dependency with a known historical advisory and confirming correlation surfaces it, then removing it and confirming the finding clears. That test separates correlation from ingestion, since a feed that arrives and is never matched against the inventory proves nothing. Feed liveness is covered by the source-silence check from KSI-MLA-LET, since advisory feeds are listed sources. Sufficiency is bounded twice: scanner coverage bounds the dependency half as stated in SVC-EIS, and the absence of contractual notification bounds the provider half, since public bulletins are pull rather than push and a vulnerability a provider chooses not to publish is invisible.
- **Limitations and residual risk.** Contractual notification is unavailable, since the persona has no vendor agreements, so the push half of the statement's own mechanism list cannot be used and public bulletins substitute with no completeness or timeliness guarantee; this is the principal reason the determination is bounded. Supply chain incident coordination under IR-6(3) is unavailable for the same reason. Provider change monitoring depends on providers publishing changes, and a silent default change is invisible to every mechanism here. The synthetic environment has a small dependency set, so correlation is easy and the real difficulty, thousands of transitive dependencies across many services, never arises.

### Tests

- Negative control for `scr-mon-cfg-dependencies-audited` (collector/self_test.py): a workflow where no scanning step.
- Negative control for `scr-mon-cfg-every-manifest-audited` (collector/self_test.py): inputs that must fail it: a manifest audited nowhere, the audit step removed, nothing tracked.
- Negative control for `scr-mon-ops-historical-advisory-surfaced` (collector/self_test.py): inputs that must fail it: never run, latest failed, errored, too old, another scenario's record.
- Negative control for `scr-mon-ops-third-parties-monitoring-position` (collector/self_test.py): inputs that must fail it: an unregistered action owner, same value, other kind, entry without a monitoring status, nothing in use.
- Designed, not yet automated: A known advisory affecting a resource in use is correlated and surfaced (Test with a historical advisory for a package in the manifest).

## KSI-SCR-MIT — Partially Implemented

### Implementation

- Three verbs, and only the first two are fully available to this persona. MON already identifies upstream vulnerabilities; what MIT adds is risk that is not a vulnerability, being a dependency that is unmaintained, a supplier that could disappear, or a component past its support window, plus the mitigation response. The twelve mappings split cleanly and that split decides the determination. On the technical side, SA-22 unsupported system components is the most useful mapping here and the one nothing in this project covered: a component can be fully patched and still unsupported, whether a base image past its support window, a runtime no longer receiving security fixes, or an abandoned provider, and that is a supply chain risk with no advisory attached but a checkable one, since every component version can be compared against its published support window. SI-7(1) integrity checks is already satisfied by digest pinning and lockfile hash verification. SC-18 mobile code reads oddly in a cloud context and the applicable instance is code executed that did not come from our build, being third-party pipeline actions and anything pulled at run time, which action pinning and digest pinning bound and which leaves the position that nothing is fetched and executed at run time. CA-7(4) risk monitoring is the ongoing review element. On the procurement side, SR-5 acquisition strategies, SR-6 supplier assessments, SA-9 external system services, and SA-10 and SA-11 developer configuration management and testing are obligations placed on a supplier through a contract, and a persona with no vendor agreements cannot assess suppliers, impose development requirements or negotiate acquisition terms; that is unavailable rather than skipped, the same distinction MON drew for contractual notification. One mapping sits in the middle and is built rather than declared: SA-15(3) criticality analysis is supplier-facing in the control's sense, but the question of which dependencies would hurt most if compromised or abandoned is answerable from our own architecture, which puts it on the buildable side of the line this project has drawn repeatedly. Mitigation is honestly bounded: a real provider can change supplier, negotiate terms or require remediation contractually, whereas the available set here is pin, vendor, replace, remove, or accept with a recorded reason.
- **1. End-of-support checking** — Every component version compared against its published support window, with findings kept distinct from vulnerability findings because the remediation differs -- a patched component can still be unsupported. Support windows sourced from endoflife.date. *(Repo and AWS; provisioned by Terraform and CI)*
- **2. Maintenance signal** — Last release date and maintainer activity per direct dependency, against a declared staleness threshold. *(Repo; provisioned by CI)*
- **3. Criticality rating** — Each dependency rated by what it would affect if compromised or abandoned, based on where it sits in the architecture. *(Repo; provisioned by Version control)*
- **4. No runtime fetch** — Build-time resolution only. Nothing downloaded and executed at run time, checked rather than assumed. *(Both; provisioned by Terraform and CI)*
- **5. Risk register** — Identified risks with mitigation or accepted-risk entries, each carrying a reason and a review date. *(Repo; provisioned by Version control)*
- **6. Effectiveness signal report** — Section emitted by the shared signal report generator, aggregating this indicator's signals over the period. One generator serves all indicators that declare signals; no per-indicator scheduled job. *(AWS; provisioned by Terraform)*

### Validation

- **Cycle.** Required: 3 days, 3 months. Current practice: the collector runs daily from GitHub Actions (.github/workflows/collect.yml), as a read-only identity in each cloud with no stored credential, and this record was emitted by that run. Daily is stricter than every required cycle above (DECISIONS.md, 2026-10-01).
- **CFG** — Every component carries a support-window position. Source: Component inventory versus published support data. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per run. *Row `KSI-SCR-MIT.verify.1`: partly automated by `scr-mit-cfg-risks-dispositioned`. Not covered: Support-window positions are recorded for the components judged at risk -- the base image, the Python runtime -- not reconciled against every component (Lambda runtimes, the database engine, providers).*
- **CFG** — Every direct dependency has a maintenance signal and a criticality rating. Source: Repository files, checked in CI. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per run. *Row `KSI-SCR-MIT.verify.2`: not automated -- gap. Direct dependencies carry no recorded maintenance signal or criticality rating; the supply-chain risk register holds the components judged risky, not every dependency. Risk: An unmaintained dependency would not be flagged until it carried an advisory.*
- **CFG** — No build or task definition fetches code at run time. Source: Build definitions and task definitions. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per run. *Row `KSI-SCR-MIT.verify.3`: automated by `scr-mit-cfg-no-runtime-fetch`.*
- **CFG** — Risk register entries carry a reason and a review date. Source: Repository file, checked in CI. Required every 3 months; implemented: Per review. *Row `KSI-SCR-MIT.verify.4`: automated by `scr-mit-cfg-risks-dispositioned`.*
- **OPS** — Zero components past end of support with no recorded position, standing query, zero results expected. Source: Component versions versus published support windows. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per run. *Row `KSI-SCR-MIT.validate.1`: partly automated by `scr-mit-cfg-risks-dispositioned`. Not covered: Support-window positions are recorded for the components judged at risk -- the base image, the Python runtime -- not reconciled against every component (Lambda runtimes, the database engine, providers).*
- **OPS** — Zero dependencies exceeding the staleness threshold with no recorded position, standing query, zero results expected. Source: Release date signals. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per run. *Row `KSI-SCR-MIT.validate.2`: not automated -- gap. No staleness threshold is declared and dependency release dates are not collected. Risk: A dependency that stops being released is noticed only when an advisory lands against it.*
- **OPS** — Zero identified risks without a mitigation or accepted-risk entry, standing query, zero results expected. Source: Risk register. Required every 3 months; implemented: Per review. *Row `KSI-SCR-MIT.validate.3`: automated by `scr-mit-cfg-risks-dispositioned`.*
- **OPS** — Zero runtime fetches observed, standing query, zero results expected. Source: Egress records, already absent by the architecture established under KSI-CNA-RNT. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per run. *Row `KSI-SCR-MIT.validate.4`: partly automated by `cna-rnt-ops-task-internet-egress-blocked`. Not covered: Runtime fetches cannot occur from the app tier -- no route out, shown by the egress tests -- rather than being observed absent in egress records.*
- **OPS** — Signal report section generated for the period, with signals computed including nil results. Source: Signal report output. Required every 3 months; implemented: Per review. *Row `KSI-SCR-MIT.validate.5`: automated by `scr-mit-ops-signal-report`.*
- **Automated evidence.** 4 collector check(s) in the run on 2026-10-26: 4 PASS. Historical metrics (SDR-CSX-KMT) cover 19 day(s) of CI runs since 2026-10-02, in the metrics evidence object below; the year is what exists, not a full year.

### Assessment

- **No independent assessor is engaged.** The statements below are the provider's own assurance reasoning from the determination. They are not an independent assessment and should not be read as one.
- **Automation assurance.** Accuracy is tested by pinning a dependency to a known end-of-life version and confirming it is flagged distinctly from a vulnerability finding, which matters because an unsupported component needs replacing rather than updating and a combined finding would route it to the wrong remediation. Sufficiency is bounded by published support data, which is declared by maintainers and inconsistent across ecosystems, so a component whose maintainer publishes nothing is unassessable and is recorded as such rather than assumed supported.
- **Limitations and residual risk.** Supplier-facing mitigation is unavailable, since there are no agreements, so supplier assessment, contractually imposed development requirements and acquisition terms are unaddressed; that is why this determination is partial. Criticality is rated against our own architecture rather than through the supplier-facing analysis the mapped control contemplates. Support-window data is inconsistent and sometimes absent, and a component with no published support policy is unassessable. The synthetic environment has a small dependency set with negligible transitive depth, so concentration risk, where many dependencies trace to one maintainer, cannot arise and is not tested. The periodic human review step is out of scope project-wide: judging whether signals indicate a problem is organizational activity, the same category as the personnel and training material excluded at KSI-PIY-GIV. Signal generation is machine-based and is built; the judgment on top is declared, not performed.

### Tests

- Negative control for `scr-mit-cfg-manifest-integrity-checked` (collector/self_test.py): a workflow where digest recorded before the signature is verified.
- Negative control for `scr-mit-cfg-no-runtime-fetch` (collector/self_test.py): inputs that must fail it: entrypoint fetches, declared command fetches, nothing read.
- Negative control for `scr-mit-cfg-risks-dispositioned` (collector/self_test.py): inputs that must fail it: review date passed, mitigation unstated, no disposition.
- Negative control for `scr-mit-ops-signal-report` (collector/self_test.py): inputs that must fail it: no report, a section missing, a signal not computed, a signal absent, stale.

## KSI-RPL-RRO — Partially Implemented

### Implementation

- This indicator is upstream of the other three in its cluster and of CNA-OFA, which already borrowed its phrasing to resolve what appropriately meant: backups align with these objectives, plans align with them, and recovery is tested against them, so nothing in the cluster means anything until they exist. Both halves of the referent matter. Needs alone would produce aspirational numbers and capabilities alone would produce numbers that merely describe what the architecture happens to do, so alignment means the objectives sit where the two meet and the review asks whether they still do. That makes this structurally different from most determinations in the project: almost everything else checks whether reality matches a declared standard, whereas here the standard itself is under review. Objectives are declared per resource class rather than as a single pair, because a uniform number would be wrong for most resources. Compute is stateless with automatic replacement, so its recovery time is minutes and it has no recovery point. The database recovers by restore from snapshot with point-in-time recovery, measured in tens of minutes, with its recovery point bounded by the backup mechanism. Object storage and logs are multi-zone by construction. The analytics warehouse is different in kind, holding derived data, so recovery means re-running the pipeline rather than restoring a backup, and treating it as a store to be backed up would be building recovery for something reconstructible. Capabilities are already fixed by decisions made elsewhere, principally the single-AZ choice recorded under CNA-OFA, so the database objective is a restore time rather than a failover time; declaring numbers the architecture cannot meet would be the same failure as declaring a blast radius wider than reality. Persistently reviewed carries a trigger beyond the cycle here, because an architecture change can invalidate an objective silently, so review is scheduled and also prompted by changes to backup configuration, availability posture or recovery mechanism, using the change surfaces established under CMT-LMC.
- **1. Objective register** — Recovery time and recovery point objectives per resource class, with the basis for each and a stated reason where one does not apply. *(Repo; provisioned by Version control)*
- **2. Capability bound check** — Each declared objective compared against what its mechanism can actually deliver. An objective shorter than the backup interval is a number nobody can meet. *(Both; provisioned by Terraform)*
- **3. Change-triggered review** — Objective review prompted by changes to backup configuration, availability posture or recovery mechanism. *(Both; provisioned by Terraform)*
- **4. Effectiveness signal report** — Section emitted by the shared signal report generator, aggregating this indicator's signals over the period. One generator serves all indicators that declare signals; no per-indicator scheduled job. *(AWS; provisioned by Terraform)*
- **5. Revision record** — Every objective change with a reason and the measurement or need that prompted it. *(Repo; provisioned by Version control)*

### Validation

- **Cycle.** Required: 3 months. Current practice: the collector runs daily from GitHub Actions (.github/workflows/collect.yml), as a read-only identity in each cloud with no stored credential, and this record was emitted by that run. Daily is stricter than every required cycle above (DECISIONS.md, 2026-10-01).
- **CFG** — Every resource class has an objective pair or a recorded not-applicable reason. Source: Objective register versus inventory from KSI-PIY-GIV. Required every 3 months; implemented: Per review. *Row `KSI-RPL-RRO.verify.1`: automated by `rpl-rro-ops-register-objective`.*
- **CFG** — Declared objectives are within mechanism capability. Source: Register versus backup interval and restore measurement. Required every 3 months; implemented: Per review. *Row `KSI-RPL-RRO.verify.2`: partly automated by `cna-ofa-ops-timed-point-in-time-restore`. Not covered: Only the database's objective is held to a measurement (15.7 minutes against 60); the other twenty objectives are declared from design.*
- **CFG** — Review schedule and change trigger are configured. Source: Scheduler and event rule configuration. Required every 3 months; implemented: Per review. *Row `KSI-RPL-RRO.verify.3`: not automated -- descoped. Periodic human review cycles were cut on 2026-09-14: a quarterly review by one contributor over statistically empty signals would be a ceremony. Signal generation was kept, as the shared signal report. Risk: Objectives are revisited when something changes them, by the operator, not on a schedule.*
- **CFG** — Every objective revision carries a reason. Source: Repository history, checked in CI. Required every 3 months; implemented: Per review. *Row `KSI-RPL-RRO.verify.4`: not automated -- gap. Objective revisions are commits to registers/resources.yaml; their reasons are in the commit message and DECISIONS.md, which nothing checks. Risk: An objective could change without a recorded reason.*

Required every 3 months; implemented: Per review. *Row `KSI-RPL-RRO.validate.1`: automated by `rpl-rro-ops-register-objective`.*

Required every 3 months; implemented: Per review. *Row `KSI-RPL-RRO.validate.2`: partly automated by `cna-ofa-ops-timed-point-in-time-restore`. Not covered: Only the database's objective is held to a measurement (15.7 minutes against 60); the other twenty objectives are declared from design.*
- **OPS** — Every architecture change affecting recovery capability has a corresponding review record. Source: Change records from KSI-CMT-LMC versus review records. Required every 3 months; implemented: Per review. *Row `KSI-RPL-RRO.validate.3`: not automated -- gap. Nothing ties an architecture change to a recovery review record. Risk: A change affecting recovery would not prompt a review.*
- **OPS** — Signal report section generated for the period, with signals computed including nil results. Source: Signal report output. Required every 3 months; implemented: Per review. *Row `KSI-RPL-RRO.validate.4`: automated by `rpl-rro-ops-signal-report`.*

### Assessment

- **No independent assessor is engaged.** The statements below are the provider's own assurance reasoning from the determination. They are not an independent assessment and should not be read as one.
- **Automation assurance.** Accuracy is tested by declaring a recovery point objective shorter than the backup interval and confirming the capability check fails it, then correcting it. The change trigger is tested by altering backup retention and confirming a review is prompted. Sufficiency is bounded by the asserted half: the capability side is measurable and checked, whereas the needs side is a judgment made on behalf of a fictional business and no check validates whether the chosen numbers would satisfy a real customer.
- **Limitations and residual risk.** Business needs are asserted. There is no customer, no contractual availability commitment and no revenue impact to weigh, so the capability half of the referent is real and measurable while the needs half is invented, which is the fundamental limit of this determination and why it is partial. The objectives are bounded by architecture decisions made for cost reasons, principally single-AZ, so they describe this environment rather than what a real Class C provider would target. No objective covers correlated failure, since the declared numbers assume one thing fails at a time. The synthetic environment has no real recovery events, so objectives are exercised deliberately rather than validated against incidents. The periodic human review step is out of scope project-wide: judging whether signals indicate a problem is organizational activity, the same category as the personnel and training material excluded at KSI-PIY-GIV. Signal generation is machine-based and is built; the judgment on top is declared, not performed.

### Tests

- Negative control for `rpl-rro-ops-register-objective` (collector/self_test.py): inputs that must fail it: inventoried class without an entry, entry without the dimension, reduced availability without a reason, no backup without a reason, objective half-declared, evaluated less often than the plan, data store naming a missing check, empty statement, empty inventory.
- Negative control for `rpl-rro-ops-signal-report` (collector/self_test.py): inputs that must fail it: no report, a section missing, a signal not computed, a signal absent, stale.

### Evidence

- *Report, 2026-10-26* — Every resource class in the live AWS and GCP inventories has an entry in registers/resources.yaml recording a recovery time and point objective, or a recorded reason that none applies; zero unaccounted. Result: PASS — all 15 inventoried classes carry a recorded objective position ([definition](https://github.com/elvie-valmores/fedramp-20x-ksi-assessment/blob/97cdfd2eee91745843bbb9d17758668091a06d60/collector/checks/rpl-rro-ops-register-objective.json))

Result: PASS — 1 section(s), all 3 signals computed over 90 days ([definition](https://github.com/elvie-valmores/fedramp-20x-ksi-assessment/blob/97cdfd2eee91745843bbb9d17758668091a06d60/collector/checks/rpl-rro-ops-signal-report.json))
