## KSI-RPL-ABO — Partially Implemented

### Implementation

- Alignment is narrower than backups existing. A resource can be backed up daily and still fail this indicator if its declared recovery point objective is one hour, so the check is a comparison against the objectives declared under RRO rather than a presence check, and that comparison is mechanical once the objectives exist, which is the payoff for working RRO first. CM-2(3) is the mapping most easily overlooked and it widens the scope: retention of previous baseline configurations is not a data backup but the ability to recover a prior configuration, which here means state version history and prior task definitions and image revisions. That connects to ACM, which established state as a security-relevant asset with versioning; ABO adds that the versioning must be deep enough to recover a prior baseline, which is a retention question ACM never asked. Two resource classes are deliberately unbacked and RRO already established why: the analytics warehouse holds derived data reconstructible by re-running the pipeline, and the landing bucket is intermediate and regenerable, so a backup for either would be machinery with no benefit. Those are recorded as decided rather than absent, because a coverage check would otherwise report them as gaps every cycle and become noise. CP-6 alternate storage site is the first of the data-centre-era controls that dominate ARP, and the reading is set here rather than improvised there: in a cloud context the applicable property is that backups do not share a failure domain with what they back up, which snapshots and versioned objects satisfy at zone granularity since the provider stores them separately from the instance. What this architecture lacks is cross-region backup, so a region-level failure takes the backups with it; that is declared rather than argued away and follows the same cost reasoning as the single-AZ decision. SI-12 information retention is handled by different mechanisms in different places, since log retention is enforced by object lock which cannot be shortened while backup retention is enforced by lifecycle policy which can, and the determinations state which applies where rather than implying uniform handling.
- **1. Backup configuration** — Automated snapshots with point-in-time recovery, retention matching the declared recovery window. *(AWS; provisioned by Terraform)*
- **2. Configuration baseline retention** — State version depth, image retention and task definition revision history sufficient for prior-baseline recovery. *(Both; provisioned by Terraform)*
- **3. Alignment check** — Every backup interval and retention compared against the objective register from KSI-RPL-RRO. *(Both; provisioned by Terraform)*
- **4. Coverage reconciliation** — Inventory compared against backup coverage, with deliberately unbacked resources recognised as decided rather than flagged. *(Both; provisioned by Terraform)*
- **5. Failure domain check** — Backup location compared against source location at both zone and region granularity. *(Both; provisioned by Terraform)*

### Validation

- **Cycle.** Required: 3 days. Current practice: the collector runs daily from GitHub Actions (.github/workflows/collect.yml), as a read-only identity in each cloud with no stored credential, and this record was emitted by that run. Daily is stricter than every required cycle above (DECISIONS.md, 2026-10-01).
- **CFG** — Backups configured with retention matching declared windows. Source: RDS backup configuration; object versioning settings. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per run. *Row `KSI-RPL-ABO.verify.1`: partly automated by `svc-sin-cfg-aws-database-settings` (judged only while aws-phase1 stands). Not covered: The database's automated backup retention, judged while phase 1 stands; object versioning on the stores is checked elsewhere (state bucket, Object Lock) and not joined to the declared windows here.*
- **CFG** — Configuration baseline retention is sufficient for the declared recovery depth. Source: State backend versioning; registry and task definition retention. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per run. *Row `KSI-RPL-ABO.verify.2`: partly automated by `svc-acm-cfg-aws-state-bucket`. Not covered: State versioning is checked; that superseded versions are kept the declared 90 days is shown by the prior-state deliberate test, not here. Registry and task definition retention are the lifecycle and teardown settings, not checked.*
- **CFG** — Every resource has backup coverage or a recorded decision that one is unnecessary. Source: Coverage register versus inventory from KSI-PIY-GIV. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per run. *Row `KSI-RPL-ABO.verify.3`: automated by `rpl-abo-ops-register-backup`.*
- **CFG** — Backup locations compared against source locations. Source: Snapshot and object storage location metadata. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per run. *Row `KSI-RPL-ABO.verify.4`: not automated -- gap. Backups are in the same region as their source: RDS automated backups and the S3 stores' versions are in us-east-1, with no cross-region copy. Risk: A regional outage would take the backups with the source. Recorded with the zone-level availability position (CNA-OFA).*
- **OPS** — Zero resources with backup intervals exceeding their declared objective, standing query, zero results expected. Source: Backup configuration versus the register from KSI-RPL-RRO. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per run. *Row `KSI-RPL-ABO.validate.1`: partly automated by `svc-sin-cfg-aws-database-settings` (judged only while aws-phase1 stands). Not covered: The database's backup configuration -- point-in-time recovery and 7-day retention -- meets its 5-minute recovery point; other classes' backup intervals are not compared with their objectives.*
- **OPS** — Zero resources in the inventory without backup coverage or a recorded decision, standing query, zero results expected. Source: Reconciliation against the inventory generated for KSI-PIY-GIV. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per run. *Row `KSI-RPL-ABO.validate.2`: automated by `rpl-abo-ops-register-backup`.*
- **OPS** — A prior configuration baseline is recoverable to the declared depth. Source: Deliberate test: restore a prior state version and a prior task definition. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per run. *Row `KSI-RPL-ABO.validate.3`: partly automated by `rpl-abo-ops-prior-state-recoverable`. Not covered: Recoverability is shown by reading back the oldest state version and task definition, not by applying them, and it is run every 30 days, not 3. The declared depth is 90 days; the state bucket holds 17, all it has existed.*
- **OPS** — Backups exist at the expected recovery point when checked. Source: Snapshot timestamps against the declared window. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per run. *Row `KSI-RPL-ABO.validate.4`: partly automated by `svc-sin-cfg-aws-backups-restorable`. Not covered: Retained backups are checked restorable under a live key; their timestamps are not compared with a declared recovery point.*
- **OPS** — Zero unencrypted backups, standing query, zero results expected. Source: Snapshot and object encryption status, per KSI-SVC-SIN criteria. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per run. *Row `KSI-RPL-ABO.validate.5`: partly automated by `svc-sin-cfg-aws-backups-restorable`. Not covered: Database backups only, judged per backup. Object stores are judged by their bucket's default encryption (svc-sin-cfg-aws-stores-use-declared-keys), not object by object.*
- **Automated evidence.** 2 collector check(s) in the run on 2026-10-26: 2 PASS. Historical metrics (SDR-CSX-KMT) cover 19 day(s) of CI runs since 2026-10-02, in the metrics evidence object below; the year is what exists, not a full year.

### Assessment

- **No independent assessor is engaged.** The statements below are the provider's own assurance reasoning from the determination. They are not an independent assessment and should not be read as one.
- **Automation assurance.** Accuracy is tested by shortening a backup retention below its declared window and confirming the alignment check fails, then restoring it. The configuration baseline test restores a prior state version and a prior task definition, because retaining versions and being able to recover from one are different claims. The failure domain check is tested by comparing a backup's stored location against its source and confirming the region-level gap is reported rather than passed. Sufficiency is bounded by what the provider exposes about backup storage location: physical separation from the instance is provider-asserted rather than observable, so the zone-level claim is inherited while the region-level gap is directly observable.
- **Limitations and residual risk.** There is no cross-region backup, so a region-level failure takes the backups with it and the failure-domain property holds at zone granularity while failing at region granularity; this follows the same cost reasoning as the single-AZ decision and is declared rather than argued around. Backup storage separation at zone level is provider-asserted rather than observable. Retention mechanisms differ in kind across the project, since object lock cannot be shortened and lifecycle policy can, so retention handling is not uniform and each determination states which applies. The synthetic environment has small data volumes, so backup and restore are fast in a way they would not be at scale, where restore time is dominated by data size.

### Tests

- Negative control for `rpl-abo-ops-prior-state-recoverable` (collector/self_test.py): inputs that must fail it: never run, latest failed, errored, too old, another scenario's record.
- Negative control for `rpl-abo-ops-register-backup` (collector/self_test.py): inputs that must fail it: inventoried class without an entry, entry without the dimension, reduced availability without a reason, no backup without a reason, objective half-declared, evaluated less often than the plan, data store naming a missing check, empty statement, empty inventory.
- Designed, not yet automated: A prior configuration baseline is recoverable to the declared depth (Deliberate test: restore a prior state version and a prior task definition).

The newest recorded run must have passed within 30 days. Result: PASS — passed 21.0 day(s) ago: an older state version of the same lineage and a prior task definition are both retrievable ([definition](https://github.com/elvie-valmores/fedramp-20x-ksi-assessment/blob/97cdfd2eee91745843bbb9d17758668091a06d60/collector/checks/rpl-abo-ops-prior-state-recoverable.json))

## KSI-RPL-TRC — Implemented

### Implementation

- This is the indicator the commitment under CNA-OFA was made for, where a tested restore with a measured time was argued to be better evidence than an untested failover; it is also the one determination in this project where the evidence is stronger than a typical provider's, because most providers configure recovery and never exercise it. Incidents and contingencies are two categories rather than a phrase, because the defined meaning of incident covers occurrences jeopardising integrity and confidentiality as well as availability, so the statement reaches recovery from compromise and not only from failure. Those are different operations: restoring after an instance failure returns to the last good state, whereas recovering after a compromise requires restoring to a moment before a known event, which is what point-in-time recovery makes possible and a snapshot alone does not. The boundary with the incident response cluster is tense rather than subject: all three of its indicators are retrospective, covering after-action reports, review of past incidents for patterns, and review of procedure effectiveness, whereas TRC tests the capability, which is why IR-3 incident response testing maps here. Backup integrity verification is distinct from restore success, since a backup can restore cleanly and contain corrupt data; the restore test proves the mechanism and the consistency check proves the backup, and only the second catches the failure that is invisible. The reconstruction test exists to validate a claim rather than a mechanism: RRO declared the analytics warehouse reconstructible rather than backed up, and until the pipeline has actually rebuilt it from source that is an assumption, so testing it converts a scoping decision into a demonstrated capability.
- **1. Timed restore test** — Restore into a disposable instance, measure elapsed time, record the result, destroy the instance. *(AWS; provisioned by Terraform and CI)*
- **2. Point-in-time restore test** — Restore to a chosen prior moment, confirming recovery from compromise is possible rather than only recovery from failure. *(AWS; provisioned by Terraform and CI)*
- **3. Backup integrity verification** — Restored data checked for consistency, distinct from the restore having succeeded. *(AWS; provisioned by CI)*
- **4. Reconstruction test** — Pipeline re-run rebuilding analytics from source, validating the reconstructible claim made under KSI-RPL-RRO. *(GCP; provisioned by Terraform and CI)*
- **5. Test record** — Every test records what was tested, elapsed time, outcome, and whether it met the objective. Successful runs produce records too. *(AWS; provisioned by Terraform)*
- **6. Objective-to-test mapping** — Every declared objective mapped to the test that demonstrates it. *(Repo; provisioned by Version control)*

### Validation

- **Cycle.** Required: 3 days. Current practice: the collector runs daily from GitHub Actions (.github/workflows/collect.yml), as a read-only identity in each cloud with no stored credential, and this record was emitted by that run. Daily is stricter than every required cycle above (DECISIONS.md, 2026-10-01).
- **CFG** — Every declared objective maps to a demonstrating test. Source: Mapping versus the register from KSI-RPL-RRO. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per run. *Row `KSI-RPL-TRC.verify.1`: not automated -- gap. One of the 21 declared recovery objectives -- the database's -- has a demonstrating test (registers/recovery-tests.yaml). Risk: Twenty objectives are declared from design and unmeasured.*
- **CFG** — Test schedules exist and are enabled. Source: Scheduler configuration. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per run. *Row `KSI-RPL-TRC.verify.2`: not automated -- gap. Recovery tests run when the operator runs them, per session (deliberate/), not on a schedule; the environment they need stands only during sessions. Risk: A test can lapse past its cadence; the deliberate_test checks then fail, which is how a lapse is seen.*
- **CFG** — Test record destination is configured. Source: Record store configuration. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per run. *Row `KSI-RPL-TRC.verify.3`: not automated -- manual. Test records go to deliberate-tests/ in the Object Locked log store, written by deliberate/run.py; recovery measurements are also entered in registers/recovery-tests.yaml. Evidence: deliberate/run.py; registers/recovery-tests.yaml. Risk: The destination is fixed in the runner, not a configuration a check reads.*
- **OPS** — Every test has run within its cadence with a record, including successful runs. Source: Test record store. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per declared cadence. *Row `KSI-RPL-TRC.validate.1`: automated by `rpl-trc-ops-deliberate-tests-current`.*
- **OPS** — Measured restore time is within the declared objective. Source: Test records versus the register from KSI-RPL-RRO. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per declared cadence (monthly). *Row `KSI-RPL-TRC.validate.2`: automated by `cna-ofa-ops-timed-point-in-time-restore`.*
- **OPS** — A point-in-time restore to a chosen moment succeeds. Source: Test result. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per declared cadence (quarterly). *Row `KSI-RPL-TRC.validate.3`: automated by `cna-ofa-ops-timed-point-in-time-restore`.*
- **OPS** — Restored data passes consistency verification. Source: Integrity check output. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per declared cadence (monthly). *Row `KSI-RPL-TRC.validate.4`: not automated -- gap. The restore test times the instance to available, encrypted and private; it does not connect and verify the restored data. Risk: A restore that came up with corrupt or partial data would pass the timing test.*
- **OPS** — The pipeline rebuilds analytics from source successfully. Source: Reconstruction test result. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per declared cadence (quarterly). *Row `KSI-RPL-TRC.validate.5`: not automated -- gap. The analytics pipeline re-reads every extract each run and merges idempotently (2026-10-02), but a rebuild from an empty warehouse has not been run: past 30 days the warehouse is the only copy (DECISIONS.md, 2026-10-03). Risk: Reconstruction is proven only within the extracts' 30 days.*
- **OPS** — Zero declared objectives without a demonstrating test, standing query, zero results expected. Source: Objective register versus test mapping. Required every 3 days; implemented: Per run. *Row `KSI-RPL-TRC.validate.6`: not automated -- gap. As verify 1: twenty objectives have no demonstrating test. Risk: As verify 1.*
- **Automated evidence.** 1 collector check(s) in the run on 2026-10-26: 1 PASS. Historical metrics (SDR-CSX-KMT) cover 19 day(s) of CI runs since 2026-10-02, in the metrics evidence object below; the year is what exists, not a full year.

### Assessment

- **No independent assessor is engaged.** The statements below are the provider's own assurance reasoning from the determination. They are not an independent assessment and should not be read as one.
- **Automation assurance.** Accuracy is tested by deliberately corrupting a restored dataset and confirming the integrity verification fails it, which separates the integrity check from the restore check. The test record is validated by confirming a successful run produces a record, since a framework that records only failures cannot demonstrate that testing occurred. Sufficiency is bounded by what can be exercised: zone and region failure cannot be simulated and provider-managed service recovery is inherited, so the tested set is the subset this project controls.
- **Limitations and residual risk.** Zone and region failure cannot be simulated, so the availability half of recovery is tested only at the resource level; a task can be killed and a zone cannot. There is no cross-region capability to test, per KSI-RPL-ABO. Provider-managed recovery for managed services is inherited and untested. The synthetic environment has small data volumes, so measured restore times are not representative, since restore time at scale is dominated by data size. Testing recovery from compromise exercises the mechanism and not the detection: knowing which moment to restore to depends on establishing when the compromise began, which is an investigation problem this determination does not address.

### Tests

- Negative control for `rpl-trc-ops-deliberate-tests-current` (collector/self_test.py): inputs that must fail it: never run, latest failed, errored, too old, another scenario's record.
- Designed, not yet automated: Every test has run within its cadence with a record, including successful runs (Test record store).
- Designed, not yet automated: Measured restore time is within the declared objective (Test records versus the register from KSI-RPL-RRO).
- Designed, not yet automated: A point-in-time restore to a chosen moment succeeds (Test result).
- Designed, not yet automated: The pipeline rebuilds analytics from source successfully (Reconstruction test result).
- Designed, not yet automated: Zero declared objectives without a demonstrating test, standing query, zero results expected (Objective register versus test mapping).

### Evidence

- *Report, 2026-10-26* — Every scenario in the deliberate test catalogue (deliberate/scenarios.yaml) has a passing record younger than its declared cadence plus five days, failures and successful runs alike recorded in the Object Locked log store. Result: PASS — all 14 catalogued tests passed within their cadence ([definition](https://github.com/elvie-valmores/fedramp-20x-ksi-assessment/blob/97cdfd2eee91745843bbb9d17758668091a06d60/collector/checks/rpl-trc-ops-deliberate-tests-current.json))
- *Report, 2026-10-26* — Historical metrics (SDR-CSX-KMT): daily collector results from CI for KSI-RPL-TRC (the daily schedule and any run started by hand in CI; never a workstation run). Past 30 days: data on 14 of 30 days (2026-10-07 to 2026-10-26); every check passed on 13 of 14; mean daily pass rate 93%; not passing: rpl-trc-ops-deliberate-tests-current on 1 day(s). Past year: data on 14 of 365 days (2026-10-07 to 2026-10-26); every check passed on 13 of 14; mean daily pass rate 93%; not passing: rpl-trc-ops-deliberate-tests-current on 1 day(s). The record store began 2026-10-07, so the year is what exists, not a full year.

## KSI-RPL-ARP — Partially Implemented

### Implementation

- Recovery plan resolves the way documented procedure did under CMT-RVP. This project has no plan document and a plan is arguably non-machine, but recovery here is executed by automation that is versioned, reviewable and executed exactly as written, so the automation is the plan; that is a stronger claim than a document would be, because a plan never executed is a description of intent whereas this one runs monthly under TRC. The sixteen mappings are taken in groups with a position each rather than covered collectively, because most are not directly satisfiable. CP-2 and its enhancements are the core and are satisfied by the encoded recovery path, with resume-mission mapping onto the per-resource objectives declared under RRO. CP-6 and enhancements follow the reading set in ABO, a failure-domain property rather than a physical location, satisfied at zone granularity through provider-managed storage and failing at region granularity for want of cross-region replication, with accessibility during disruption inherited. CP-7 alternate processing changes meaning in a cloud context: multi-zone compute with automatic replacement is alternate processing that is always running rather than a site to fail over to, so it is satisfied continuously at zone granularity and fails at region, while priority of service provisions are a contractual arrangement and unavailable to a persona with no agreements, the same limit reached in the supply chain cluster. CP-8 telecommunications does not map onto an offering that procures no telecommunications service, since connectivity is part of the platform, so it is inherited in substance and inapplicable in form. The provider-controlled portion is therefore small: the encoded recovery path, its alignment with the objectives, and its review. That is a thin indicator dressed in sixteen mappings, and saying so is more useful than constructing coverage for each one. What keeps it from being empty is that the plan-to-objective mapping is real and checkable in both directions, since a path with no objective behind it is unexplained work.
- **1. Encoded recovery paths** — Per resource class: what recovers, in what order, by what mechanism, to what objective. *(Repo; provisioned by Version control)*
- **2. Plan-to-objective mapping** — Every objective in the register mapped to a path, and every path mapped to an objective. *(Repo; provisioned by Version control)*
- **3. Control position register** — Each of the sixteen mappings with its position — satisfied, inherited, unavailable or inapplicable — and a reason. *(Repo; provisioned by Version control)*
- **4. Dependency ordering** — Recovery order where one resource depends on another, so the path is a sequence rather than a set. *(Repo; provisioned by Version control)*
- **5. Effectiveness signal report** — Section emitted by the shared signal report generator, aggregating this indicator's signals over the period. One generator serves all indicators that declare signals; no per-indicator scheduled job. *(AWS; provisioned by Terraform)*

### Validation

- **Cycle.** Required: 3 months. Current practice: the collector runs daily from GitHub Actions (.github/workflows/collect.yml), as a read-only identity in each cloud with no stored credential, and this record was emitted by that run. Daily is stricter than every required cycle above (DECISIONS.md, 2026-10-01).
- **CFG** — Every objective maps to a path and every path to an objective. Source: Mapping versus the register from KSI-RPL-RRO. Required every 3 months; implemented: Per review. *Row `KSI-RPL-ARP.verify.1`: automated by `rpl-arp-cfg-recovery-paths`.*
- **CFG** — Every mapped control carries a recorded position with a reason. Source: Control position register, checked in CI. Required every 3 months; implemented: Per review. *Row `KSI-RPL-ARP.verify.2`: automated by `rpl-arp-cfg-control-positions`.*

### Assessment

- **No independent assessor is engaged.** The statements below are the provider's own assurance reasoning from the determination. They are not an independent assessment and should not be read as one.
- **Automation assurance.** Accuracy is tested by adding an objective with no path and confirming the mapping check fails it, and by removing a position from the register and confirming the completeness check fires. Sufficiency is bounded by the fact that most of this indicator's content is inherited or unavailable: the checks verify that positions are recorded and that paths align with objectives, and they do not verify that the inherited portions actually work, because those are provider-managed and unobservable.
- **Limitations and residual risk.** This is the thinnest determination in the cluster and the weakest in the project. Of sixteen mappings, roughly four are directly satisfied, six are inherited from the provider, four are unavailable without contractual arrangements, and two are inapplicable in form to a cloud-hosted offering, so the provider-controlled portion is small relative to the mapping count. Alternate processing and storage are satisfied at zone granularity and fail at region, per KSI-RPL-ABO. Priority of service provisions are contractual and unavailable, the same limit reached in the supply chain cluster. Telecommunications controls do not map onto an offering that procures no telecommunications service. There is no independent review of whether the plan is appropriate, which is the same self-assessment limit recorded under CMT-RVP. The periodic human review step is out of scope project-wide: judging whether signals indicate a problem is organizational activity, the same category as the personnel and training material excluded at KSI-PIY-GIV. Signal generation is machine-based and is built; the judgment on top is declared, not performed.

### Tests

- Negative control for `rpl-arp-cfg-control-positions` (collector/self_test.py): inputs that must fail it: a mapped control missing, an unmapped control added, unknown position, no reason.
- Negative control for `rpl-arp-cfg-recovery-paths` (collector/self_test.py): inputs that must fail it: objective with no path, path recovering nothing with an objective, path encoded in a missing file, circular order, unknown predecessor.
- Negative control for `rpl-arp-ops-signal-report` (collector/self_test.py): inputs that must fail it: no report, a section missing, a signal not computed, a signal absent, stale.
- Designed, not yet automated: Every path has been exercised within its cadence (Test records from KSI-RPL-TRC).

## KSI-INR-RIR — Partially Implemented

### Implementation

- Documented procedure resolves as it did under CMT-RVP and for the same reason: the responder built for IAM-SUS is the incident response procedure for the cases it covers, being detection, severity routing, containment, recording and escalation. It is versioned, it executes exactly as written, and it is the only path those responses take, where a separate prose runbook would describe intent. The scope is narrower than a full incident response procedure and that is the honest shape: what is encoded is detection-to-containment for one incident class, plus escalation into a human path that does not exist, whereas a complete procedure covers declaration, roles, communication, containment, eradication, recovery and closure across all incident types. The mappings split mostly along whether a person or a recipient is required. Incident handling, automated incident handling processes, system-generated alerts and the response plan itself are encoded and reviewable. Incident reporting and automated reporting require a recipient, and a real Class C provider reports to FedRAMP and to affected agencies within defined timeframes; this persona has neither, so the reporting half is unavailable rather than unbuilt, the same distinction drawn for contractual notification under SCR-MON. Supply chain coordination was already declared unavailable there. Incident response assistance has no subject, since there are no users to assist. Effectiveness follows the method established in CMT-RVP, computed from records the responder already produces: whether it fired when a finding arrived, time from finding to containment, how often a containment needed reversing, and how many findings routed to the human path and sat there. That last signal is the interesting one here, because the human path is the gap.
- **1. Encoded procedure** — Severity tiers, actions, exclusions and escalation. Built under KSI-IAM-SUS and referenced here rather than duplicated. *(Both; provisioned by Terraform)*
- **2. Response effectiveness signals** — Time from finding to action, reversal rate, escalation rate, and escalations without disposition. *(AWS; provisioned by Terraform)*
- **3. Control position register** — Each of the ten mappings with its position and reason. *(Repo; provisioned by Version control)*
- **4. Escalation disposition** — Every escalation to the human path carries a recorded outcome. *(Repo and AWS; provisioned by Terraform)*
- **5. Effectiveness signal report** — Section emitted by the shared signal report generator, aggregating this indicator's signals over the period. One generator serves all indicators that declare signals; no per-indicator scheduled job. *(AWS; provisioned by Terraform)*

### Validation

- **Cycle.** Required: 3 months. Current practice: the collector runs daily from GitHub Actions (.github/workflows/collect.yml), as a read-only identity in each cloud with no stored credential, and this record was emitted by that run. Daily is stricter than every required cycle above (DECISIONS.md, 2026-10-01).
- **CFG** — Procedure is encoded and versioned, and changes to it are recorded. Source: Repository history; change alerting from KSI-CMT-LMC. Required every 3 months; implemented: Per review. *Row `KSI-INR-RIR.verify.1`: not automated -- manual. The procedure is docs/INCIDENT-RESPONSE.md, versioned in git; changes are commits, not alerted. Evidence: docs/INCIDENT-RESPONSE.md and its git history. Risk: A change to the procedure is recorded but not alerted.*
- **CFG** — Every severity tier has a defined response. Source: Responder configuration. Required every 3 months; implemented: Per review. *Row `KSI-INR-RIR.verify.2`: not automated -- manual. Each alert class has a defined response in the manual procedure: triage, the narrowest containment, recovery by redeploy, reconciliation and the record. Severity routing to automated actions went with the responder (2026-10-03). Evidence: docs/INCIDENT-RESPONSE.md, versioned in the repository. Risk: The response is defined per alert class rather than per severity tier, and a person carries it out; its timing is not measured.*
- **CFG** — Every mapped control carries a recorded position with a reason. Source: Position register, checked in CI. Required every 3 months; implemented: Per review. *Row `KSI-INR-RIR.verify.3`: automated by `inr-rir-cfg-control-positions`.*
- **CFG** — Escalation disposition recording is configured. Source: Workflow configuration. Required every 3 months; implemented: Per review. *Row `KSI-INR-RIR.verify.4`: not automated -- gap. Dispositions are recorded as prose in DECISIONS.md (the runbook's step 5); no structured disposition record is configured. Risk: An undispositioned alert is not visible as a number (the signal report lists dispositions as not collected).*
- **OPS** — Every exercised response produced a record. Source: Response records from KSI-IAM-SUS. Required every 3 months; implemented: Per review. *Row `KSI-INR-RIR.validate.1`: not automated -- gap. No response has been exercised: the responder was descoped and no incident or exercise has occurred. Risk: The manual procedure is unexercised.*
- **OPS** — Zero escalations without a recorded disposition, standing query, zero results expected. Source: Escalation records. Required every 3 months; implemented: Per review. *Row `KSI-INR-RIR.validate.2`: not automated -- gap. As verify 4: dispositions are prose, so escalations without one cannot be counted. Risk: As verify 4.*
- **OPS** — Zero mapped controls without a position, standing query, zero results expected. Source: Position register. Required every 3 months; implemented: Per review. *Row `KSI-INR-RIR.validate.3`: automated by `inr-rir-cfg-control-positions`.*
- **OPS** — Time from finding to containment is within the declared expectation. Source: Response records. Required every 3 months; implemented: Per review. *Row `KSI-INR-RIR.validate.4`: not automated -- gap. No incident has occurred, and containment is manual, so there is no finding-to-containment time to measure. Risk: The containment time is unknown until an incident or an exercise measures it; the declared expectation is unproven.*
- **OPS** — Signal report section generated for the period, with signals computed including nil results. Source: Signal report output. Required every 3 months; implemented: Per review. *Row `KSI-INR-RIR.validate.5`: automated by `inr-rir-ops-signal-report`.*
- **Automated evidence.** 2 collector check(s) in the run on 2026-10-26: 2 PASS. Historical metrics (SDR-CSX-KMT) cover 19 day(s) of CI runs since 2026-10-02, in the metrics evidence object below; the year is what exists, not a full year.

### Assessment

- **No independent assessor is engaged.** The statements below are the provider's own assurance reasoning from the determination. They are not an independent assessment and should not be read as one.
- **Automation assurance.** Accuracy is tested by generating a sample finding at each severity and confirming the response matches the encoded tier, then by leaving an escalation undispositioned and confirming the check fires. Sufficiency is bounded twice: there have been no real incidents, so effectiveness is measured against self-generated findings, and the human half of the procedure does not exist, so escalations terminate in a record rather than a response.
- **Limitations and residual risk.** The encoded procedure covers detection-to-containment for one incident class, so declaration, communication, eradication, recovery and closure are not encoded, which is the largest gap here. Incident reporting is unavailable, since a real provider reports to FedRAMP and to affected agencies within defined timeframes and this persona has neither. Incident response assistance has no subject, as there are no users to assist. Effectiveness is measured against self-generated sample findings rather than real incidents. The human path is the weakest point and it is where the procedure ends: escalations are recorded and then nothing happens, because there is nobody to act. The periodic human review step is out of scope project-wide: judging whether signals indicate a problem is organizational activity, the same category as the personnel and training material excluded at KSI-PIY-GIV. Signal generation is machine-based and is built; the judgment on top is declared, not performed.

### Tests

- Negative control for `inr-rir-cfg-control-positions` (collector/self_test.py): inputs that must fail it: a mapped control missing, an unmapped control added, unknown position, no reason.
- Negative control for `inr-rir-ops-signal-report` (collector/self_test.py): inputs that must fail it: no report, a section missing, a signal not computed, a signal absent, stale.

Result: PASS — all 10 controls positioned: 3 inapplicable, 3 partial, 1 satisfied, 3 unavailable ([definition](https://github.com/elvie-valmores/fedramp-20x-ksi-assessment/blob/97cdfd2eee91745843bbb9d17758668091a06d60/collector/checks/inr-rir-cfg-control-positions.json))

## KSI-INR-AAR — Not Implemented

### Implementation

- **Deferred.** The subject does not exist. There have been no incidents, so there are no after-action reports to generate and no lessons to incorporate. A sliver was considered and rejected: the responder built under IAM-SUS produces structured records for every exercised response, and those records are real, but they are records of sample findings this project generated to test its own machinery. An after-action report about a test you ran yourself is a test record, and calling it an after-action report would manufacture coverage in exactly the way this project has refused elsewhere. The lessons-learned half is also organizational: incorporating a lesson means changing how people work, and where it means changing how the machinery works, the mechanism already exists under CMT-RVP and INR-RIR as effectiveness review. Resulting risk to customers: a provider that does not produce after-action reports repeats incidents it has already had, and the absence is invisible until the second occurrence.
- **Resulting risk.** This is a deferral by absent subject rather than by scope, in the same shape as SVC-RUD where the triggering condition could not arise. The mechanism that would produce the reports exists and is exercised; what does not exist is anything to report on.

### Validation

- **Cycle.** Required: 3 months. Current practice: the collector runs daily from GitHub Actions (.github/workflows/collect.yml), as a read-only identity in each cloud with no stored credential, and this record was emitted by that run. Daily is stricter than every required cycle above (DECISIONS.md, 2026-10-01).
- **N/A** — No evidence produced. Source: Deferred. Required every 3 months; implemented: Not implemented.

## KSI-INR-RPI — Not Implemented

### Implementation

- **Deferred.** This indicator needs two things the environment cannot supply: past incidents, and enough of them for patterns to exist. Even if a single incident had occurred, pattern analysis over one record is arithmetic rather than review, and the indicator's value is specifically in what becomes apparent across a corpus that no single incident would show. The statutory meaning of vulnerability is broad enough that a pattern here could be a misconfiguration recurring rather than a code defect, which makes the indicator more interesting in a real environment and no more reachable in this one. Incident monitoring under IR-5 is built in substance, since detection and response records are produced and retained, but monitoring incidents that have not happened produces an empty corpus. Resulting risk to customers: a provider that does not review its incident history misses the systemic cause behind repeated symptoms and treats each occurrence as novel.
- **Resulting risk.** The retention and structure that would make this analysis possible exist, since response records are normalized and queryable alongside everything else. What is missing is the history, which no amount of building supplies.

### Validation

- **Cycle.** Required: 3 months. Current practice: the collector runs daily from GitHub Actions (.github/workflows/collect.yml), as a read-only identity in each cloud with no stored credential, and this record was emitted by that run. Daily is stricter than every required cycle above (DECISIONS.md, 2026-10-01).

## KSI-CED-RAT — Not Implemented

### Implementation

- **Deferred.** The statement names four audiences and every one of them is employees. This project has five declared roles as an entitlement baseline in code, which is design input for authorization decisions, and no employees to train; the roles exist so that access can be bounded, not so that people can be counted. Every mapping sits in the awareness and training family or its neighbours, covering training content, delivery, records and role-based instruction, and none has an infrastructure expression: training completion is a fact about a person, not a resource state. The split-by-subject rule applied elsewhere does not rescue anything here. SVC-PRR kept its machine-based half because resource state is machine-based even when plans and procedures are not, and CMT-RVP and INR-RIR treated encoded pipelines as documented procedures because those pipelines execute. Training executes in people, and there is nothing encoded to point at. Fabricating completion records for a fictional workforce would be exactly the invention this project declined when it excluded personnel registers, and it would be the weakest kind of coverage: a spreadsheet asserting that people who do not exist attended training that did not happen. Resulting risk to customers: a provider whose staff are untrained will misconfigure, mishandle and misreport regardless of how good its automation is, and most incidents originate in the half of the organization this indicator covers and this project does not.
- **Resulting risk.** This is the most complete deferral in the project, in that nothing about the indicator is addressed and nothing could be without fabricating a workforce. It is also the indicator whose absence a real assessor would weigh most heavily against a small provider, because a two-person team's security posture depends more on what those two people know than on any control they configure.

### Validation

- **Cycle.** Required: 3 months. Current practice: the collector runs daily from GitHub Actions (.github/workflows/collect.yml), as a read-only identity in each cloud with no stored credential, and this record was emitted by that run. Daily is stricter than every required cycle above (DECISIONS.md, 2026-10-01).
